\displaystyle\qquad{}=\lambda\bigl(\bigl[\overline{\partial}_{E,h},\partial_{E,h}\bigl]+\bigl[\theta_{E,h}\theta^{\dagger}_{E,h}\bigl]\bigr)+\overline{\partial}_{E,h}\theta_{E,h}+\lambda^{2}\partial_{E,h}\theta^{\dagger}_{E,h}=0.

(2.8)

From (
2.6
) and (
2.7
),
we obtain

\displaystyle\partial_{E,h}\theta_{E,h}=-\lambda^{-1}\big(1-|\lambda|^{2}\big)\theta_{E,h}^{2}.

Because

(\overline{\partial}_{E,h}\theta^{\dagger}_{E,h})^{\dagger}=\partial_{E,h}\theta_{E,h}
,
we obtain

\displaystyle\overline{\partial}_{E,h}\theta_{E,h}^{\dagger}=\overline{\lambda}^{-1}\big(1-|\lambda|^{2}\big)\big(\theta^{\dagger}_{E,h}\big)^{2}.

(2.9)

Note that
G(h)^{1,1}=0
is equivalent to

\overline{\partial}_{E,h}\theta_{E,h}=0
and

\partial_{E,h}\theta_{E,h}^{\dagger}=0
.
To obtain
G(h)=0
, it is enough to prove

\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big(\theta_{E,h}^{2}\big(\theta_{E,h}^{\dagger}\big)^{2}\big)=0
.
Indeed, there exists
C\neq 0
depending on
\dim Y

such that for any Kähler form
\omega
of
Y

we obtain

\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big(\theta_{E,h}^{2}\big(\theta_{E,h}^{\dagger}\big)^{2}\big)\omega^{\dim Y-2}=C\bigl|\theta_{E,h}^{2}\bigr|^{2}_{h,\omega}\omega^{\dim Y}
.
Hence, the vanishing

\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big(\theta_{E,h}^{2}\big(\theta_{E,h}^{\dagger}\big)^{2}\big)=0

implies
\theta_{E,h}^{2}=\big(\theta_{E,h}^{\dagger}\big)^{2}=0

and
\partial_{E,h}^{2}=\overline{\partial}_{E,h}^{2}=0
.

From (
2.9
) and
\partial_{E,h}\theta^{\dagger}_{E,h}=0
,
we obtain
\partial_{E,h}\overline{\partial}_{E,h}\theta_{E,h}^{\dagger}=\overline{\lambda}^{-1}\big(1-|\lambda|^{2}\big)\partial_{E,h}\bigl((\theta^{\dagger}_{E,h})^{2}\bigr)=0
.
We also have

\overline{\partial}_{E,h}\partial_{E,h}\theta_{E,h}^{\dagger}=0
.
Hence, we obtain the following equality:

\displaystyle 0=\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big(\theta_{E,h}\partial_{E,h}\overline{\partial}_{E,h}\theta^{\dagger}_{E,h}\big)=\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\bigl(\theta_{E,h}\cdot\big[\overline{\partial}_{E,h}\partial_{E,h}+\partial_{E,h}\overline{\partial}_{E,h},\theta_{E,h}^{\dagger}\big]\bigr).

(2.10)

From (
2.8
),

\overline{\partial}_{E,h}\theta_{E,h}=0

and

\partial_{E,h}\theta_{E,h}^{\dagger}=0
,
we obtain

\displaystyle\big[\overline{\partial}_{E,h},\partial_{E,h}\big]+\big[\theta_{E,h},\theta_{E,h}^{\dagger}\big]=0.

Hence, we obtain the following:

\displaystyle\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\bigl(\theta_{E,h}\cdot\big[\overline{\partial}_{E,h}\partial_{E,h}+\partial_{E,h}\overline{\partial}_{E,h},\theta_{E,h}^{\dagger}\big]\bigr)

\displaystyle=\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\bigl(\theta_{E,h}\cdot\bigl[{-}\big[\theta_{E,h},\theta_{E,h}^{\dagger}\big],\theta_{E,h}^{\dagger}\bigr]\bigr)

\displaystyle=-2\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\bigl(\theta_{E,h}^{2}\big(\theta^{\dagger}_{E,h}\big)^{2}\bigr).

(2.11)

We obtain the claim of the proposition
from (
2.10
) and (
2.11
).
∎

By using Proposition
2.15

we can improve
[
49
, Corollary 2.30]

as follows.

Corollary 2.16
.

If
\lambda\neq 0
,
the pluri-harmonicity of the metric
h
is
equivalent to
the vanishing

G(h)^{1,1}=0
,
i.e.,
\overline{\partial}_{E,h}\theta_{E,h}=0
.

Remark 2.17
.

In
[
49
, Lemma 2.29]
, the claim

\big[\,\overline{\partial}_{V,h},\partial_{V,h}\big]+\big[\theta_{V,h},\theta_{V,h}^{\dagger}\big]=0

is incorrect, in general. The author thanks Pengfei Huang for pointing out it.

Remark 2.18
.

If
\lambda=1
,
the claim of Proposition
2.15

also follows from
a Bochner type formula
[
48
, Proposition 21.39]
,
which originally goes back to
the study of Simpson
[
64
]

in the context of harmonic bundles,
the study of Corlette
[
11
]

in the context of harmonic metrics
for flat bundles on Riemannian manifolds,
and the study of Siu
[
69
]

in the context of harmonic maps.

2.7
Wild harmonic bundles

2.7.1
Higgs case

Let
X
be a complex manifold
with a simple normal crossing hypersurface

H=\bigcup_{i\in\Lambda}H_{i}
.
Let
\big(E,\overline{\partial}_{E},\theta,h\big)
be a harmonic bundle
on
X\setminus H
.
It is called wild on
(X,H)

if the following holds:

•

Let
\Sigma_{\theta}\subset T^{\ast}(X\setminus H)

denote the spectral cover of
\theta
,
i.e.,
\Sigma_{\theta}
denotes the support
of the coherent
\mathcal{O}_{T^{\ast}(X\setminus H)}
-module
induced by
\big(E,\overline{\partial}_{E},\theta\big)
.
Then, the closure of
\Sigma_{\theta}

in the relatively projective completion of

T^{\ast}X
with respect to
X
is complex analytic.

A wild harmonic bundle
\big(E,\overline{\partial}_{E},\theta,h\big)

is called unramifiedly good at
P\in H
if the following holds:

•

There exists a good set of irregular values

\mathcal{I}_{P}\subset\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H)_{P}/\mathcal{O}_{X,P}
,
a neighbourhood
X_{P}
,
and a decomposition

\displaystyle\big(E,\overline{\partial}_{E},\theta\big)_{|X_{P}\setminus H}=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}_{P}}\big(E_{\mathfrak{a}},\overline{\partial}_{E_{\mathfrak{a}}},\theta_{\mathfrak{a}}\big)

such that the closure of the spectral cover
\Sigma_{\mathfrak{a}}

of
\theta_{\mathfrak{a}}-{\rm d}\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}\mathop{\rm id}\nolimits_{E_{\mathfrak{a}}}

in
T^{\ast}X_{P}(\log(X_{P}\cap H))

is proper over
X_{P}
,
where
\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}
denote lifts of
\mathfrak{a}

to
\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H)_{P}
.

A wild harmonic bundle

\big(E,\overline{\partial}_{E},\theta,h\big)

is called good at
P\in H

if the following holds:

•

There exist a neighbourhood
X_{P}

and a covering
\varphi_{P}\colon X_{P}^{\prime}\longrightarrow X_{P}

ramified along
H_{P}^{\prime}

such that the pull back

\varphi_{P}^{-1}\big(E,\overline{\partial}_{E},\theta,h\big)_{|X_{P}}

is unramifiedly good wild at any point of

\varphi_{P}^{-1}(H)
.

We say that

\big(E,\overline{\partial}_{E},\theta,h\big)
is good wild
(resp. unramifiedly good wild) on
(X,H)

if it is good wild (resp. unramifiedly good wild)
at any point of
H
.

Note that not every wild harmonic bundle on
(X,H)

is necessarily good on
(X,H)
.
But, the following is known

[
55
, Corollary 15.2.8]
.

Theorem 2.19
.

Let
\big(E,\overline{\partial}_{E},\theta,h\big)
be a wild harmonic bundle
on
(X,H)
.
Then, there exists a proper birational morphism

\varphi\colon X^{\prime}\longrightarrow X
of complex manifolds such that

(i)

H^{\prime}:=\varphi^{-1}(H)
is simple normal crossing,

(ii)

X^{\prime}\setminus H^{\prime}\simeq X\setminus H
,

(iii)

\varphi^{-1}\big(E,\overline{\partial}_{E},\theta,h\big)

is good wild on
(X^{\prime},H^{\prime})
.

2.7.2
The case of
\boldsymbol{\lambda}
-flat bundles

A
\lambda
-flat bundle
\big(E,\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

with a pluri-harmonic metric
h
on
X\setminus H

is called (good, unramifiedly good) wild
if the associated Higgs bundle with a pluri-harmonic metric

\big(E,\overline{\partial}_{E,h},\theta_{E,h},h\big)
is a (good, unramifiedly good) wild harmonic bundle.

2.7.3
Prolongation of good wild harmonic bundles to good filtered
\boldsymbol{\lambda}
-flat bundles

The following is one of the fundamental theorems
in the study of wild harmonic bundles

[
51
, Theorem 7.4.3]
.

Theorem 2.20
.

If
\big(E,\mathbb{D}^{\lambda},h\big)
is a good wild harmonic bundle
on
(X,H)
,
then
\big(\mathcal{P}^{h}_{\ast}E,\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

is a good filtered
\lambda
-flat bundle on
(X,H)
.

The following is a consequence of
the norm estimate for good wild harmonic bundles

[
51
, Theorem 11.7.2]
.

Theorem 2.21
.

Let
\big(E,\mathbb{D}^{\lambda},h_{i}\big)

(i=1,2)
be
good wild harmonic bundles on
X\setminus H

such that

\mathcal{P}^{h_{1}}_{\ast}E=\mathcal{P}^{h_{2}}_{\ast}E
.
Then,
h_{i}
are mutually bounded
around any point of
H
.

2.7.4
Prolongation of good wild harmonic bundles in the projective case

Suppose that
X
is projective and connected.
Let
L
be any ample line bundle on
X
.
The following is proved in
[
51
, Propositions 13.6.1 and 13.6.4]
.

Proposition 2.22
.

Let
\big(E,\mathbb{D}^{\lambda},h\big)
be a good wild harmonic bundle
on
(X,H)
.

•

\big(\mathcal{P}^{h}_{\ast}E,\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
is

\mu_{L}
-polystable
with
\mu_{L}\big(\mathcal{P}^{h}_{\ast}E\big)=0
.

•

We obtain

c_{1}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}E)=0

and

\int_{X}\mathop{\rm ch}\nolimits_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}E)c_{1}(L)^{\dim X-2}=0
.

•

Let
h^{\prime}
be another pluri-harmonic metric of

\big(E,\mathbb{D}^{\lambda},h\big)

such that
\mathcal{P}^{h^{\prime}}_{\ast}E=\mathcal{P}^{h}_{\ast}E
.
Then, there exists a decomposition of the
\lambda
-flat bundle

\big(E,\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)=\bigoplus\big(E_{j},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{j}\big)

such that

(i)
the decomposition is orthogonal
with respect to both
h
and
h^{\prime}
,

(ii)

h_{|E_{i}}=a_{i}\cdot h^{\prime}_{|E_{i}}
for some
a_{i}>0
.

•

Let
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{1},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{1}\big)

be any direct summand
of
\big(\mathcal{P}^{h}_{\ast}E,\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
.
Let
\big(E_{1},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{1}\big)
be
the
\lambda
-flat bundle on
X\setminus H

obtained as the restriction of
\big(\mathcal{V}_{1},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{1}\big)
,
and let
h_{1}
be the metric of
E_{1}
induced by
h
.
Then,
\big(E_{1},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{1},h_{1}\big)
is a harmonic bundle. In particular, we obtain

c_{1}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{1})=0

and

\int_{X}\mathop{\rm ch}\nolimits_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{1})c_{1}(L)^{\dim X-2}=0
.

2.8
Main existence theorem in this paper

Let
X
be a smooth connected projective complex manifold
with a simple normal crossing hypersurface
H
.
Let
L
be any ample line bundle on
X
.
Let
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
be a good filtered

\lambda
-flat bundle on
(X,H)
.
Let
\big(E,\overline{\partial}_{E},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
be the
\lambda
-flat bundle
obtained as the restriction of
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

to
X\setminus H
.

Theorem 2.23
.

Suppose that

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
is
\mu_{L}
-polystable,
and that the following vanishing holds:

\displaystyle\mu_{L}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})=0,\qquad\int_{X}\mathop{\rm ch}\nolimits_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})c_{1}(L)^{\dim X-2}=0.

(2.12)

Then, there exists a pluri-harmonic metric
h

of
\big(E,\overline{\partial}_{E},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
such that

\big(\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)_{|X\setminus H}\simeq\big(E,\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

extends to

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)\simeq\big(\mathcal{P}^{h}_{\ast}E,\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
.

We proved the claim of the theorem in the case
\lambda=1

in
[
51
, Theorem 16.1.1]
. We shall explain the proof in
Sections
3
–
7
.
Note that the one dimensional case is due to
Biquard–Boalch
[
3
]
.

Corollary 2.24
.

There exists the equivalence of the following objects
for each
\lambda
:

•

Good wild harmonic bundles on
(X,H)
.

•

\mu_{L}
-polystable good filtered
\lambda
-flat bundles

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

satisfying the condition
(\ref{eq;19.2.18.30})
.

Remark 2.25
.

One of the referees raised the following interesting question.
Let
L
be a big and nef line bundle on
X

such that there exists a positive current

T
representing
c_{1}(L)

whose restriction to
X\setminus H

is a smooth Kähler form
with at most Poincaré growth near
H
. We do not assume that
L
is ample. We can define the slope
\mu_{L}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})

for a filtered sheaves,
by using which we can introduce a stability condition
for good filtered
\lambda
-flat bundles.
Then, we may ask whether a statement
similar to Theorem
2.23
holds.
This question might also be related with a generalization
of Kobayashi–Hitchin correspondence
to the context of
\mathcal{D}
-modules.

2.8.1
Outline of the proof
Theorem
2.23

Let us explain a rough outline of the proof. We shall omit some technical details.
Let
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

be a
\mu_{L}
-stable good filtered
\lambda
-flat bundle on
(X,H)

such that

\mathop{\rm ch}\nolimits_{i}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})=0

(i=1,2)
.
Let
\big(V,\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
be the
\lambda
-flat bundle
on
X\setminus H

obtained as the restriction of
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

to
X\setminus H
.

In the case
\dim X=1
,
we shall apply the argument in
[
63
]

as follows.
For each
P\in H
,
we take a holomorphic coordinate neighbourhood

\big(X_{P},z_{P}\big)
around
P
. We take a Kähler metric
g_{X\setminus H}

such that
g_{X\setminus H|X_{P}\setminus\{P\}}

(P\in H)

are mutually bounded with

|z_{P}|^{2\eta-2}{\rm d}z_{P}\,{\rm d}\overline{z}_{P}

for some
\eta>0
.
If
\eta
is sufficiently small,
there exists a Hermitian metric
h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}
of
V

such that

(i)

\mathcal{P}^{h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}}_{\ast}V=\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}
,
and

(ii)

G(h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits})
is bounded with respect to

h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}
and
g_{X\setminus H}
,

(iii)

\det(h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits})
is flat.
(See Corollary
3.28
.
Though we state it as a corollary of Proposition
3.27
,
which also deals with a perturbation,
it is easy to deduce it directly from
the estimate in the tame case
[
63
]
.)
Moreover for any filtered
\lambda
-flat subsheaf

\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}
,

\deg(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}^{\prime})
is equal
to the analytic degree of
\big(\mathcal{V}^{\prime},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)_{|X\setminus H}

with respect to
h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}
and
g_{X\setminus H}
.
Then, by
[
62
, Theorem 1]
,
if
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
is stable
of degree
0
,
there exists a harmonic metric
h
of

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

such that

h
and
h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}
are mutually bounded
(Theorem
4.1
).
Let us note that the proof allows us to
obtain the inequality for the Donaldson functional

M(h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits},h)\leq 0
(Proposition
4.4
).
This inequality is useful for the study of
the continuity of the family of harmonic metrics
of some family of good filtered
\lambda
-flat bundles
(Proposition
4.5
).

For the higher dimensional case,
we use the same strategy in
[
46
,
49
]
and
[
51
]
.
It is a key step to study the case
\dim X=2
.
There are two naive ideas which are not available as they are.

One is to apply
[
62
, Theorem 1]

by constructing a Hermitian metric
h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}
of
V

such that

(i)

\mathcal{P}_{\ast}^{h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}}V=\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}
,

(ii)

G(h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits})
is dominated in an appropriate way,

(iii)

\det(h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits})
is flat.
For the construction of such a Hermitian metric
h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}
,
a compatibility condition seems necessary
between the nilpotent parts of the induced endomorphisms

\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{i}(\mathbb{D}^{\lambda})
and
\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{j}(\mathbb{D}^{\lambda})

on
{}^{i,j}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V})
.
(See Section
3.5.3
for
the endomorphisms
\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{i}\big(\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
.)
Once we prove the existence of a pluri-harmonic,
it turns out that such a compatibility condition is satisfied.
However, before proving the existence,
it is not clear whether such a compatibility condition
is satisfied. As a result, it is difficult to construct
a Hermitian metric
h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}
with the desired property,
in general.

The other is to use Mehta–Ramanathan type theorem
(Proposition
3.8
),
according to which
there exists
m>0
such that
for the
0
-set
Y\subset X
of a generic section of

H^{0}\big(X,L^{\otimes m}\big)
,
the restriction

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)_{|Y}
is also stable.
Hence,
if we fix a flat metric
h_{\det(\mathcal{V})}

of
\det(\mathcal{V})_{|X\setminus H}

adapted to
\det(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})
,
there exists a harmonic metric

h_{Y}
of

\big(V,\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)_{|Y\setminus H}

adapted to
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{|Y}

such that

\det(h_{Y})=h_{\det(\mathcal{V})|Y\setminus H}
.
If we can prove that there exists a Hermitian metric
h
of
V

such that
h_{|Y}=h_{Y}
for such generic hypersurfaces
Y
,
then
h
should be the desired pluri-harmonic metric
for
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
.
But, the existence of such
h
is not clear.

Roughly speaking, we combine these two ideas
as follows.
For any small
\epsilon>0
,
there exists a filtered bundle

\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}
over
\mathcal{V}

such that

(i)

\big(\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

is a
\mu_{L}
-stable good filtered
\lambda
-flat bundle,

(ii)

\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{i}\big(\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
are semisimple
for
\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}
,

(iii)

\det\big(\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}\big)=\det(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})
,

(iv)
the difference of

\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}

and
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}
are dominated by
\epsilon
.
(See Section
3.7.2

for more precise conditions.)
The last condition implies that

\lim\limits_{\epsilon\to 0}\int\mathop{\rm ch}\nolimits_{2}\big(\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\big)=0
.
For
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}^{(\epsilon)}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
,
we can construct
h^{(\epsilon)}_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}

such that

(i)

\mathcal{P}_{\ast}^{h^{(\epsilon)}_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}}V=\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}
,

(ii)

G\big(h^{(\epsilon)}_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}\big)
is dominated in an appropriate way,

(iii)

\det\big(h^{(\epsilon)}_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}\big)=h_{\det(\mathcal{V})}
. By
[
62
, Theorem 1]
,
there exists a Hermitian–Einstein metric
h^{(\epsilon)}_{\mathop{\rm HE}\nolimits}

of
\big(V,\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
such that

h^{(\epsilon)}_{\mathop{\rm HE}\nolimits}
and
h^{(\epsilon)}_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}

are mutually bounded,
and that
\det\big(h^{(\epsilon)}_{\mathop{\rm HE}\nolimits}\big)=h_{\det(\mathcal{V})}
.
(See Section
3.1
for Hermitian–Einstein metrics
of Higgs bundles.)
Moreover,
G\big(h^{(\epsilon)}_{\mathop{\rm HE}\nolimits}\big)\to 0
in
L^{2}
as
\epsilon\to 0
.
Hence, we would like to construct
the desired pluri-harmonic metric
as
\lim\limits_{\epsilon\to 0}h^{(\epsilon)}_{\mathop{\rm HE}\nolimits}
.
If
\epsilon
is sufficiently small,

\big(\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)_{|Y}

is also stable for the
0
-set
Y

of a generic section of
H^{0}\big(X,L^{\otimes m}\big)
,
and hence

\big(\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)_{|Y}

has a harmonic metric
h^{(\epsilon)}_{Y}

such that
\det\big(h^{(\epsilon)}_{Y}\big)=h_{\det(\mathcal{V})|Y\setminus H}
. By the continuity of a family of harmonic metrics mentioned above,
the sequence
h^{(\epsilon)}_{Y}
is convergent to
h_{Y}

as
\epsilon\to 0

(Proposition
4.5
).
Because
h^{(\epsilon)}_{\mathop{\rm HE}\nolimits|Y}
is not necessarily
a harmonic metric of

\big(\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)_{|Y}
,
it is not necessarily equal to
h^{(\epsilon)}_{Y}
.
But, because the
L^{2}
-norm of
G\big(h^{(\epsilon)}_{\mathop{\rm HE}\nolimits|Y}\big)

is dominated by
\epsilon
,
we can deduce the convergence of
the sequence
h^{(\epsilon)}_{\mathop{\rm HE}\nolimits|Y}
to
h_{Y}
as
\epsilon\to 0

(Proposition
4.8
).
Hence, we obtain the convergence of
h^{(\epsilon)}_{\mathop{\rm HE}\nolimits}

almost everywhere, and the limit satisfies

h_{|Y}=h_{Y}
for the
0
-set of generic section
s

of
H^{0}\big(X,L^{\otimes m}\big)
.
Thus, we can prove the theorem in the case
\dim X=2
.
(See Section
7.2
for a more precise argument.)

In the case
\dim X\geq 3
,
we use an induction on
\dim X
. By the Mehta–Ramanathan type theorem,
there exists
m>0
such that
for the
0
-sets
Y_{i}

(i=1,2)
of generic sections of

H^{0}\big(X,L^{\otimes m}\big)
,

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)_{|Y_{i}}

and
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)_{|Y_{1}\cap Y_{2}}

are
\mu_{L}
-stable. By fixing a flat metric

h_{\det(\mathcal{V})}
for
\det\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
,
there exist pluri-harmonic metric
h_{Y_{i}}
of

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)_{|Y_{i}}

such that
\det(h_{Y_{i}})=h_{\det(\mathcal{V})|Y_{i}\setminus H}
.
Because
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)_{|Y_{1}\cap Y_{2}}

is also
\mu_{L}
-stable,
we obtain that

h_{Y_{1}|(Y_{1}\cap Y_{2})\setminus H}=h_{Y_{2}|(Y_{1}\cap Y_{2})\setminus H}
.
Hence,
there exists a Hermitian metric
h
of

V_{|X\setminus(H\cup W)}
for a finite subset
W
,
such that

h_{|Y\setminus H}=h_{Y}

for the
0
-set
Y
of a generic section of

H^{0}\big(X,L^{\otimes m}\big)
.
It is easy to see that
h
is the desired pluri-harmonic metric.
(See Section
7.3
for a more precise argument.)

3
Preliminaries

3.1
Hermitian–Einstein metrics of
\boldsymbol{\lambda}
-flat bundles

Let
Y
be a Kähler manifold with a Kähler form
\omega
.
Let
\big(E,\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
be a
\lambda
-flat bundle on
Y

with a Hermitian metric.
Recall that
h
is called a Hermitian–Einstein metric
of the
\lambda
-flat bundle
if
\Lambda_{\omega}G(h)^{\bot}=0
,
where
G(h)^{\bot}
denote the trace-free part of

G(h)
,
and
\Lambda_{\omega}
denote the adjoint
of the multiplication by
\omega
(see
[
35
, Section 3.2]
).
The following is a generalization of
Kobayashi–Lübke inequality
to the context of
\lambda
-flat bundles
due to Simpson
[
62
, Proposition 3.4]
.

Proposition 3.1

(Simpson)
.

If
h
is a Hermitian–Einstein metric,
there exists
C>0
depending only on
n=\dim Y

such that the following holds:

\displaystyle\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\bigl(\big(G(h)^{\bot}\big)^{2}\bigr)\omega^{n-2}=C\bigl|G(h)^{\bot}\bigr|_{h,\omega}^{2}\omega^{n}.

As a result,
if
\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\bigl(\big(G(h)^{\bot}\big)^{2}\bigr)\omega^{n-2}=0
,
then we obtain
G(h)^{\bot}=0
.

3.2
Rank one case

Let
X
be an
n
dimensional smooth connected
projective variety
with a simple normal crossing hypersurface
H
.
Let
\omega
be a Kähler form.
Let
H=\bigcup_{i\in\Lambda}H_{i}
be
the irreducible decomposition.
Let
g_{i}
be a
C^{\infty}
-Hermitian metric
of the line bundle
\mathcal{O}(H_{i})
.
Let
\sigma_{i}
denote the section of
\mathcal{O}_{X}(H_{i})

induced by
the inclusion
\mathcal{O}_{X}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}(H_{i})
.

Let
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
be a good filtered

\lambda
-flat bundle on
(X,H)
of rank one.
For each
i\in\Lambda
, there uniquely exists

a_{i}\in]{-}1,0]
such that

{}^{i}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{a_{i}}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})\neq 0
.
Let
A
be the constant determined by

\displaystyle A\int_{X}\omega^{n}=2\pi n\big(1+|\lambda|^{2}\big)\int_{X}c_{1}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})\omega^{n-1}.

The following proposition is standard.

Proposition 3.2
.

There exists a Hermitian metric
h

of the line bundle
E:=\mathcal{V}_{|X\setminus D}

such that

(i)

\sqrt{-1}\Lambda_{\omega}G(h)=A
,

(ii)

h\prod_{i\in\Lambda}|\sigma_{i}|_{g_{i}}^{2a_{i}}

is a Hermitian metric of
\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}(\mathcal{V})

of
C^{\infty}
-class.
Such a metric is unique up to
the multiplication by a positive constant.
Moreover,
if
c_{1}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})=0
,
then
R(h)=0
holds,
and hence

h
is a pluri-harmonic metric
of
\big(E,\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
.

Proof.

Note that
G(h)=\big(1+|\lambda|^{2}\big)R(h)
holds in the rank one case.
(See
[
49
, Lemma 2.31]
.)
Let
h_{0}^{\prime}
be a
C^{\infty}
-metric of

\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}E
. We obtain the metric

h_{0}:=h_{0}^{\prime}\cdot\prod_{i\in\Lambda}|\sigma_{i}|^{-2a_{i}}_{g_{i}}

of
E
on
X\setminus H
.
It is well known that

\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}R(h_{0})
naturally extends to
a closed
(1,1)
-form on
X
of
C^{\infty}
-class
which represents
c_{1}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}E)
. By the condition of
A
,
we obtain

\int_{X}\bigl(\sqrt{-1}\Lambda_{\omega}R(h_{0})-\big(1+|\lambda|^{2}\big)^{-1}A\bigr)\omega^{n}=0
.
Note that

\sqrt{-1}\Lambda_{\omega}R(h_{0}{\rm e}^{\varphi})=\sqrt{-1}\Lambda_{\omega}R(h_{0})+\sqrt{-1}\Lambda_{\omega}\overline{\partial}\partial\varphi
.
Hence,
there exists
an
{\mathbb{R}}
-valued
C^{\infty}
-function
\varphi_{0}

such that

\sqrt{-1}\Lambda_{\omega}R(h_{0}{\rm e}^{\varphi_{0}})-\big(1+|\lambda|^{2}\big)^{-1}A=0
.
The metric
h=h_{0}{\rm e}^{\varphi_{0}}
has the desired property.
The uniqueness is clear.

Suppose that
c_{1}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}E)=0
. In the rank one case,
a Hermitian metric of
E
is
a pluri-harmonic metric of
\big(E,\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
,
if and only if
R(h)=0
.
Because the cohomology class of
R(h_{0})
is
0
,
there exists
an
{\mathbb{R}}
-valued
C^{\infty}
-function
\varphi_{0}

such that

R(h_{0}{\rm e}^{\varphi_{0}})=0

by the standard
\partial\overline{\partial}
-lemma. By the uniqueness, we obtain the second claim of the lemma.
∎

For the metric
h
in Proposition
3.2
,

\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}R(h)
induces
a closed
(1,1)
-form on
X
of
C^{\infty}
-class
which represents
c_{1}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}E)
.

3.3
\boldsymbol{\beta}
-subobject and socle for reflexive filtered
\boldsymbol{\lambda}
-flat sheaves

Let
X
and
H
be as in Section
3.2
.
Let
L
be an ample line bundle
L
on
X
.
For any coherent
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module
\mathcal{M}
,
we set
\deg_{L}(\mathcal{M}):=\int_{X}c_{1}(\mathcal{M})c_{1}(L)^{\dim X-1}
.

3.3.1
\boldsymbol{\beta}
-subobjects

Let
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

be a reflexive filtered
\lambda
-flat sheaf on
(X,H)
.
For any
A\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
let
\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{0}}}\mathcal{V},A)

denote the family of saturated coherent subsheaves
\mathcal{F}

of
\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{0}}}\mathcal{V}

such that
\deg_{L}(\mathcal{F})\geq-A

and that

\mathcal{F}(\ast H)
is a
\lambda
-flat subsheaf of
\mathcal{V}
.
Any
\mathcal{F}\in\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{0}}}\mathcal{V},A)

induces a reflexive filtered sheaf

\mathcal{P}_{\ast}(\mathcal{F}(\ast H))

by
\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{c}}}(\mathcal{F}(\ast H)):=\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{c}}}\mathcal{V}\cap\mathcal{F}(\ast H)

for any
{\boldsymbol{c}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\Lambda}
. We set
f_{A}(\mathcal{F}):=\mu_{L}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}(\mathcal{F}(\ast H)))
.
Thus, we obtain a function

f_{A}
on
\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{0}}}\mathcal{V},A)
.

Lemma 3.3
.

The image

f_{A}\bigl(\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{0}}}\mathcal{V},A)\bigr)

is a finite subset of
{\mathbb{R}}
. In particular,

f_{A}
has the maximum.

Proof.

According to
[
20
, Lemma 2.5]
,
the family
\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{0}}}\mathcal{V},A)

is bounded.
Hence, by using the flattening stratifications
[
57
, Section 8]
,
it is easy to see that
there exists a finite decomposition

\mathcal{S}(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{0}}}\mathcal{V},A)=\coprod_{i=1}^{N}\mathcal{S}_{i}(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{0}}}\mathcal{V},A)

such that

f_{A}
is constant on each
\mathcal{S}_{i}(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{0}}}\mathcal{V},A)
.
∎

It is standard that any reflexive filtered
\lambda
-flat sheaf
has a
\beta
-subobject,
i.e., the following holds.

Proposition 3.4
.

For any reflexive filtered
\lambda
-flat sheaf

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
,
there uniquely exists a non-zero
\lambda
-flat
subsheaf
\mathcal{V}_{0}\subset\mathcal{V}

such that the following holds
for any non-zero reflexive
\lambda
-flat subsheaf
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{V}
:

•

\mu_{L}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}^{\prime})\leq\mu_{L}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{0})

holds.

•

If
\mu_{L}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}^{\prime})=\mu_{L}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{0})

holds,
then we obtain
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{V}_{0}
.

Proof.

By the formula (
2.1
),
there exists
N>0
such that
the following holds for any
saturated subsheaf
\mathcal{F}\subset\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{0}}}\mathcal{V}
:

\displaystyle\left|\deg_{L}(\mathcal{F})-\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(\mathcal{F})\mu_{L}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{F}(\ast H))\right|<N.

We set
A_{0}:=|\deg_{L}(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{0}}}\mathcal{V})|+10N
.
Let
B_{0}
denote the maximum of
f_{A_{0}}
.
Then, it is easy to see that

\mu_{L}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}^{\prime})\leq B_{0}

for any saturated
\lambda
-flat subsheaf
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{V}
.
Moreover,
if
\mu_{L}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}^{\prime})=B_{0}
,
then
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}^{\prime},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{V}^{\prime}}\big)

is
\mu_{L}
-semistable,
where
\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{V}^{\prime}}
denote
the flat
\lambda
-connection
induced by
\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}
.

Suppose that the
\lambda
-flat subsheaves

\mathcal{V}_{i}\subset\mathcal{V}

(i=1,2)
satisfy

\mu_{L}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{i})=B_{0}
. We obtain the subsheaf

\mathcal{V}_{1}+\mathcal{V}_{2}\subset\mathcal{V}
.
Because
\mathcal{V}_{1}+\mathcal{V}_{2}
is a quotient of

\mathcal{V}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{V}_{2}
,
we obtain a filtered sheaf
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}(\mathcal{V}_{1}+\mathcal{V}_{2})

over
\mathcal{V}_{1}+\mathcal{V}_{2}
.
induced by
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{2}
.
Then, by the
\mu_{L}
-semistability of

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{i},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{i}\big)
,
we obtain that

B_{0}=\mu_{L}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{2})\leq\mu_{L}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}(\mathcal{V}_{1}+\mathcal{V}_{2}))
.
Let
\mathcal{V}_{3}
denote the saturated subsheaf of
\mathcal{V}

generated by
\mathcal{V}_{1}+\mathcal{V}_{2}
. We obtain a filtered sheaf
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{3}

by
\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V}_{3}=\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}(\mathcal{V})\cap\mathcal{V}_{3}
.
Because the natural morphism

\mathcal{P}_{\ast}(\mathcal{V}_{1}+\mathcal{V}_{2})\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{3}

is generically an isomorphism,
we obtain

\mu_{L}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}(\mathcal{V}_{1}+\mathcal{V}_{2}))\leq\mu_{L}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{3})\leq B_{0}

by Lemma
2.5
.
Hence, we obtain
\mu_{L}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{3})=B_{0}
.
Then, the claim of the lemma is clear.
∎

3.3.2
Socle

Let
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

be a
\mu_{L}
-semistable reflexive filtered
\lambda
-flat sheaf
on
(X,H)
.
Let
\mathcal{T}

denote the family of saturated
\lambda
-flat
subsheaves
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{V}

such that
the induced filtered
\lambda
-flat sheaf

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}^{\prime},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{V}^{\prime}}\big)
is
\mu_{L}
-stable
with
\mu_{L}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}^{\prime})=\mu_{L}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})
.
Let
\mathcal{V}_{1}
be the saturated
\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H)
-submodule of

\mathcal{V}

generated by
\sum_{\mathcal{V}^{\prime}\in\mathcal{T}}\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
.
It is a
\lambda
-flat subsheaf of
\mathcal{V}
.

Proposition 3.5
.

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{1},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{V}_{1}}\big)

is equal to the direct sum

\bigoplus_{k=1}^{\ell}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}^{(k)},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{V}^{(k)}}\big)

of a tuple of
\mu_{L}
-stable filtered
\lambda
-flat subsheaves
of
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
. In particular,

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{1},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{1}\big)

is
\mu_{L}
-polystable.
The filtered
\lambda
-flat subsheaf

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{1},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{V}_{1}}\big)

is called the socle of
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
.

Proof.

Let
\mathcal{V}^{(i)}

(i=1,2)
be saturated
\lambda
-flat
subsheaves of
\mathcal{V}
such that

(i)

\mu_{L}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}^{(i)}\big)=\mu_{L}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})
,

(ii)

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}^{(1)},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{V}^{(1)}}\big)
is
\mu_{L}
-semistable,

(iii)

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}^{(2)},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{V}^{(2)}}\big)
is
\mu_{L}
-stable.

Lemma 3.6
.

Either
\mathcal{V}^{(2)}\subset\mathcal{V}^{(1)}

or
\mathcal{V}^{(1)}\cap\mathcal{V}^{(2)}=0
holds.

Proof.

Let us consider the morphism

\iota_{1}-\iota_{2}\colon\mathcal{V}^{(1)}\oplus\mathcal{V}^{(2)}\longrightarrow\mathcal{V}
,
where
\iota_{i}\colon\mathcal{V}^{(i)}\longrightarrow\mathcal{V}

denote the inclusions.
Let
\mathcal{K}
denote the kernel. We obtain a filtered sheaf
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{K}

over
\mathcal{K}

by
\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{K}:=\mathcal{K}\cap\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}(\mathcal{V}_{1}\oplus\mathcal{V}_{2})
.
The projection

\mathcal{V}^{(1)}\oplus\mathcal{V}^{(2)}\longrightarrow\mathcal{V}^{(2)}

induces

\mathcal{K}\simeq\mathcal{V}^{(1)}\cap\mathcal{V}^{(2)}=:\mathcal{I}
.
It induces a morphism of filtered
\lambda
-flat sheaves

g\colon\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{K},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{K}}\big)\longrightarrow\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}^{(2)},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{V}^{(2)}}\big)
. We set

\mu_{0}:=\mu_{L}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})
.
Because

\bigoplus_{i=1,2}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}^{(i)},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{V}^{(i)}}\big)

and
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
are
\mu_{L}
-semistable
with the same slope
\mu_{0}
,
we obtain that

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{K},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{K}}\big)

is also
\mu_{L}
-semistable
with
\mu_{L}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{K})=\mu_{0}
.

Suppose that
\mathcal{K}\neq 0
,
i.e.,
\mathcal{I}\neq 0
.
Because
\mathcal{I}
is a subsheaf of
\mathcal{V}^{(2)}
,
we also obtain a filtered sheaf
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{I}

induced by
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}^{(2)}
.
Because
\mathcal{I}\simeq\mathcal{K}
,
we obtain a filtered sheaf
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{\ast}\mathcal{I}
over
\mathcal{I}

induced by
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{K}
.
Then, we obtain

\displaystyle\mu_{0}=\mu_{L}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{K})=\mu_{L}(\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{\ast}\mathcal{I})\leq\mu_{L}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{I})\leq\mu_{L}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}^{(2)}\big)=\mu_{0}.

Because
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}^{(2)},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{V}^{(2)}}\big)

is
\mu_{L}
-stable
and because
\mathcal{I}\neq 0
,
we obtain that

\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(\mathcal{I})=\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits\mathcal{V}^{(2)}
,
i.e.,
\mathcal{I}
and
\mathcal{V}^{(2)}

are generically isomorphic.
Because
\mu_{L}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{I})=\mu_{L}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}^{(2)}\big)
,
Lemma
2.5
implies that

\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{I}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}^{(2)}

is an isomorphism in codimension
1
.
Hence, there exists a closed algebraic subset
Z\subset X

such that

(i)
the codimension of
Z
is larger than
2
,

(ii)

\mathcal{V}^{(2)}_{|X\setminus Z}\subset\mathcal{V}^{(1)}_{|X\setminus Z}
.
Because
\mathcal{V}^{(1)}
is reflexive
we obtain that
\mathcal{V}^{(2)}\subset\mathcal{V}^{(1)}
.
∎

Let us study the case where
\mathcal{V}^{(1)}\cap\mathcal{V}^{(2)}=0
.
Let
\mathcal{V}^{(3)}
denote
the saturated
\lambda
-flat subsheaf of
\mathcal{V}
generated by

\mathcal{V}^{(1)}+\mathcal{V}^{(2)}
.
Let
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}^{(3)}

denote the filtered sheaf over
\mathcal{V}^{(3)}

induced by
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}
.

Lemma 3.7
.

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}^{(3)},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{V}^{(3)}}\big)

is
\mu_{L}
-semistable,
and the induced morphism

g\colon\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}^{(1)}\oplus\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}^{(2)}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}^{(3)}

is an isomorphism in codimension one.

Proof.

We obtain

\mu_{0}=\mu_{L}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\big(\mathcal{V}^{(1)}\oplus\mathcal{V}^{(2)}\big)\big)\leq\mu_{L}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}^{(3)}\big)\leq\mu_{L}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})=\mu_{0}
.
Hence, we obtain that

\mu_{L}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}^{(3)}\big)=\mu_{0}
and that

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}^{(3)},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{V}^{(3)}}\big)

is
\mu_{L}
-semistable.
Because

g\colon\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}^{(1)}\oplus\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}^{(2)}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}^{(3)}

is generically an isomorphism,
and because they have the same slope,

g
is an isomorphism in codimension one
by Lemma
2.5
.
∎

By Lemma
3.7
,
it is easy to observe that
there exists a finite sequence
of reflexive
\lambda
-flat subsheaves
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{j}

(j=1,\ldots,m)

such that

(i)
the induced filtered
\lambda
-flat sheaves

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{j},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{\mathcal{V}_{j}^{\prime}}\big)
are
\mu_{L}
-stable,

(ii)
the image of the induced morphism

g\colon\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}:=\bigoplus\mathcal{V}^{\prime}_{j}\longrightarrow\mathcal{V}_{1}

is generically an isomorphism.
Because

\mu_{0}=\mu_{L}\bigl(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}\bigr)\leq\mu_{L}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{1})\leq\mu_{L}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})=\mu_{0}
,
we obtain that

\mu_{L}\bigl(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}\bigr)=\mu_{L}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{1})=\mu_{L}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})
.
Hence,

g
is an isomorphism in codimension one
by Lemma
2.5
.
Because both
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}

and
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{1}

are reflexive,
we obtain that

\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}\simeq\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{1}
.
Thus, we obtain Proposition
3.5
.
∎

3.4
Mehta–Ramanathan type theorems

Let
X
be a smooth connected
n
-dimensional
projective variety
with a simple normal crossing hypersurface
H
.
Let
H=\bigcup_{i\in\Lambda}H_{i}
be the irreducible decomposition.
Let
L
be an ample line bundle on
X
.

3.4.1
Restriction to general curves

Let
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
be
a reflexive filtered
\lambda
-flat sheaf
on
(X,H)
.
There exists a Zariski closed subset

W\subset X
with
\dim W<\dim H

such that

(i)
the singular locus of
H
is contained in
W
,

(ii)

\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{|X\setminus W}

is a filtered bundle on
(X\setminus W,H\setminus W)
.

Let
Y
be a smooth curve in
X
such that

(i)

Y\cap W=\varnothing
,

(ii)

Y
intersects with the smooth part of
H
transversally.
Set
H_{Y}:=H\cap Y
. We obtain a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{Y}(\ast H_{Y})
-module

\mathcal{V}_{|Y}
.
It is equipped with the induced flat
\lambda
-connection

\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{|Y}
.
Let
{\boldsymbol{b}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{H_{Y}}
.
For any
P\in H_{Y}
,
there exists
i\in\Lambda

such that
P\in H_{i}
. We choose
{\boldsymbol{a}}(P,{\boldsymbol{b}})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\Lambda}

such that
a(P,{\boldsymbol{b}})_{i}=b(P)
,
and we obtain
an
\mathcal{O}_{Y,P}
-submodule

\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{b}}}\big(\mathcal{V}_{|Y}\big)_{P}:=\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}(P,{\boldsymbol{b}})}(\mathcal{V})_{P}

of
\big(\mathcal{V}_{|Y}\big)_{P}
,
which is independent of the choice of
{\boldsymbol{a}}(P,{\boldsymbol{b}})

as above.
There exists a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{Y}
-module

\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{b}}}\big(\mathcal{V}_{|Y}\big)\subset\mathcal{V}_{|Y}

whose stalk at
P
is
\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{b}}}\big(\mathcal{V}_{|Y}\big)_{P}
.
Thus, we obtain a filtered
\lambda
-flat bundle

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\big(\mathcal{V}_{|Y}\big),\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{|Y}\big)

which is denoted by
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)_{|Y}
.

3.4.2
The stability condition

Proposition 3.8
.

A reflexive filtered
\lambda
-flat sheaf

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

on
(X,H)

is
\mu_{L}
-stable (resp.
\mu_{L}
-semistable)
if and only if the following holds:

•

For any
m_{1}>0
,
there exists
m>m_{1}

such that

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)_{|Y}

is
\mu_{L}
-stable
(
resp.
\mu_{L}
-semistable
)
,
where
Y
denotes a generic
1
-dimensional
complete intersection of
hypersurfaces of
L^{\otimes m}
.

Proof.

The case
\lambda=1
is already studied in
[
51
, Section 13.2]
.
The case
\lambda\neq 0
is reduced to the case
\lambda=1
.
As for the case
\lambda=0
,
we can prove the claim of the proposition
by the argument in
[
46
, Section 3.4]
,
which closely follows
the arguments of Mehta–Ramanathan
[
43
,
44
]

and Simpson
[
64
]
. We use
\mathcal{W}=\Omega^{1}(ND)

for a large
N
instead of
\Omega^{1}(\log D)

in
[
46
, Section 3.4]
.
(See also
[
51
, Section 13.2]
.)
∎

3.4.3
Restrictions of morphisms and the polystability condition

Let us give a complement on
the restriction of
morphisms of reflexive filtered
\lambda
-flat sheaves
to generic complete intersection curves,
which is a variant of
[
64
, Lemma 3.9]
.
Let
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{i},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

(i=1,2)

be reflexive filtered
\lambda
-flat sheaves
on
(X,H)
.
Let
\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits\bigl(\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{1},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{1}\big),\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{2},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{2}\big)\bigr)

denote the vector space of
morphisms of filtered
\lambda
-flat sheaves

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{1},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{1}\big)\longrightarrow\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{2},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{2}\big)
. We shall prove
a refined claim (Proposition
3.16
)
of the following proposition
in Sections
3.4.4
–
3.4.6
.

Proposition 3.9
.

There exists a positive integer
m_{0}>0

such that the restriction

\displaystyle\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits\bigl(\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{1},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{1}\big),\bigl(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{2},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{2}\bigl)\bigr)\longrightarrow\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits\bigl(\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{1},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{1}\big)_{|Y},\bigl(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{2},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{2}\bigl)_{|Y}\bigr)

is an isomorphism
for a generic
1
-dimensional complete intersection
Y

of hypersurfaces of
L^{\otimes m}

(m\geq m_{0})
.

Before going to the proof of Proposition
3.9
,
we state a variant of Proposition
3.8

on the
\mu_{L}
-polystability condition.

Corollary 3.10
.

A reflexive filtered
\lambda
-flat sheaf

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

on
(X,H)

is
\mu_{L}
-polystable
if and only if the following holds:

•

For any
m_{1}>0
,
there exists
m>m_{1}

such that

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)_{|Y}

is
\mu_{L}
-polystable,
where
Y
denotes the
1
-dimensional
complete intersection of
generic hypersurfaces of
L^{\otimes m}
.

Proof.

If
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
is

\mu_{L}
-polystable,
we obtain a decomposition

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)=\bigoplus\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{i},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{i}\big)

into
\mu_{L}
-stable filtered
\lambda
-flat sheaves.
Applying Proposition
3.8

to each stable component,
we obtain the “only if” claim.

Let
m_{1}
be an integer larger than
m_{0}

in Proposition
3.9

for

\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits\bigl(\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big),\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)\bigr)
.
Suppose that there exists
m>m_{1}

such that

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)_{|Y}

is
\mu_{L}
-polystable
for a generic
1
-dimensional
complete intersection
Y
of
hypersurfaces of
L^{\otimes m}
.
We obtain the decomposition

\displaystyle\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)_{|Y}=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{\ell}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{Y,i},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{Y,i}\big)

(3.1)

into stable filtered Higgs bundles.
Let
\pi_{Y,i}
denote the endomorphisms of

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)_{|Y}

obtained by composing the projection

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)_{|Y}\longrightarrow\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{Y,i},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{Y,i}\big)

with respect to
the decomposition (
3.1
),
with the inclusion

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{Y,i},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{Y,i}\big)\longrightarrow\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)_{|Y}
.
Note that they satisfy

\pi_{Y,i}\circ\pi_{Y,i}=\pi_{Y,i}
,

\pi_{Y,i}\circ\pi_{Y,j}=0

(i\neq j)

and

\sum\pi_{Y,i}=\mathop{\rm id}\nolimits
. By Proposition
3.9
,
there uniquely exist the endomorphisms

\pi_{i}
of
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

such that
\pi_{i|Y}=\pi_{Y,i}
. By Proposition
3.9
again,
they satisfy

\pi_{i}\circ\pi_{i}=\pi_{i}
,

\pi_{i}\circ\pi_{j}=0

(i\neq j)

and

\sum\pi_{i}=\mathop{\rm id}\nolimits
.
Let
\mathcal{V}_{i}\subset\mathcal{V}
denote the image of
\pi_{i}
. We define

\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V}_{i}=\mathcal{V}_{i}\cap\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V}

for any
{\boldsymbol{a}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\Lambda}
.
Because
\pi_{i}
are compatible with
\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}

and the filtration
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}
,
we obtain the decomposition

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)=\bigoplus\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{i},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{i}\big)
.
By the construction,

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{i},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{i}\big)_{|Y}=\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{Y,i},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{Y,i}\big)

are stable.
Hence,

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{i},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{i}\big)

are

\mu_{L}
-stable with

\mu_{L}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{i},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{i}\big)=\mu_{L}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{j},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{j}\big)

(i\neq j)
, i.e.,

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

is
\mu_{L}
-polystable.
∎

3.4.4
General Enriques–Severi lemma due to Mehta–Ramanathan

To prove Proposition
3.9
,
we recall the general Enriques–Severi lemma
in
[
43
]
.
Recall
n=\dim X
.
For a positive integer
m
,
let
S_{m}
denote the projective space of
lines in
H^{0}\big(X,L^{\otimes m}\big)
.
For sequences
{\boldsymbol{m}}=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{t})\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}^{t}

with
t\leq n-1
,
we set
S_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}:=\prod S_{m_{i}}
.
There exists the correspondence variety

Z_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}\subset X\times S_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}
,
i.e.,

Z_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}=\bigl\{(x,s_{1},\ldots,s_{t})\in X\times S_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}\mid s_{i}(x)=0,\,1\leq i\leq t\bigr\}
.
For any
s\in S_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}
, we set

X_{s}:=Z_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}\times_{S_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}}\times\{s\}
.

Let
F
be a coherent reflexive
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-module on
X
.
For any
{\boldsymbol{m}}=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{t})\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}^{t}

with
t\leq n-1
,
and for any
s\in S_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}
,
we set
F_{s}:=F\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}_{X_{s}}
.
For any integer
m
,
let
F_{s}(m)=F_{s}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}L^{\otimes m}
.

According to
[
43
, Proposition 1.5]
,
there exists a non-empty Zariski open subset

\widetilde{S}_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}\subset S_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}

such that the following holds.

•

\widetilde{S}_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}\times_{S_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}}Z_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}\longrightarrow\widetilde{S}_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}
is smooth.

•

For any
s\in S_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}
,

F_{s}
is a reflexive
\mathcal{O}_{X_{s}}
-module.

In the proof of
[
43
, Proposition 3.2]
,
the following proposition is proved.

Proposition 3.11
.

Let
t\leq n-2
.
There exists a positive integer
m_{0}

depending only on
F
such that the following holds:

•

For any
{\boldsymbol{m}}=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{t})\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}^{t}

with
m_{i}\geq m_{0}
,
there exists a non-empty Zariski open subset

U\subset\widetilde{S}_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}

such that
H^{1}(X_{s},F_{s}(-\ell))=0

for any
s\in U
and any
\ell\geq m_{0}
.

Corollary 3.12
.

Let
t\leq n-1
.
There exists a positive integer
m_{0}

depending only on
F
such that the following holds:

•

For any
{\boldsymbol{m}}=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{t})\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}^{t}

with
m_{i}\geq m_{0}
,
there exists a non-empty Zariski open subset

U\subset\widetilde{S}_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}
such that

H^{0}(X_{s},F_{s}(-\ell))=0

for any
s\in U

and any
\ell\geq m_{0}
.

Proof.

Let
m_{0}
be a positive integer
as in Proposition
3.11
. We also assume
H^{0}(X,F(-\ell))=0

for any
\ell\geq m_{0}
. We use an induction on
t
.
For
{\boldsymbol{m}}=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{t})
with
m_{i}\geq m_{0}
,
we set

{\boldsymbol{m}}^{\prime}=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{t-1})
. By the assumption of the induction
and Proposition
3.11
,
there exists a non-empty Zariski open subset

U_{1}^{\prime}\subset\widetilde{S}_{{\boldsymbol{m}}^{\prime}}

such that

H^{i}(X_{s^{\prime}},F_{s^{\prime}}(-\ell))=0

(i=0,1)

for any
s^{\prime}\in U_{1}^{\prime}

and any
\ell\geq m_{0}
.

For any
s\in S_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}
,
let
s^{\prime}
denote the image
s
in
S_{{\boldsymbol{m}}^{\prime}}

by the projection
S_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}\longrightarrow S_{{\boldsymbol{m}}^{\prime}}
.
There exists the exact sequence

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X_{s^{\prime}}}(-m_{t})\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X_{s^{\prime}}}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X_{s}}\longrightarrow 0.

(3.2)

By
[
43
, Proposition 1.5]
,
there exists a Zariski open subset

U_{1}\subset\widetilde{S}_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}

such that if
s\in U_{1}

then we obtain the following exact sequence
from (
3.2
)
by taking the tensor product with
F
:

\displaystyle 0\longrightarrow F_{s^{\prime}}(-m_{t})\longrightarrow F_{s^{\prime}}\longrightarrow F_{s}\longrightarrow 0.

We shrink
U_{1}
so that

U_{1}^{\prime}
contains the image of
U_{1}

by the projection
S_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}\longrightarrow S_{{\boldsymbol{m}}^{\prime}}
.
Let
\ell\geq m_{0}
.
For any
s\in U_{1}
,
we obtain

H^{0}(X_{s^{\prime}},F_{s^{\prime}}(-\ell))=0

and

H^{1}(X_{s^{\prime}},F_{s^{\prime}}(-\ell-m_{t}))=0

because
s^{\prime}\in U_{1}^{\prime}
.
Hence, we obtain

H^{0}(X_{s},F_{s}(-\ell))=0
.
∎

Corollary 3.13
.

Let
t\leq n-1
.
There exists
m_{0}
depending only on
F

such that the following holds:

•

For any
{\boldsymbol{m}}=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{t})\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}^{t}

with
m_{i}\geq m_{0}
,
there exists a non-empty Zariski open subset

U\subset\widetilde{S}_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}

such that
the natural morphism

H^{0}(X,F)\longrightarrow H^{0}(X_{s},F_{s})

is an isomorphism
for any
s\in U
.

Proof.

It is enough to apply the argument
in the first paragraph of the proof of
[
43
, Proposition 3.2]

with Proposition
3.11

and Corollary
3.12
.
(This is essentially pointed out
in
[
64
, Lemma 3.9]
.)
∎

Let
T^{\ast}_{X_{s}}X
denote
the conormal bundle of
X_{s}
in
X

for any
s\in\widetilde{S}_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}
.

Corollary 3.14
.

Let
t\leq n-1
.
There exists
m_{0}
depending only on
F

such that the following holds:

•

For any
{\boldsymbol{m}}=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{t})\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}^{t}

with
m_{i}\geq m_{0}
,
there exists a non-empty Zariski open subset

U\subset\widetilde{S}_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}
such that

H^{0}\big(X_{s},T^{\ast}_{X_{s}}X\otimes F_{s}\big)=0

for any
s\in U
.

Proof.

Because

T^{\ast}_{X_{s}}X\simeq\bigoplus_{j=1}^{t}\mathcal{O}_{X_{s}}(-m_{j})
,
the claim follows from Corollary
3.12
.
∎

3.4.5
Flat sections of reflexive filtered
\boldsymbol{\lambda}
-flat sheaves

Let
H
be a simple normal crossing hypersurface of
X

with the irreducible decomposition

H=\bigcup_{i\in\Lambda}H_{i}
.
Let
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

be a reflexive filtered
\lambda
-flat sheaf
on
(X,H)
.
Note that there exists a Zariski closed subset

W\subset X
with
\dim W<\dim H

such that

(i)

W
contains the singular locus of
H
,

(ii)

\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{|X\setminus W}

is a filtered bundle on
(X\setminus W,H\setminus W)
.
For any
{\boldsymbol{m}}\in{\mathbb{Z}}^{n-1}

and for any
s\in S_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}
,
we set
H_{s}:=X_{s}\times_{X}H
.

According to
[
43
, Proposition 1.5]
,
there exists a non-empty Zariski open subset

S^{\circ}_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}\subset S_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}

such that the following holds:

•

Z_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}\times_{S_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}}S^{\circ}_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}\longrightarrow S^{\circ}_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}

is smooth.

•

For any
s\in S^{\circ}_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}
,

X_{s}\cap W=\varnothing
holds,
and
X_{s}
intersects with
H

in
H\setminus W
transversally.
Moreover,

\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V}_{s}:=\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V}_{|X_{s}}

({\boldsymbol{a}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\Lambda})

are locally free
\mathcal{O}_{X_{s}}
-modules.

There exists a non-negative integer
N
such that

\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}
induces a morphism of sheaves

\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}
:
\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V}\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log H)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(NH)
.
For
j=0,1,\ldots,n
, we set

\displaystyle\mathcal{C}^{j}_{N}(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V})=\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V}\otimes\Omega^{j}_{X}(\log H)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(jNH).

The flat
\lambda
-connection
\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}
induces

\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\colon\mathcal{C}^{j}_{N}(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V})\longrightarrow\mathcal{C}^{j+1}_{N}(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V})

such that

\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\wedge\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}=0
.
Thus,
we obtain a complex of sheaves

\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}_{N}\big(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
on
X
.
Clearly, the following holds:

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}^{0}\bigl(X,\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}_{N}\big(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)\bigr)=\mathop{\rm Ker}\nolimits\Bigl(H^{0}(X,\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V})\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}}}{{\longrightarrow}}H^{0}\bigl(X,\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V}\otimes\Omega_{X}^{1}(\log H)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(NH)\bigr)\Bigr).

For any
s\in S^{\circ}_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}
,
we obtain the filtered
\lambda
-flat bundle

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{s},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{s}\big):=\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)_{|X_{s}}
.
Let
{\boldsymbol{a}}(s)
denote the image of

{\mathbb{R}}^{\Lambda}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{R}}^{H_{s}}

induced by the natural map
H_{s}\longrightarrow\Lambda
.
Let
\iota_{s}\colon X_{s}\longrightarrow X
denote the inclusion. We obtain the natural morphism of complexes of sheaves

\mathcal{C}_{N}^{\bullet}\big(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)\longrightarrow\iota_{s\ast}\mathcal{C}_{N}^{\bullet}\big(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}(s)}\mathcal{V}_{s},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{s}\big)
,
which induces

\displaystyle\mathbb{H}^{0}\big(X,\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}_{N}\big(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)\big)\longrightarrow\mathbb{H}^{0}\big(X_{s},\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}_{N}\big(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}(s)}\mathcal{V}_{s},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{s}\big)\big).

(3.3)

The following proposition is essentially
[
64
, Lemma 3.9]
.

Proposition 3.15
.

There exists a positive integer
m_{0}>0

such that the following claim holds
for any
{\boldsymbol{m}}=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{n-1})
with
m_{i}\geq m_{0}

and a non-empty Zariski open subset
U\subset S^{\circ}_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}
.

•

For any
s\in U
,
the natural morphism
(
3.3
)

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

According to Corollary
3.13
,
if
m_{0}
is sufficiently large,
there exists a non-empty Zariski open subset

U_{1}\subset S^{\circ}_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}

such that
the following natural morphisms are isomorphisms
for any
s\in U
:

\displaystyle H^{0}(X,\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V})\longrightarrow H^{0}(X_{s},\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}(s)}\mathcal{V}_{s}),

\displaystyle H^{0}\bigl(X,\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log H)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(NH)\bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad{}\longrightarrow H^{0}\bigl(X_{s},\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}(s)}\mathcal{V}_{s}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X_{s}}}\bigl(\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log H)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X_{s}}(NH_{s})\bigr)\bigr).

There exists the following exact sequence:

\displaystyle 0

\displaystyle\longrightarrow T_{X_{s}}^{\ast}X\otimes\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}(s)}\mathcal{V}_{s}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X_{s}}(NH_{s})\longrightarrow\bigl(\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log H)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}_{X_{s}}(NH_{s})\bigr)\otimes\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}(s)}\mathcal{V}_{s}

\displaystyle\longrightarrow\Omega^{1}_{X_{s}}(\log H_{s})\otimes\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}(s)}\mathcal{V}_{s}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X_{s}}(NH_{s})\longrightarrow 0.

According to Corollary
3.14
,
if
m_{0}
is sufficiently large,
there exists a non-empty Zariski open subset

U_{2}\subset U_{1}
such that the following holds
for any
s\in U_{2}
:

\displaystyle H^{0}\bigl(X_{s},T_{X_{s}}^{\ast}X\otimes\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}(s)}\mathcal{V}_{s}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X_{s}}(NH_{s})\bigr)=0.

Hence, the natural morphism

\displaystyle H^{0}\bigl(X_{s},(\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log H)\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{O}_{X_{s}}(NH_{s}))\otimes\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}(s)}\mathcal{V}_{s}\bigr)

\displaystyle\qquad\longrightarrow H^{0}\bigl(X_{s},\Omega^{1}_{X_{s}}(\log H_{s})\otimes\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}(s)}\mathcal{V}_{s}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X_{s}}(NH_{s})\bigr)

is injective for any
s\in U_{2}
. We obtain the injectivity of
the following natural morphism
for any
s\in U_{2}
:

\displaystyle H^{0}\bigl(X,\Omega^{1}_{X}(\log H)\otimes\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(NH)\bigr)\longrightarrow H^{0}\bigl(X_{s},\Omega^{1}_{X_{s}}(\log H_{s})\otimes\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}(s)}\mathcal{V}_{s}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X_{s}}(NH_{s})\bigr).

Then, we obtain the claim of the proposition.
∎

3.4.6
Morphisms of reflexive filtered
\boldsymbol{\lambda}
-flat sheaves

Let
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{i}

(i=1,2)

be reflexive filtered sheaves
with meromorphic flat
\lambda
-connection
\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{i}

on
(X,H)
.
Let
\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits\bigl(\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{1},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{1}\big),\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{2},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{2}\big)\bigr)

denote the vector space of
morphisms of filtered
\lambda
-flat sheaves

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{1},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{1}\big)\longrightarrow\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{2},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{2}\big)
.

Proposition 3.16
.

There exists a positive integer
m_{0}>0

such that the following claim holds
for any
{\boldsymbol{m}}=(m_{1},\ldots,m_{n-1})
with
m_{i}\geq m_{0}

and for a non-empty Zariski open subset
U\subset S_{{\boldsymbol{m}}}
.

•

For any
s\in U
,
let
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{i,s},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{i,s}\big)

denote the induced filtered
\lambda
-flat bundles
on
(X_{s},H_{s})
.
Then, the natural morphism

\displaystyle\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits\bigl(\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{1},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{1}\big),\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{2},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{2}\big)\bigr)\longrightarrow\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits\bigl(\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{1,s},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{1,s}\big),\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{2,s},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{2,s}\big)\bigr)

is an isomorphism.

Proof.

For any
{\boldsymbol{a}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\Lambda}
,
let
\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}{\mathcal{H}{\rm om}}(\mathcal{V}_{1},\mathcal{V}_{2})

denote the subsheaf of
the
\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H)
-module

{\mathcal{H}{\rm om}}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H)}(\mathcal{V}_{1},\mathcal{V}_{2})

determined as follows
for any open subset
U\subset X
:

\displaystyle H^{0}\bigl(U,\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}({\mathcal{H}{\rm om}}(\mathcal{V}_{1},\mathcal{V}_{2}))\bigr)\!=\bigl\{f\!\in\!H^{0}(U,{\mathcal{H}{\rm om}}(\mathcal{V}_{1},\mathcal{V}_{2}))\mid f\big(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{b}}}\mathcal{V}_{1|U}\big)\!\subset\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}+{\boldsymbol{b}}}\big(\mathcal{V}_{2|U}\big)\,\forall{\boldsymbol{b}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\Lambda}\bigr\}.

It is easy to see that

\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}{\mathcal{H}{\rm om}}(\mathcal{V}_{1},\mathcal{V}_{2})
are reflexive

\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules.
Thus, we obtain a reflexive filtered sheaf

\mathcal{P}_{\ast}{\mathcal{H}{\rm om}}(\mathcal{V}_{1},\mathcal{V}_{2})

with the induced flat
\lambda
-connection
\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}
. We can easily observe that

\displaystyle\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits\bigl(\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{1},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{1}\big),\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{2},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{2}\big)\bigr)=\mathbb{H}^{0}\bigl(X,\mathcal{C}^{\bullet}_{N}\bigl(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{0}}}{\mathcal{H}{\rm om}}(\mathcal{V}_{1},\mathcal{V}_{2}),\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}\bigr)\bigr)

for any large
N
,
where
{\boldsymbol{0}}=(0,\ldots,0)\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\Lambda}
.
Then, the claim follows from Proposition
3.15
.
∎

3.5
Good filtered
\boldsymbol{\lambda}
-flat bundles and ramified coverings

3.5.1
Pull back

Let
X
be any complex manifold with a simple normal crossing
hypersurface
H
.
Let
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
be a good
filtered
\lambda
-flat bundle. We set
e:=\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(\mathcal{V})!
.

For any point
P\in X
,
let
(X_{P},z_{1},\ldots,z_{n})
denote
an admissible holomorphic coordinate neighbourhood around
P
. We set
H_{P}:=X_{P}\cap H

and
H_{P,i}:=H_{P}\cap\{z_{i}=0\}
. We set
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{P},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{P}\big):=\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)_{|X_{P}}
.

By using the coordinate system,
we may regard
X_{P}

as an open subset of
{\mathbb{C}}^{n}
.
Let
\varphi_{P}
:
{\mathbb{C}}^{n}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{n}

be given by

\varphi_{P}(\zeta_{1},\ldots,\zeta_{n})=\big(\zeta_{1}^{e},\ldots,\zeta_{\ell(P)}^{e},\zeta_{\ell(P)+1},\ldots,\zeta_{n}\big)
. We set

\widetilde{X}_{P}:=\varphi_{P}^{-1}(X_{P})
,

\widetilde{H}_{P}:=\varphi_{P}^{-1}(H_{P})

and

\widetilde{H}_{P,i}:=\varphi_{P}^{-1}(H_{P,i})
. We set

G_{P}:=\prod_{i=1}^{\ell(P)}\{\alpha_{i}\in{\mathbb{C}}\mid\alpha_{i}^{e}=1\}
.
It is identified with the Galois group of the ramified covering

\varphi_{P}

by the action as in Section
2.3.2
.

We obtain the
G_{P}
-equivariant
good filtered
\lambda
-flat bundle

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{P},\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}_{P}\big):=\varphi_{P}^{\ast}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{P},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{P}\big)

on
\big(\widetilde{X}_{P},\widetilde{H}_{P}\big)
.

Lemma 3.17
.

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{P},\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}_{P}\big)

is unramifiedly good.

Proof.

See
[
51
, Lemma 2.2.7]
.
∎

3.5.2
The associated graded bundles

We obtain
the
G_{P}
-equivariant filtered bundles

{}^{1}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{c}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{P}\big)

(c\in{\mathbb{R}})

on
\big(\widetilde{H}_{P,1},\partial\widetilde{H}_{P,1}\big)
.
There exists the
G_{P}
-equivariant decomposition

\displaystyle{}^{1}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{c}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{P}\big)=\bigoplus_{\ell=0}^{e-1}\mathbb{G}_{\ell}{}^{1}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{c}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{P}\big),

where
(\alpha,1,\ldots,1)

acts on
\mathbb{G}_{\ell}{}^{1}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{c}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{P}\big)

as the multiplication by
\alpha^{\ell}
.

Lemma 3.18
.

The pull back naturally induces the isomorphism

(\varphi_{|\widetilde{H}_{P,1}})^{\ast}\bigl({}^{1}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{c/e}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{P})\bigr)\simeq\mathbb{G}_{0}{}^{1}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{c}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{P}\big)
.
As a result,

{}^{1}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{c/e}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{P})

is the descent of

\mathbb{G}_{0}{}^{1}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{c}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{P}\big)
.
More generally,
the pull back and the multiplication by
\zeta_{1}^{\ell}

induces an isomorphism

(\varphi_{|\widetilde{H}_{P,1}})^{\ast}\bigl({}^{1}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{(c+\ell)/e}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{P})\bigr)\simeq\mathbb{G}_{\ell}{}^{1}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{c}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{P}\big)
.

Clearly, there exist a similar decomposition

{}^{i}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{c}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{P}\big)=\bigoplus_{\ell=0}^{e-1}\mathbb{G}_{\ell}{}^{i}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{c}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{P}\big)

and isomorphisms for any
i=1,\ldots,\ell(P)
.

3.5.3
Residues

Let us recall that we obtain the endomorphisms

\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{j}\big(\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

(j\in\Lambda)

on
{}^{j}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits_{c}^{F}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})

by using Lemma
3.18
.
(See
[
51
, Section 2.5.2]

for more detailed explanations.)

Let
P
be any point of
H
.
First, let us construct the residues

\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{1}\big(\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{P}\big)

on

{}^{1}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{P})
.
At any
Q\in\widetilde{H}_{P,1}
,
we obtain the formal decomposition

\big(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{P},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{P}\big)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{X}_{P},\widehat{Q}}=\bigoplus\big(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathfrak{a}},\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}_{\mathfrak{a}}\big)

as in (
2.4
).
For
a_{1}-1<c\leq a_{1}
,
we obtain the endomorphisms

\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{1}\big(\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}_{P}^{\lambda}\big)_{Q}

of
{}^{1}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{c}\big(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}\big)_{|Q}

as the residue of

\bigoplus\bigl(\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}_{\mathfrak{a}}-{\rm d}\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}\mathop{\rm id}\nolimits_{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathfrak{a}}}\bigr)
at
Q
.
According to

[
51
, Lemma 2.5.2]
,
by varying
Q\in\widetilde{H}_{P,1}
,
we obtain the endomorphism

\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{1}\big(\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}_{P}^{\lambda}\big)

of the filtered bundle

{}^{1}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{c}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{P}\big)
.
It is
G_{P}
-equivariant.
Hence, we obtain

\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{1}\big(\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{P}\big)

on
{}^{1}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{c}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{P})

as the descent of

\frac{1}{e}\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{1}(\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}_{P})

on

\mathbb{G}_{0}{}^{1}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{ec}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{P}\big)
.
The factor
\frac{1}{e}

comes from the relation

{e}\,{\rm d}\zeta_{1}/\zeta_{1}={\rm d}z_{1}/z_{1}
.
Similarly, we obtain

\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{i}\big(\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{P}\big)

{}^{i}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{c}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{P})

for
i=1,\ldots,\ell(P)
.

It is easy to see that
there exists a globally defined endomorphism

\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{j}\big(\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

on
{}^{j}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{c}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})

which is equal to the endomorphisms
constructed locally around
P\in H_{j}
as above.

3.5.4
Parabolic weights

We introduce some notation. We set

{\mathcal{P}{\rm ar}}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},j):=\bigl\{b\in{\mathbb{R}}\mid{}^{j}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{b}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})\neq 0\bigr\}

for
j\in\Lambda
.
Because we shall often use the pull back by a ramified covering
as in Section
3.5.1
,
for a fixed
e
,
it is convenient to consider

\displaystyle\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}{\rm ar}}}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},j):=\bigl\{c+m/e\mid c\in{\mathcal{P}{\rm ar}}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},j),\,m\in{\mathbb{Z}}\bigr\}.

Note that

{\mathcal{P}{\rm ar}}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{P},j\big)=\bigl\{eb\mid b\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}{\rm ar}}}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{P},j)\bigr\}

for
P\in H
.
(See Lemma
3.18
.)
We define

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathop{\rm gap}\nolimits}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},j):=\min\bigl\{|b_{1}-b_{2}|\mid b_{1},b_{2}\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}{\rm ar}}}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},j),\,b_{1}\neq b_{2}\bigr\}.

If
\Lambda
is finite, we also set

\widetilde{\mathop{\rm gap}\nolimits}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}):=\min\limits_{j\in\Lambda}\widetilde{\mathop{\rm gap}\nolimits}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},j)
.

For each
{\boldsymbol{a}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\Lambda}
, we set

\displaystyle{\mathcal{P}{\rm ar}}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},{\boldsymbol{a}},i):={\mathcal{P}{\rm ar}}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},i)\cap]a_{i}-1,a_{i}],\qquad\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}{\rm ar}}}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},{\boldsymbol{a}},i):=\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}{\rm ar}}}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},i)\cap]a_{i}-1,a_{i}].

We remark the following obvious lemma.

Lemma 3.19
.

For each
j
,
there exists
a_{j}\in{\mathbb{R}}

such that

|a_{j}-b|>(4e\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits\mathcal{V})^{-1}

for any
b\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}{\rm ar}}}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},j)
.

3.6
Approximation by model filtered
\boldsymbol{\lambda}
-flat bundles

3.6.1
Model filtered
\boldsymbol{\lambda}
-flat bundles

Let
Z
be a complex manifold.
Let
Y
be a neighbourhood of

\{0\}\times Z
in
{\mathbb{C}}\times Z
. We set
H_{Y}:=\{0\}\times Z
.
Let
e
be a positive integer.
Let
\zeta
be the standard complex coordinate of
{\mathbb{C}}
.
Consider

\varphi\colon{\mathbb{C}}\times Z\longrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}\times Z

induced by
\zeta\longmapsto\zeta^{e}
. We set

\widetilde{Y}:=\varphi^{-1}(Y)

and
\widetilde{H}:=\varphi^{-1}(H)
.
The induced morphism
\widetilde{Y}\longrightarrow Y

is also denoted by
\varphi
.
Let
G
denote the group of the
e
-th roots of
1
,
which is naturally identified with the Galois group of
the ramified covering
\varphi
.

Let
\mathcal{I}
be a finite subset
of
H^{0}\big(\widetilde{Y},\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Y}}\big({\ast}\widetilde{H}\big)\big)

which is preserved by the
G
-action.
Let
S_{1}
and
S_{2}
be finite subsets of

]{-}1,0]

and
{\mathbb{C}}
, respectively.
Let
V_{\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha}

((\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha)\in\mathcal{I}\times S_{1}\times S_{2})

be finite dimensional
{\mathbb{C}}
-vector spaces
equipped with a nilpotent endomorphism

f_{\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha}
.
Note that

V_{\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha}
may be
0
. We suppose that

\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha}V_{\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha}

is a
G
-representation
such that

(i)
it is
G
-equivariant as a vector bundle
over
\mathcal{I}
,

(ii)

\bigoplus f_{\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha}
commutes with
the
G
-action.

We set

\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha}:=\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Y}}\big({\ast}\widetilde{H}\big)\otimes V_{\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha}
. We define the filtered bundle

\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha}

over
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha}

by setting

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{b}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha}:=\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Y}}\big([b-a]\widetilde{H}\big)\otimes V_{\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha}

for any
b\in{\mathbb{R}}
,
where
[b-a]:=\max\{n\in{\mathbb{Z}}\mid n\leq b-a\}
. We define
the flat
\lambda
-connection
\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}_{\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha}

on
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha}

by setting

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}_{\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha}(v)={\rm d}\mathfrak{a}\cdot v+(\alpha v+f_{\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha}(v))\,{\rm d}\zeta/\zeta

for any
v\in V_{\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha}
,
which we regard as a section of
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha}

in a natural way.
Thus, we obtain a
G
-equivariant
filtered
\lambda
-flat bundle

\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha},\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}_{\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha}\big)
,
called a model filtered
\lambda
-flat bundle.
If
\mathcal{I}
induces a good set of irregular values
in
\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Y}}\big({\ast}\widetilde{H}\big)_{Q}/\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Y},Q}

at each
Q\in\widetilde{H}
,
then
\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha},\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}_{\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha}\big)

is an unramifiedly good filtered
\lambda
-flat bundle.
It induces a filtered
\lambda
-flat bundle
on
(Y,H)
as the descent,
which is also called a model filtered
\lambda
-flat bundle.

3.6.2
Approximation of good filtered
\boldsymbol{\lambda}
-flat bundles

Let
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

be any good filtered
\lambda
-flat bundle
on
(Y,H)
.
Assume the following condition:

Condition 3.20
.

For each
a\in{\mathcal{P}{\rm ar}}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})
,
the conjugacy classes of

\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits\big(\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

on

\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{a}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})_{|P}

are independent of
P\in H
.
Note that this condition is trivially satisfied
if
\lambda\neq 0
.

We set
e:=\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(\mathcal{V})!
.
Let
\varphi
and
\big(\widetilde{Y},\widetilde{H}\big)

be as in Section
3.6.1
. We set

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}},\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}\big):=\varphi^{\ast}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
.
For each
Q\in\widetilde{H}
,
there exists a decomposition

\displaystyle\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}},\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}\big)\otimes\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Y},\widehat{Q}}=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}(Q)}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathfrak{a}},\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}_{\mathfrak{a}}\big)

as in (
2.5
). We obtain the vector spaces

\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{a}\big(\mathcal{P}_{0}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathfrak{a}}\big)_{|Q}

(-1<a\leq 0)

equipped with the endomorphisms

\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits\big(\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}\big)
.
Condition
3.20

is equivalent to the following.

•

The conjugacy classes of

\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits\big(\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}\big)

on

\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{a}\big(\mathcal{P}_{0}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathfrak{a}}\big)_{|Q}

are independent of
Q\in\widetilde{H}

for any
-1<a\leq 0
.

In particular,
the condition implies that there exists a decomposition

\displaystyle\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{a}\big(\mathcal{P}_{0}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathfrak{a}}\big)=\bigoplus_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{C}}}\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{a}\big(\mathcal{P}_{0}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathfrak{a}}\big)

on
\widetilde{H}
,
where
\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits\big(\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}\big)-\alpha\mathop{\rm id}\nolimits
are nilpotent
on
\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{a}\big(\mathcal{P}_{0}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathfrak{a}}\big)
.

Fix
P\in H
.
Let
\widetilde{P}\in\widetilde{H}

be determined by
\varphi\big(\widetilde{P}\big)=P
. We set

V_{\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha}:=\mathbb{E}_{\alpha}\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{a}\big(\mathcal{P}_{0}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathfrak{a}}\big)_{|\widetilde{P}}
.
Let
f_{\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha}

be the nilpotent part of

\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits\big(\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}\big)

on

V_{\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha}
.
For a neighbourhood

Y_{P}
of
P
in
Y
,
we set

H_{P}:=Y_{P}\cap H
,

\widetilde{Y}_{P}:=\varphi^{-1}(Y_{P})

and

\widetilde{H}_{P}:=\varphi^{-1}(H_{P})
. We may assume that
any
\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}\big(\widetilde{P}\big)

has a lift
\widetilde{\mathfrak{a}}

in
H^{0}\big(\widetilde{Y}_{P},\mathcal{O}_{\widetilde{Y}_{P}}\big({\ast}\widetilde{H}_{P}\big)\big)
.
From the set

\{(\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha)\}\subset\mathcal{I}\big(\widetilde{P}\big)\times]{-}1,0]\times{\mathbb{C}}

and
the tuples
(V_{\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha},f_{\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha})
,
we obtain a model filtered
\lambda
-flat bundle

\displaystyle\bigl(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{0},\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}_{0}^{\lambda}\bigr):=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha}\bigl(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha},\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}_{\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha}\bigl)

on
\big(\widetilde{Y}_{P},\widetilde{H}_{P}\big)
.
It is unramifiedly good and naturally
G
-equivariant.
As the descent,
we obtain a good filtered
\lambda
-flat bundle

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{0},\mathbb{D}_{0}^{\lambda}\big)

on
(Y_{P},H_{P})
.

Lemma 3.21

(assume Condition
3.20
)
.

For any positive integer
m
,
there exist a neighbourhood
Y_{P}

of
P
in
Y

and an isomorphism of filtered bundles

\Phi_{m}\colon\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{0}\simeq\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{|Y_{P}}

such that the following holds:

•

We set
\widetilde{\Phi}_{m}:=\varphi^{\ast}(\Phi_{m})

and

A:=\big(\widetilde{\Phi}_{m}\big)^{\ast}\big(\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}\big)-\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}_{0}^{\lambda}

on
\widetilde{Y}_{P}
.
Let

A=\sum A_{(\mathfrak{b},b,\beta),(\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha)}

be the decomposition such that

\displaystyle A_{(\mathfrak{b},b,\beta),(\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha)}\in\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits\bigl(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha},\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathfrak{b},b,\beta}\bigr)\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\widetilde{Y}_{P}}.

Then,
we obtain the following for any
c\in{\mathbb{R}}
:

\displaystyle A_{(\mathfrak{b},b,\beta),(\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha)}\cdot\mathcal{P}_{c}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha}\subset\mathcal{P}_{c-10m}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathfrak{b},b,\beta}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\widetilde{Y}_{P}}\qquad(\mathfrak{a}\neq\mathfrak{b}),

\displaystyle A_{(\mathfrak{a},b,\beta),(\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha)}\cdot\mathcal{P}_{c}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha}\subset\mathcal{P}_{c}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathfrak{a},b,\beta}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\widetilde{Y}_{P}}\big(\log\widetilde{H}_{P}\big),\qquad(a,\alpha)\neq(b,\beta),

\displaystyle\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits A_{(\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha),(\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha)}\bigl(\mathcal{P}_{c}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha}\bigr)\subset\mathcal{P}_{<c}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha}.

Here,

\mathcal{P}_{<c}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathfrak{b},b,\beta}=\bigcup_{d<c}\mathcal{P}_{d}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathfrak{b},b,\beta}
.

Proof.

By
[
51
, Proposition 2.4.4]
,
for any large inter
N
,
there exists a
G
-equivariant decomposition of
filtered bundles

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{|\widetilde{Y}_{P}}=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}(\widetilde{P})}\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(N)}_{\mathfrak{a}}

on
\big(\widetilde{Y}_{P},\widetilde{H}_{P}\big)

such that the following holds.

•

Let
\pi^{(N)}_{\mathfrak{a}}
denote the projection of

\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{|\widetilde{Y}_{P}}

onto
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(N)}_{\mathfrak{a}}
,
and let
\iota^{(N)}_{\mathfrak{a}}
denote the inclusion of

\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(N)}_{\mathfrak{a}}

into
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{|\widetilde{Y}_{P}}
.
Then, for any
\mathfrak{a}\neq\mathfrak{b}
and
for any
c\in{\mathbb{R}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\pi^{(N)}_{\mathfrak{b}}\circ\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}\circ\iota^{(N)}_{\mathfrak{a}}\bigl(\mathcal{P}_{c}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(N)}_{\mathfrak{a}}\bigr)\subset\mathcal{P}_{c-2N}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(N)}_{\mathfrak{b}}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\widetilde{Y}_{P}}.

•

For
\mathfrak{a}
, we set

\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda(N)}_{\mathfrak{a}}:=\pi^{(N)}_{\mathfrak{a}}\circ\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}\circ\iota^{(N)}_{\mathfrak{a}}-{\rm d}\mathfrak{a}\mathop{\rm id}\nolimits_{\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(N)}_{\mathfrak{a}}}
.
Then, for any
c\in{\mathbb{R}}
, we obtain

\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda\,(N)}\bigl(\mathcal{P}_{c}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(N)}_{\mathfrak{a}}\bigr)\subset\mathcal{P}_{c}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(N)}_{\mathfrak{a}}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{\widetilde{Y}_{P}}\big(\log\widetilde{H}_{P}\big),
