\displaystyle\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda\,(N)}\circ\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda\,(N)}\bigl(\mathcal{P}_{c}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(N)}_{\mathfrak{a}}\bigr)\subset\mathcal{P}_{c-N}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{(N)}_{\mathfrak{a}}\otimes\Omega^{2}_{\widetilde{Y}_{P}}\big(\log\widetilde{H}_{P}\big).

Then, the claim of the lemma is clear.
∎

3.7
Perturbation of good filtered
\boldsymbol{\lambda}
-flat bundles

3.7.1
Curve case

Let
C
be a Riemann surface
with a finite subset
D\subset C
.
Let
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

be a good filtered
\lambda
-flat bundle on
(C,D)
. We set
e:=\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(\mathcal{V})!
. We choose

\eta>0
such that

10e\eta<\widetilde{\mathop{\rm gap}\nolimits}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})
.

For any
0<10\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(\mathcal{V})\epsilon\leq\eta
,
and for any
P\in D
,
let
\psi_{\epsilon,P}
be a map

\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}{\rm ar}}}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},P)\longrightarrow{\mathbb{R}}

such that

(i)

|\psi_{\epsilon,P}(b)-b|<2\epsilon
,

(ii)
if
e(b_{1}-b_{2})\in{\mathbb{Z}}

then
\psi_{\epsilon,P}(b_{1})-b_{1}=\psi_{\epsilon,P}(b_{2})-b_{2}
. We define

\varphi_{\epsilon,P}\colon{\mathbb{Z}}\times\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}{\rm ar}}}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},P)\longrightarrow{\mathbb{R}}

by

\displaystyle\varphi_{\epsilon,P}(k,b):=\psi_{\epsilon,P}(b)+\epsilon k.

We take
{\boldsymbol{a}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{D}

as in Lemma
3.19
.
For each
P\in D
and

b\in{\mathcal{P}{\rm ar}}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},{\boldsymbol{a}},P)
,
we obtain the endomorphism

\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{P}\big(\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

of
\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{b}\big(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V}_{|P}\big)
.
Let
W_{\bullet}\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{b}\big(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V}_{|P}\big)

denote the weight filtration
associated with
the nilpotent part of
\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{P}(\mathbb{D}^{\lambda})
.
For any
(k,b)\in{\mathbb{Z}}\times{\mathcal{P}{\rm ar}}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},{\boldsymbol{a}},P)
,
we obtain the subspace

W_{k}\bigl(F_{b}\big(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V}_{|P}\big)\bigr)

as the pull back of

W_{k}\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{b}\big(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V}_{|P}\big)

by the projection

F_{b}\big(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V}_{|P}\big)\longrightarrow\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{b}\big(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V}_{|P}\big)
. We define the filtration
F^{(\epsilon)}

on
\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V}_{|P}

indexed by
]a(P)-1,a(P)]

as follows:

\displaystyle F^{(\epsilon)}_{c}\bigl(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V}_{|P}\bigr):=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(k,b)\in{\mathbb{Z}}\times{\mathcal{P}{\rm ar}}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},{\boldsymbol{a}},P)\\
\varphi_{\epsilon,P}(k,b)\leq c\end{subarray}}W_{k}F_{b}\big(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V}_{|P}\big).

We obtain the corresponding filtered bundle

\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}
.
Note the following lemma.

Lemma 3.22
.

\big(\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
is
a good filtered
\lambda
-flat bundle.

Proof.

It is enough to consider the case
where
C
is a neighbourhood of
D=\{P\}=\{0\}
in
{\mathbb{C}}
. We obtain

\varphi\colon\widetilde{C}\longrightarrow C
,

\widetilde{D}
,

G
,
and
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}},\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}\big)

as in Section
3.5.1
. We set
\widetilde{a}:=ea(P)

and
\widetilde{P}:=\varphi^{-1}(P)
.
For
(k,b)\in{\mathbb{Z}}\times{\mathcal{P}{\rm ar}}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}},\widetilde{a})
,
we construct

W_{k}F_{b}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\widetilde{a}}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{|\widetilde{P}}\big)

as above.

For
b\in{\mathcal{P}{\rm ar}}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}},\widetilde{a}\big)
,
note that

b/e\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}{\rm ar}}}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})
,
and we set

\displaystyle\widetilde{\varphi}_{\epsilon,P}(k,b):=e\psi_{\epsilon,P}(k,b/e)+ke\epsilon.

We set

\displaystyle F^{(\epsilon)}_{d}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\widetilde{a}}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{|\widetilde{P}}\big):=\sum_{\widetilde{\varphi}_{\epsilon,P}(k,b)\leq d}W_{k}F_{b}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\widetilde{a}}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{|\widetilde{P}}\big).

We obtain the corresponding

G
-equivariant filtered bundle

\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}
. We can easily observe that

\varphi^{\ast}\mathcal{P}_{\ast}^{(\epsilon)}\mathcal{V}\simeq\mathcal{P}_{\ast}^{(\epsilon)}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}

by using

10^{2}e\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(\mathcal{V})\epsilon<\widetilde{\mathop{\rm gap}\nolimits}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})
.

There exists the decomposition

\displaystyle\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}},\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}\big)\otimes{\mathbb{C}}[\![\zeta]\!]=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}}\bigl(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathfrak{a}},\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}_{\mathfrak{a}}\bigr),

as in (
2.4
). We apply the same procedure to
each

\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathfrak{a}}

by using
\widetilde{\varphi}_{\epsilon,P}
,
and we obtain filtered bundles

\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathfrak{a}}

for which
\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}_{\mathfrak{a}}-{\rm d}\mathfrak{a}\mathop{\rm id}\nolimits

are logarithmic.
Because

\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}[\![\zeta]\!]=\bigoplus\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{\mathfrak{a}}
,
we obtain that

\big(\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}},\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}\big)

is unramifiedly good.
Hence, we obtain that

\big(\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

is good.
∎

Suppose that
C
is compact and connected. We clearly obtain

\lim\limits_{\epsilon\to 0}c_{1}\big(\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}\big)=c_{1}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})
.
The following is also standard.

Lemma 3.23
.

Suppose that
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
is stable.
Then, if
\epsilon>0
is sufficiently small,

\big(\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

is also stable.

Proof.

For any positive integer
s\leq\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(\mathcal{V})
,
and for any
P\in D
,
let
\mathcal{T}(s,P)
be the set of real numbers
expressed as

\displaystyle\sum_{b\in{\mathcal{P}{\rm ar}}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},{\boldsymbol{a}},P)}b\,s_{b},

where
s_{b}
are non-negative integers such that

\sum s_{b}=s
.
Let
\mathcal{S}(s)
denote the set of
real numbers expressed as

\frac{1}{s}\bigl(m+\sum_{P\in D}c_{P}\bigr)
,
where
m\in{\mathbb{Z}}
and
c_{P}\in\mathcal{T}(s,P)
.
Then,

\bigcup_{0<s\leq\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits\mathcal{V}}\mathcal{S}(s)

is discrete in
{\mathbb{R}}
.
Hence, there exists
\delta>0

such that
if
t\in\bigcup_{0<s\leq\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits\mathcal{V}}\mathcal{S}(s)

satisfies
t<\mu(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})
,
we obtain

t<\mu(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})-\delta
.
Then, the claim of the lemma is clear.
∎

3.7.2
Surface case

Let
X
be a complex projective surface
with a simple normal crossing hypersurface

H=\bigcup_{i\in\Lambda}H_{i}
.
Let
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
be
a good filtered
\lambda
-flat bundle
on
(X,H)
. We shall explain a similar perturbation of
good filtered
\lambda
-flat bundles.
Set
e:=\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(\mathcal{V})!
. We choose
\eta>0
such that

0<10e\eta<\widetilde{\mathop{\rm gap}\nolimits}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})
.

For any
0<10\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(\mathcal{V})\epsilon\leq\eta
,
let
\psi_{\epsilon,i}
be a map

\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}{\rm ar}}}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},i)\longrightarrow{\mathbb{R}}

such that

(i)

|\psi_{\epsilon,i}(b)-b|\allowbreak<2\epsilon
,

(ii)
if
e(b_{1}-b_{2})\in{\mathbb{Z}}

then
\psi_{\epsilon,i}(b_{1})-b_{1}=\psi_{\epsilon,i}(b_{2})-b_{2}
. We define

\varphi_{\epsilon,i}\colon{\mathbb{Z}}\times{\mathcal{P}{\rm ar}}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},i)\longrightarrow{\mathbb{R}}

by

\varphi_{\epsilon,i}(k,b):=\psi_{\epsilon,i}(b)+\epsilon k
.

We take

{\boldsymbol{a}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\Lambda}

for
\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}{\rm ar}}}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},i)

(i\in\Lambda)

as in Lemma
3.19
.
The eigenvalues of
the endomorphism
\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{i}\big(\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

on
\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{b}\big(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V}_{|H_{i}}\big)

are constant on
H_{i}

because
H_{i}
are compact. We obtain the well defined
nilpotent part
N_{i,b}
of

\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{i}\big(\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
.
There exists a finite subset
Z_{i}\subset H_{i}

such that the conjugacy classes
of the nilpotent part of
N_{i,b|Q}

(Q\in H_{i}\setminus Z_{i})
are constant. We obtain the weight filtration
W

of
\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{b}\big(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V}_{|H_{i}\setminus Z_{i}}\big)

by algebraic vector subbundles
whose restriction to
Q\in H_{i}\setminus Z_{i}

are the weight filtration of

N_{i,b|Q}
.
It uniquely extends to a filtration of

\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{b}\big(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V}_{|H_{i}}\big)

by algebraic subbundles,
which is also denoted by
W
.

For any
(k,b)\in{\mathbb{Z}}\times{\mathcal{P}{\rm ar}}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},{\boldsymbol{a}},i)
,
let

W_{k}F_{b}\big(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V}_{|H_{i}}\big)

denote the subbundle
of
\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V}_{|H_{i}}

obtained as the pull back of

W_{k}\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{b}\big(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V}_{|H_{i}}\big)

by the projection

F_{b}\big(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V}_{|H_{i}}\big)\longrightarrow\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{b}\big(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V}_{|H_{i}}\big)
. We define the filtration
F^{(\epsilon)}

on
\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V}_{|H_{i}}

indexed by
]a_{i}-1,a_{i}]

as follows:

\displaystyle F^{(\epsilon)}_{c}\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V}_{|H_{i}}:=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}(k,b)\in{\mathbb{Z}}\times{\mathcal{P}{\rm ar}}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},{\boldsymbol{a}},i)\\
\varphi_{\epsilon,i}(k,b)\leq c\end{subarray}}W_{k}F_{b}\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V}_{|H_{i}}.

We obtain the corresponding filtered bundle

\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}
over
\mathcal{V}
.
As in the curve case (see Lemma
3.22
),
we obtain the following.

Lemma 3.24
.

\big(\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

is a good filtered
\lambda
-flat bundle.

We clearly have

\lim\limits_{\epsilon\to 0}c_{1}\big(\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}\big)=c_{1}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})

and

\lim\limits_{\epsilon\to 0}\mathop{\rm ch}\nolimits_{2}\big(\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}\big)=\mathop{\rm ch}\nolimits_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})
.
The following is standard,
and similar to Lemma
3.23
.
(See also
[
46
, Proposition 3.28]
.)

Lemma 3.25
.

Let
L
be an ample line bundle on
X
.
Suppose that
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
is

\mu_{L}
-stable.
Then, if
\epsilon
is sufficiently small,

\big(\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

is also
\mu_{L}
-stable.

3.8
Some families of auxiliary metrics
on a punctured disc

3.8.1
Regular model case

Let
V
be a finite dimensional vector space over
{\mathbb{C}}

with a nilpotent endomorphism
f
.
Let
(a,\alpha)\in{\mathbb{R}}\times{\mathbb{C}}
.
Let
X
be a neighbourhood of
0
in
{\mathbb{C}}
. We set
H:=\{0\}
. We set
\mathcal{V}=\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H)\otimes V
.
From
(a,\alpha)
and
(V,f)
,
we obtain a model filtered
\lambda
-flat bundle

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

by applying the construction
in Section
3.6.1

in the case
\mathfrak{a}=0
and
e=1
.

Fix
0<\eta<1
.
For any
0\leq 10\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(\mathcal{V})\epsilon\leq\eta
,
we take
a(\epsilon)

such that
|a-a(\epsilon)|<2\epsilon
,
and we obtain the regular filtered
\lambda
-flat bundle

\big(\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

as in Section
3.7.1
. We set
E:=\mathcal{V}_{|X\setminus H}
. We consider the Kähler metric
g_{\epsilon}:=\big(\eta^{2}|z|^{2\eta-2}+\epsilon^{2}|z|^{2\epsilon-2}\big)\,{\rm d}z\,{\rm d}\overline{z}

of
X\setminus H
.

Proposition 3.26
.

There exist Hermitian metrics
h^{(\epsilon)}

of
E

for
0\leq 10\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(\mathcal{V})\epsilon\leq\eta

such that the following holds:

•

\mathcal{P}^{h^{(\epsilon)}}_{\ast}E=\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}
.

•

h^{(0)}
is a Hermitian metric of
\big(E,\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
,
and
h^{(\epsilon)}\to h^{(0)}
in the
C^{\infty}
-sense
locally on
X\setminus H
.

•

There exist
C_{i}>0

(i=0,1,2)

such that
the following conditions are satisfied for any
\epsilon
:

\displaystyle\bigl|G\big(h^{(\epsilon)}\big)\bigr|_{g_{\epsilon},h^{(\epsilon)}}\leq C_{0},\qquad C_{1}^{-1}|z|^{2\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(\mathcal{V})\epsilon}h^{(0)}\leq h^{(\epsilon)}\leq C_{1}|z|^{-2\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(\mathcal{V})\epsilon}h^{(0)},

\displaystyle C_{2}^{-1}\det(h_{0})\leq\det\big(h^{(\epsilon)}\big)\leq C_{2}\det\big(h^{(0)}\big).

•

Let
B_{i}^{(\epsilon)}

(i=1,2)

be the
C^{\infty}
-endomorphisms of
E

determined by the condition

\mathbb{D}^{\lambda\star}_{h^{(\epsilon)}}(v)=B^{(\epsilon)}_{1}(v)\,{\rm d}z/z+B^{(\epsilon)}_{2}(v)\,{\rm d}\overline{z}/\overline{z}

for
v\in V
.
Then,
there exists
C_{3}>0
such that

\big|B^{(\epsilon)}_{i}\big|_{h^{(\epsilon)}}\leq C_{3}

holds for any
\epsilon
.

Moreover,
for any
v_{1},v_{2}\in V
,

h^{(\epsilon)}(v_{1},v_{2})

depends only on
|z|
,
where
v_{j}
are naturally regarded as holomorphic sections
of
\mathcal{V}
.

In the case
\lambda\neq 0
,
such a family of Hermitian metrics
h^{(\epsilon)}

is constructed in

[
49
, Sections 4.3 and 4.4.1]
. We shall explain the case
\lambda=0

in Section
3.8.4
.

3.8.2
General case

Let
S_{0}
be a finite set.
Let
S_{1}
be a finite subset of

]0,1]
.
Let
S_{2}
be a finite subset of
{\mathbb{C}}
.
Let
V_{i,a,\alpha}

(
(i,a,\alpha)\in S_{0}\times S_{1}\times S_{2}
)
be finite dimensional
{\mathbb{C}}
-vector spaces
equipped with a nilpotent endomorphism
f_{i,a,\alpha}
.
Set
r:=\sum\dim V_{i,a,\alpha}
and
e:=r!
.
Take
\eta>0
such that

\displaystyle 10e\eta<\min\bigl\{|a-b|\mid a,b\in S_{1}\cup\{0,1\},\,a\neq b\bigr\}.

As in Section
3.8.1
,
we obtain the regular filtered
\lambda
-flat bundles

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{i,a,\alpha},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{i,a,\alpha}\big)

from
(V_{i,a,\alpha},f_{i,a,\alpha})
.
For
0\leq\epsilon<\eta/10r
,
we take
a(\epsilon)
such that

|a-a(\epsilon)|<2\epsilon
,
and we obtain

\big(\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{i,a,\alpha},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{i,a,\alpha}\big)
. We set

E_{i,a,\alpha}:=\mathcal{V}_{i,a,\alpha|X\setminus H}
. We obtain the metrics

h^{(\epsilon)}_{i,a,\alpha}
of
E_{i,a,\alpha}

as in Proposition
3.26
. We set

\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}:=\bigoplus\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{i,a,\alpha}

and

h^{(\epsilon)}:=\bigoplus h^{(\epsilon)}_{i,a,\alpha}
.

Fix a positive integer
m
and
a positive number
C
. We consider the following data:

•

For each
i\in S_{0}
,
let
\mathfrak{a}(i)
denote a polynomial

\sum_{j=1}^{m}\mathfrak{a}(i)_{j}z^{-j}\in z^{-1}{\mathbb{C}}\big[z^{-1}\big]

such that
|\mathfrak{a}(i)_{j}|\leq C
.

•

Let
A
be a holomorphic section of

{\mathcal{E}nd}(\mathcal{V})

with the decomposition

A=\sum A_{(j,b,\beta),(i,a,\alpha)}
,
where

\displaystyle A_{(j,b,\beta),(i,a,\alpha)}\in{\mathcal{H}{\rm om}}(\mathcal{V}_{i,a,\alpha},\mathcal{V}_{j,b,\beta})

If
i\neq j
,
we obtain
|A_{(j,b,\beta),(i,a,\alpha)}|_{h^{(0)}}\leq C|z|^{10m}
,
and if
i=j
,
we obtain
|A_{(i,b,\beta),(i,a,\alpha)}|_{h^{(0)}}\allowbreak\leq C|z|^{4\eta}
.

We define the flat
\lambda
-connection

\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}
on
\mathcal{V}

as follows:

\displaystyle\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}=\bigoplus_{i,a,\alpha}\bigl({\rm d}\mathfrak{a}(i)\mathop{\rm id}\nolimits_{\mathcal{V}_{i,a,\alpha}}+\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{i,a,\alpha}\bigr)+A\frac{{\rm d}z}{z}.

Let
g_{\epsilon}

be the Kähler metrics of
X\setminus H

as in Section
3.8.1
.

Proposition 3.27
.

There exists a constant
C^{\prime}
depending only on
m
and
C

such that

\displaystyle\big|G\big(h^{(\epsilon)}\big)\big|_{g_{\epsilon},h^{(\epsilon)}}\leq C^{\prime}.

3.8.3
A consequence

Let
Y
be a neighbourhood of
0
in
{\mathbb{C}}
. We set
H:=\{0\}
.
Let
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

be any good filtered
\lambda
-flat bundle
on
(Y,H)
.
Let
\big(E,\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
be the
\lambda
-flat bundle
obtained as the restriction of

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

to
Y\setminus H
. We set
e:=\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(\mathcal{V})!
.
Let
\varphi
be as in Section
3.6
,
and we set

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}},\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}\big):=\varphi^{\ast}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
. We take
\eta>0
such that

10e\eta<\widetilde{\mathop{\rm gap}\nolimits}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})
.
Let
g
be the Kähler metric

\eta^{2}|z|^{2\eta-2}\,{\rm d}z\,{\rm d}\overline{z}

of
Y\setminus H
. By using a special case of Proposition
3.27
,
we obtain the following corollary.

Corollary 3.28
.

There exists a Hermitian metric

h
of
\big(E,\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

such that

(i)

\mathcal{P}^{h}_{\ast}E=\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}
,

(ii)

|G(h)|_{h,g}

is bounded on
Y\setminus H
.

Proof.

Let

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{0},\mathbb{D}_{0}^{\lambda}\big)

be a model filtered
\lambda
-flat bundle
with an isomorphism

\Phi_{m}\colon\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{0}\allowbreak\simeq\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}

as in Lemma
3.21
,
where
m
is a sufficiently large integer. We recall that

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{0},\mathbb{D}_{0}^{\lambda}\big)

is obtained as the descent of
the
G
-equivariant model filtered
\lambda
-flat bundle

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{0},\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}_{0}\big)=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{\mathfrak{a},a,\alpha}\big)
,
and
\Phi_{m}
is induced by
a
G
-equivariant isomorphism

\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{0}\simeq\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}
.
Let
\widetilde{h}_{0}^{(0)}
be the Hermitian metric of

\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{0|\widetilde{Y}\setminus\widetilde{H}}

as in Proposition
3.26

with
\epsilon=0
. By the isomorphism
\widetilde{\Phi}_{m}
,
it induces a
G
-equivariant Hermitian metric

\widetilde{h}

of
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{|\widetilde{Y}\setminus\widetilde{H}}
.
Applying Proposition
3.27

to
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}},\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}\big)

with
\widetilde{h}
,
we obtain the boundedness of
G\big(\widetilde{h}\big)

with respect to

\varphi^{\ast}g

and
\widetilde{h}
.
Because
\widetilde{h}
is
G
-equivariant,
we obtain the Hermitian metric
h
of
E

which has the desired property.
∎

3.8.4
Proof of Proposition
3.26

Let
V_{2}:={\mathbb{C}}\,v_{1}\oplus{\mathbb{C}}v_{2}

with the nilpotent map
determined by
f_{2}(v_{1})=v_{2}
and
f_{2}(v_{2})=0
. We obtain

\mathcal{V}_{2}=V_{2}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H)

and
the Higgs field
\theta_{2}

determined by

\theta_{2}(v)=f_{2}(v)\,{\rm d}z/z
.
Let
\big(E_{2},\overline{\partial}_{E_{2}},\theta_{2}\big)
be the Higgs bundle
on
X\setminus H

obtained as the restriction of

(\mathcal{V}_{2},\theta_{2})
.
For any
\epsilon>0
,
we set

L_{\epsilon}(z):=\epsilon^{-1}(|z|^{-\epsilon}-|z|^{\epsilon})
. We also set

L_{0}:=-\log|z|^{2}
.

Lemma 3.29
.

We obtain

L_{0}(z)\leq L_{\epsilon}(z)\leq|z|^{-\epsilon}L_{0}(z)
.
There exists
C>0
such that

\displaystyle\bigl|\partial_{z}\log L_{\epsilon}(z)\bigr|\leq C|z|^{-1}

on
\{|z|\leq 1/2\}
for any
0\leq\epsilon\leq 1/2
.

Proof.

As proved in
[
49
, Section 4.2]
,

L_{0}(z)\leq L_{\epsilon}(z)
holds. We set

g_{1}(\epsilon):=-\epsilon\log|z|^{2}-\big(1-|z|^{2\epsilon}\big)

for any
z\in\Delta^{\ast}
and for
\epsilon>0
.
It is easy to check that

\partial_{\epsilon}g_{1}(\epsilon)\geq 0

and
\lim\limits_{\epsilon\to 0}g_{1}(\epsilon)=0
.
Hence, we obtain

L_{\epsilon}(z)\leq|z|^{-\epsilon}L_{0}(z)
.
For
0<a<1
and
0<\epsilon
, we set

g_{2}(\epsilon,a):=\frac{\epsilon}{2}(a^{-\epsilon}-a^{\epsilon})^{-1}(a^{-\epsilon}+a^{\epsilon})
.
Then,

\partial\log L_{\epsilon}(z)=-g_{2}(\epsilon,|z|)\frac{{\rm d}z}{z}
.
Then, we can check that

\partial_{a}g_{2}(\epsilon,a)\geq 0

and
\partial_{\epsilon}g_{2}(\epsilon,a)\geq 0
.
Then, we obtain the second claim of the lemma.
∎

Let
h_{2}^{(\epsilon)}

be the
C^{\infty}
-metric of
E_{2}

given by

\displaystyle h_{2}^{(\epsilon)}(v_{1},v_{1})=L_{\epsilon},\qquad h_{2}^{(\epsilon)}(v_{2},v_{2})=L_{\epsilon}^{-1},\qquad h_{2}^{(\epsilon)}(v_{1},v_{2})=0.

Lemma 3.30
.

\big(E_{2},\overline{\partial}_{E_{2}},\theta_{2},h_{2}^{(\epsilon)}\big)

are harmonic bundles.
Moreover,
the family of metrics
h^{(\epsilon)}_{2}

satisfies the condition
in Proposition
3.26

for
(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{2},\theta_{2})
.

Proof.

Let
H_{\epsilon}
be the matrix valued function
on
X\setminus D

determined by

(H_{\epsilon})_{i,j}:=h_{2}^{(\epsilon)}(v_{i},v_{j})
.
Then, the following holds:

\displaystyle\overline{\partial}\bigl(H_{\epsilon}^{-1}\partial H_{\epsilon}\bigr)

\displaystyle=\begin{pmatrix}\overline{\partial}\partial\log L_{\epsilon}&0\\
0&-\overline{\partial}\partial\log L_{\epsilon}\end{pmatrix}=\begin{pmatrix}-1&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\frac{\epsilon^{2}|z|^{-2}\,{\rm d}\overline{z}\,{\rm d}z}{\bigl(|z|^{-\epsilon}-|z|^{\epsilon}\bigr)^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\begin{pmatrix}-1&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}L_{\epsilon}^{-2}\frac{{\rm d}z\,{\rm d}\overline{z}}{|z|^{2}}.

Let
\Theta
be the matrix valued function
representing
\theta_{2}
with respect to
the frame
(v_{1},v_{2})
,
i.e.,

\theta_{2}(v_{1},v_{2})=(v_{1},v_{2})\Theta
.
Let
\theta^{\dagger}_{2,h^{(\epsilon)}}

denote the adjoint of
\theta_{2}

with respect to
h_{2}^{(\epsilon)}
.
Let
\Theta^{\dagger}_{\epsilon}

denote the matrix valued function
representing
\theta^{\dagger}_{2,h^{(\epsilon)}}
.
The following holds:

\displaystyle\Theta=\begin{pmatrix}0&0\\
1&0\end{pmatrix}\frac{{\rm d}z}{z},\qquad\Theta^{\dagger}_{\epsilon}=\begin{pmatrix}0&1\\
0&0\end{pmatrix}L_{\epsilon}^{-2}\frac{{\rm d}\overline{z}}{\overline{z}}.

Hence, we obtain

\displaystyle\bigl[\Theta,\Theta^{\dagger}_{\epsilon}\bigr]=\begin{pmatrix}-1&0\\
0&1\end{pmatrix}\cdot L_{\epsilon}^{-2}\cdot\frac{{\rm d}z\,{\rm d}\overline{z}}{|z|^{2}}.

It implies that

\displaystyle\overline{\partial}\bigl(H_{\epsilon}^{-1}\partial H_{\epsilon}\bigr)+\bigl[\Theta,\Theta^{\dagger}_{\epsilon}\bigr]=0.

It is exactly the Hitchin equation
for
\big(E_{2},\overline{\partial}_{E_{2}},\theta_{2},h_{2,\epsilon}\big)
.
The other claim is easy to see.
∎

For each
\ell\in{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}
,
we set

(V_{\ell},f_{\ell}):=\mathop{\rm Sym}\nolimits^{\ell-1}(V_{2},f_{2})
. We set

\mathcal{V}_{\ell}:=V_{\ell}\otimes\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H)

and
\theta_{\ell}:=f_{\ell}\,{\rm d}z/z
. We obtain the regular filtered Higgs bundles

\big(\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{\ell},\theta_{\ell}\big)
.
Note that

\big(\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{\ell},\theta_{\ell}\big)

is naturally isomorphic to
the
(\ell-1)
-th symmetric product
of
\big(\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{2},\theta_{2}\big)
.
Hence,
h^{(\epsilon)}_{2}

induce
harmonic metrics

h^{(\epsilon)}_{\ell}

of

\big(E_{\ell},\overline{\partial}_{E_{\ell}},\theta_{\ell}\big):=(\mathcal{V}_{\ell},\theta_{\ell})_{|X\setminus H}

satisfying the conditions in Proposition
3.26

for
\big(\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{\ell},\theta_{\ell}\big)
.

Let
(V,f)
,
(a,\alpha)

and
(\mathcal{V},\theta)

be as in Section
3.8.1
.
There exist integers
\ell_{1},\ldots,\ell_{n}

such that

\displaystyle(V,f)\simeq\bigoplus_{j=1}^{n}\big(V_{\ell_{j}},f_{\ell_{j}}\big).

We obtain

\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}\simeq\bigoplus\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{\ell_{j}}
. We obtain the harmonic metrics

\bigoplus_{j=1}^{n}|z|^{-2a(\epsilon)}h^{(\epsilon)}_{\ell_{j}}

for

\big(E,\overline{\partial}_{E},\theta\big)
. We can easily check that
they satisfy the conditions in Proposition
3.26

for
(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\theta)
.
∎

3.8.5
Proof of Proposition
3.27

We set

\Phi:=\bigoplus_{i,a,\alpha}{\rm d}\mathfrak{a}(i)\mathop{\rm id}\nolimits_{\mathcal{V}_{i,a,\alpha}}

and

\mathbb{D}^{\lambda\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits}:=\bigoplus\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{i,a,\alpha}
.
Let
\Phi^{\dagger}_{h^{(\epsilon)}}

and
A^{\dagger}_{h^{(\epsilon)}}

denote the adjoint of
\Phi
and
A

with respect to
h^{(\epsilon)}
,
respectively. We obtain the decompositions

\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}=\mathbb{D}^{\lambda\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits}+\Phi+A\,{\rm d}z/z

and

\mathbb{D}_{h^{(\epsilon)}}^{\lambda\star}=\mathbb{D}_{h^{(\epsilon)}}^{\lambda\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits\star}-\Phi_{h^{(\epsilon)}}^{\dagger}-A_{h^{(\epsilon)}}^{\dagger}\,{\rm d}\overline{z}/\overline{z}
.
Note that

\bigl[\mathbb{D}^{\lambda\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits},\Phi_{h^{(\epsilon)}}^{\dagger}\bigr]=\bigl[\mathbb{D}^{\lambda\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits\star}_{h^{(\epsilon)}},\Phi\bigr]=\bigl[\Phi,\Phi^{\dagger}_{h^{(\epsilon)}}\bigr]=0
. By the assumption,
we obtain

\displaystyle\bigl|\bigl[\Phi,A^{\dagger}_{h^{(\epsilon)}}\bigr]\bigr|_{g_{\epsilon},h^{(\epsilon)}}=\bigl|\bigl[\Phi^{\dagger}_{h^{(\epsilon)}},A\bigr]\bigr|_{g_{\epsilon},h^{(\epsilon)}}\leq 2\eta^{-2}C^{2}C_{1}^{2}|z|^{5m-4\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(\mathcal{V})\epsilon-2\eta}.

We also obtain

\bigl|\big[A,A^{\dagger}_{h^{(\epsilon)}}\big]\bigr|_{h^{(\epsilon)}}\leq 2C^{2}C_{1}^{2}|z|^{4\eta-4\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(\mathcal{V})\epsilon}
.
Because

\displaystyle\bigl[\mathbb{D}^{\lambda\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits\star}_{h^{(\epsilon)}},A\,{\rm d}z/z\bigr]=\bigl[B^{(\epsilon)}_{2},A\bigr]\,|z|^{-2}{\rm d}\overline{z}\,{\rm d}z,

we obtain

\displaystyle\bigl|\bigl[\mathbb{D}^{\lambda\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits\star}_{h^{(\epsilon)}},A\,{\rm d}z/z\bigr]\bigr|_{g_{\epsilon},h^{(\epsilon)}}=\Bigl|\bigl[\mathbb{D}^{\lambda\mathop{\rm reg}\nolimits},A^{\dagger}_{h^{(\epsilon)}}\,{\rm d}\overline{z}/\overline{z}\bigr]\bigr|_{g_{\epsilon},h^{(\epsilon)}}\leq C_{3}C|z|^{2\eta}.

Hence, we obtain the desired estimate for

G\big(h^{(\epsilon)}\big)=\bigl[\mathbb{D}^{\lambda},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda\star}_{h^{(\epsilon)}}\bigr]
.
∎

3.9
Estimate of the curvature for Hermitian–Einstein metrics

of a Higgs bundle

Let
X
be a complex surface.
Let
\big(E,\overline{\partial}_{E},\theta\big)
be a Higgs bundle on
X
.
Let
g_{i}
be a sequence of Kähler metric on
X

which is convergent to a Kähler metric
g_{\infty}

in the
C^{\infty}
-sense locally on
X
.
Let
h_{i}

(i=1,2,\ldots)
be Hermitian–Einstein metrics
of the Higgs bundle. We assume the following:

•

\int_{X}|G(h_{i})|^{2}_{h_{i},g_{i}}\to 0
.

Let
\nabla_{h_{i}}
be the Chern connection
of
\big(E,\overline{\partial}_{E},h_{i}\big)
.
Let
R(h_{i})
denote the curvature of
\nabla_{h_{i}}
.
The following proposition is a refinement
contained in the argument
in
[
46
, Section 9.1.1]
.

Proposition 3.31
.

For any relatively compact open subset
U\subset X
,
and for any
p\geq 1
,
the
L_{2}^{p}
-norms of
R(h_{i})_{|U}

with respect to
h_{i}
and
g_{\infty}

are bounded.

Proof.

Let
P
be any point of
X
.
Let
(X_{P},z_{1},z_{2})
be a holomorphic coordinate neighbourhood
of
X
around
P
.
Let us describe
\theta

as
\theta=\sum_{j=1,2}f_{j}\,{\rm d}z_{j}
.
Let
X_{P,0}
be a relatively compact neighbourhood of
P
in
X_{P}
.
According to
[
46
, Lemma 2.13]
,
there exist
C_{k}>0

(k=1,2)
,
which are independent of
h_{i}
,
such that the following inequalities hold on
X_{P,0}
:

\displaystyle|f_{j}|_{h_{i}}^{2}\leq C_{1}\cdot\exp\bigg(C_{2}\int_{X}|G(h_{i})|_{h_{i}}^{2}\bigg).

(Note that
G(h_{i})
is denoted as
F(h_{i})

in
[
46
]
.)
Let
\epsilon
denote a positive number.
After rescaling the coordinate system,
we may assume the following on
X_{P,0}
:

\displaystyle\sum|f_{j}|_{h_{i}}^{2}\leq\epsilon/100.

There exists
i_{0}

such that for
i\geq i_{0}

we obtain

\int|G(h_{i})|_{h_{i},g_{i}}^{2}\leq\epsilon/100
.
Because the
L^{2}
-norms are scale invariant,
we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{X_{P,0}}\bigl|R(h_{i})\bigr|^{2}_{h_{i},g_{i,P}}\leq\epsilon/10.

Let
X_{P,1}
be a relatively compact neighbourhood of
P

in
X_{P,0}
.
If
\epsilon
is sufficiently small,
by the theorem of Uhlenbeck

[
74
, Corollary 2.2]
,
there exists an orthonormal frame
{\boldsymbol{v}}_{i}
of
(E,h_{i})_{|X_{P,1}}

for each
i

such that the connection form
A_{i}
of
\nabla_{h_{i}}

with respect to
{\boldsymbol{v}}_{i}

satisfies
(i)

A_{i}
is
L_{1}^{2}
,

(ii)

\|A_{i}\|_{L_{1}^{2}}\leq C_{3}\|R(h_{i})\|_{L^{2}}
on
X_{P,1}

for a positive constant
C_{3}
independently from
i
,

(iii)

A_{i}
satisfies

{\rm d}_{g_{i,P}}^{\ast}A_{i}=0
,
where
{\rm d}_{g_{i,P}}^{\ast}
denotes the adjoint of
{\rm d}

with respect to the metric
g_{i,P}
.
Let
\Theta_{i}
and
\Theta_{i}^{\dagger}

represent
\theta
and
\theta^{\dagger}_{h_{i}}

with respect to the frame
{\boldsymbol{v}}_{i}
.
Then,
A_{i}
satisfies

\displaystyle\Lambda_{g_{P,i}}({\rm d}A_{i}+A_{i}\wedge A_{i})+\Lambda_{g_{P,i}}\big[\Theta_{i},\Theta_{i}^{\dagger}\big]=0.

Let
X_{P,2}
be a relatively compact neighbourhood of
P

in
X_{P,1}
. By the argument of Donaldson
in the proof of
[
15
, Corollary 23]
,
we obtain that
A_{i}
are
L_{1}^{p}
for any
p\geq 2
on
X_{P,2}
,
and that there exists
C_{4,p}>0

such that
\|A_{i}\|_{L_{1}^{p}}\leq C_{4,p}
on
X_{P,2}
,
where
C_{4,p}
are independent of
i
. In particular,
there exists
C_{5,p}>0
independently from
i

such that
\big\|R(h_{i})_{|X_{P,2}}\big\|_{L^{p}}\leq C_{5,p}
.

Let
A_{i}=A_{i}^{0,1}+A_{i}^{1,0}

be the decomposition into the
(0,1)
-part and the
(1,0)
-part.
Because
\overline{\partial}\theta=0
,
we obtain
\overline{\partial}\Theta_{i}+\big[A_{i}^{0,1},\Theta_{i}\big]=0
.
Hence, there exist
C_{6,p}>0
independently from
i

such that
\big\|\Theta_{i|X_{P,2}}\big\|_{L_{2}^{p}}\leq C_{6,p}
.

Note that

\overline{\partial}_{E}R(h_{i})=0

and
\partial_{E,h_{i}}R(h_{i})=0
.
Let
\overline{\partial}^{\ast}_{E,h_{i},g_{i,P}}

denote the formal adjoint of

\overline{\partial}_{E}
with respect to
h_{i}
and
g_{i,P}
.
Because

\Lambda_{g_{i,P}}R(h_{i})+\Lambda_{g_{i,P}}\big[\theta_{i},\theta_{i}^{\dagger}\big]=0
,
there exists
C_{7,p}>0

such that

\bigl\|\overline{\partial}^{\ast}_{E,h_{i},g_{i,P}}R(h_{i})_{|X_{P,2}}\bigr\|_{L_{1}^{p}}<C_{7,p}
.
Let
X_{P,3}
be a relatively compact neighbourhood of
P

in
X_{P,2}
.
There exists
C_{8,p}>0
independently from
i

such that

\bigl\|R(h_{i})_{|X_{P,3}}\bigr\|_{L_{2}^{p}}<C_{8,p}
.
It implies the claim of the proposition.
∎

4
Existence and continuity of harmonic metrics

in the curve case

4.1
Existence of Hermitian–Einstein metric

Let
X
be a compact Riemann surface.
Let
D\subset X
be a finite subset.
Let
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
be
a stable good filtered
\lambda
-flat bundle on
(X,D)
.
Let
\big(E,\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
be the
\lambda
-flat bundle
on
X\setminus D

obtained as the restriction of
\big(\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
.
Let
\omega
be any Kähler form of
X
.
Let
h_{\det(E)}
be a Hermitian metric of
\det(E)

such that

(i)

\Lambda_{\omega}R(h_{\det(E)})=2\pi\deg(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})\big(\int_{X}\omega\big)^{-1}
,

(ii)

h_{\det(E)}
is adapted to
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\det(\mathcal{V})
,
i.e.,

\mathcal{P}^{h_{\det(E)}}_{\ast}\det(E)\simeq\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\det(\mathcal{V})
.
(See Proposition
3.2
.)

Theorem 4.1

(Biquard–Boalch)
.

There exists a unique Hermitian–Einstein metric

h
of
\big(E,\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

adapted to
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}

such that
\det(h)=h_{\det(E)}
.
If
\deg(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})=0
,

h
is a harmonic metric.

Proof.

It is enough to prove the case
\deg(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})=0
. We explain an outline of the proof
based on the fundamental theorem of Simpson

[
62
, Theorem 1]

(and its variant
[
49
, Proposition 2.49]
)
because we obtain a consequence
on the Donaldson functional
from the proof,
which will be useful in the proof of
Proposition
4.5

below.
Set
e:=\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(\mathcal{V})!
.
Take
\eta>0
such that

10e\eta<\widetilde{\mathop{\rm gap}\nolimits}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},{\boldsymbol{a}})
.
(See Section
3.5.4
for
\widetilde{\mathop{\rm gap}\nolimits}
.)

Let
\big(X_{P},z_{P}\big)
be an admissible coordinate neighbourhood
around
P
.
Set
X_{P}^{\ast}:=X_{P}\setminus\{P\}
. We take a Kähler metric
g_{X\setminus D}

of
X\setminus D

satisfying the following condition:

•

g_{X\setminus D|X_{P}^{\ast}}

is mutually bounded with

|z_{P}|^{-2+\eta}{\rm d}z_{P}\,{\rm d}\overline{z}_{P}

on
X_{P}^{\ast}

for each
P\in D
.

Recall that
the Kähler manifold

(X\setminus D,g_{X\setminus D})

satisfies the assumptions given in

[
62
, Section 2]
,
according to
[
62
, Proposition 2.4]
.

Lemma 4.2
.

There exists a Hermitian metric
h_{0}
of
E

such that the following holds:

(a)

\big(E,{\rm d}^{\prime\prime}_{E},h_{0}\big)
is acceptable,
and
\mathcal{P}^{h_{0}}_{\ast}E=\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}
.

(b)

G(h_{0})
is bounded with respect to

h_{0}
and
g_{X\setminus D}
.

(c)

\det(h_{0})=h_{\det(E)}
.

Proof.

By Corollary
3.28
,
we obtain a Hermitian metric
h_{0}^{\prime}
of
E

satisfying
(a)
and
(b)
. We define the function
\varphi\colon X\setminus D\longrightarrow{\mathbb{R}}

by
h_{\det(E)}=\det(h_{0}^{\prime}){\rm e}^{\varphi}
.
Then,
\varphi
induces a
C^{\infty}
-function on
X
. We set
h_{0}:=h_{0}^{\prime}{\rm e}^{\varphi/\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(E)}
.
Then, the metric
h_{0}
has the desired property.
∎

For any
\lambda
-flat subbundle
E^{\prime}\subset E
,
let
h^{\prime}_{0}
denote the Hermitian metric of
E^{\prime}
induced by
h_{0}
.
Let
\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{E^{\prime}}
denote the Higgs field of
E^{\prime}

obtained as the restriction of
\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}
. We obtain the Chern connection

\nabla_{h^{\prime}_{0}}

from the
(0,1)
-part of
\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{E^{\prime}}
and
h^{\prime}_{0}
.
Let
R(h^{\prime}_{0})
denote the curvature of
\nabla_{h_{0}^{\prime}}
. We set

\displaystyle\deg(E^{\prime},h_{0}):=\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}\frac{1}{1+|\lambda|^{2}}\int_{X\setminus D}\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits G\big(E^{\prime},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{E^{\prime}},h_{0}^{\prime}\big)=\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}\int_{X\setminus D}\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits R(h^{\prime}_{0}).

Let
\Lambda_{g_{X\setminus D,\eta}}

denote the adjoint of the multiplication by
the Kähler form associated with
g_{X\setminus D,\eta}
.
Because
G(h_{0})
is bounded with respect to

h_{0}
and
g_{X\setminus D,\eta}
,

\deg(E^{\prime},h_{0})
is well defined in
{\mathbb{R}}\cup\{-\infty\}

by the Chern–Weil formula
[
62
, Lemma 3.2]

(see also
[
49
, Lemma 2.34]
):

\displaystyle\deg(E^{\prime},h_{0})=\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}\frac{1}{1+|\lambda|^{2}}\int\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\bigl(\Lambda_{g_{X\setminus D,\eta}}G(h_{0})\pi_{E^{\prime}}\bigr)-\frac{1}{2\pi}\frac{1}{1+|\lambda|^{2}}\int\bigl|\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\pi_{E^{\prime}}\bigr|^{2}.

Here,
\pi_{E^{\prime}}
denotes the orthogonal projection

E\longrightarrow E^{\prime}
with respect to
h_{0}
.

Lemma 4.3
.

\deg(E^{\prime},h_{0})/\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(E^{\prime})<\deg(E,h_{0})/\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(E)
holds.
Namely,

(E,\mathbb{D}^{\lambda},h_{0})

is analytically stable in the sense of

[
63
, Section 6]

(
see also
[
49
, Section 2.3]
)
.

Proof.

By
[
63
, Lemma 6.1]
,
we have
\deg(E,h_{0})=\deg\big(\mathcal{P}^{h_{0}}_{\ast}E\big)=0
.
Let
0\neq E^{\prime}\subsetneq E
be a
\lambda
-flat subbundle on
X\setminus D
. By
[
63
, Lemma 6.2]
,
if
\deg(E^{\prime},h_{0})\neq-\infty
,

E^{\prime}
extends to a filtered subbundle

\mathcal{P}^{h_{0}^{\prime}}_{\ast}E^{\prime}\subset\mathcal{P}^{h_{0}}_{\ast}E
,
and

\deg(E^{\prime},h_{0})=\deg\big(\mathcal{P}^{h_{0}^{\prime}}_{\ast}E^{\prime}\big)
holds.
Because
(\mathcal{P}^{h_{0}}_{\ast}E,\mathbb{D}^{\lambda})
is assumed to be stable,
we obtain

\deg(E^{\prime},h_{0})/\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits E^{\prime}<\deg\big(\mathcal{P}^{h_{0}}_{\ast}E\big)/\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits E=0
.
Hence,
\big(E,\overline{\partial}_{E},\theta,h_{0}\big)
is
analytically stable.
∎

According to the existence theorem of Simpson
[
62
, Theorem 1]

(see also
[
49
, Proposition 2.49]
),
there exists a Hermitian–Einstein metric
h
of
\big(E,\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

such that
\det(h)=\det(h_{0})

and that
h
and
h_{0}
are mutually bounded. We already know the uniqueness
as in Proposition
2.22
.
Thus, we obtain Theorem
4.1
.
∎

4.1.1
Complement on the Donaldson functional

Let
h_{0}
and
g_{X\setminus D}
be
as in the proof of Theorem
4.1
.
Let
\mathcal{H}(h_{0})
be the space of

C^{\infty}
-Hermitian metrics
h_{1}
of
E

satisfying the following condition:

•

Let
u_{1}
be the endomorphism of
E

such that

(i)

h_{1}=h_{0}{\rm e}^{u_{1}}
,

(ii)

u_{1}
is self-adjoint with respect to both
h_{0}
and
h_{1}
.
Then,

\sup_{Q\in X\setminus D}|u_{1}|_{h_{0}}(Q)+\|\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}u_{1}\|_{L^{2}}+\bigl\|\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\mathbb{D}^{\lambda\star}_{h_{0}}u_{1}\bigr\|_{L^{1}}<\infty
.
Here, we use the
L^{p}
-norms
induced by
h_{0}
and
g_{X\setminus D}
.

The Donaldson functional

M(h_{0},\bullet)\colon\mathcal{H}(h_{0})\longrightarrow{\mathbb{R}}
is defined
as in
[
62
, Section 5]

and
[
49
, Section 2.4]
.

Proposition 4.4
.

Let
h
be the Hermitian–Einstein metric
in Theorem
4.1
.
Then,
h
is contained in
\mathcal{H}(h_{0})
,
and
M(h_{0},h)\leq 0
holds.

Proof.

Let
b
be the automorphism of
E

which is self-adjoint with respect to both
h
and
h_{0}
,
and determined by
h=h_{0}\cdot b
.
The theorem of Simpson
[
62
, Theorem 1]

(see also
[
49
, Proposition 2.49]
)
implies that

b
and
b^{-1}
are bounded,
and that
\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}b
is
L^{2}

with respect to
h_{0}

and
g_{X\setminus D}
. By
[
62
, Lemma 3.1]

(see also
[
49
, Section 2.2.5]
),
we also obtain
\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\mathbb{D}^{\lambda\star}_{h_{0}}b

is
L^{1}
.
Hence,
h
is contained in
\mathcal{H}(h_{0})
. In the proof of
[
62
, Theorem 1]

and
[
49
, Proposition 2.39]
,
the metric
h
is constructed as the limit
of a subsequence of the heat flow
h_{t}

(t\geq 0)

for which
\partial_{t}M(h_{0},h_{t})\leq 0
holds.
Because
M(h_{0},h_{0})=0
by the construction,
we obtain
M(h_{0},h_{t})\leq 0
,
and hence
M(h_{0},h)\leq 0
.
∎

4.2
Continuities of some families of Hermitian metrics

4.2.1
Setting

Family of curves.

Let
\Sigma
be a compact connected oriented real
2
-dimensional

C^{\infty}
-manifold
with a finite subset
D\subset\Sigma
.
Let
J_{i}

(i=1,2,\ldots)
be a sequence of complex structures
on
\Sigma

such that
the sequence
J_{i}
is convergent to
J
in the
C^{\infty}
-sense.
Assume that there exists a neighbourhood
N(D)
of
D
in
\Sigma

such that
J_{i|N(D)}
are independent of
i
.
Let
X_{i}
denote the compact Riemann surfaces

(\Sigma_{i},J_{i})
.
Similarly, let
X
denote the compact Riemann surface

(\Sigma,J)
.
Let
\kappa_{i}\colon(T\Sigma,J)\simeq(T\Sigma,J_{i})
be
isomorphisms of complex vector bundles on
\Sigma

such that

(i)

\kappa_{i}\to\mathop{\rm id}\nolimits
,

(ii)

\kappa_{i|N(D)}=\mathop{\rm id}\nolimits
. We regard
\kappa_{i}
as isomorphisms
of complex vector bundles
TX\simeq TX_{i}
.

For
P\in D
,
let
(X_{P},z_{P})
denote an admissible coordinate neighbourhood
of
P
in
X

such that
X_{P}\subset N(D)
. We may regard
\big(X_{P},z_{P}\big)

as a holomorphic coordinate neighbourhood
of
P
in
X_{i}
.
Let
r
be a positive integer, and set
e:=r!
.
As in Section
3.5.1
,
let
\varphi_{P}\colon\widetilde{X}_{P}\longrightarrow X_{P}
be
the ramified covering given by

\varphi_{P}(\zeta_{P})=\zeta_{P}^{e}
.
Let
G_{P}
denote the Galois group of
the ramified covering
\varphi_{P}
.

Family of good filtered
\boldsymbol{\lambda}
-flat bundles.

Let
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
be
a stable good filtered
\lambda
-flat bundle of rank
r

on
(X,D)
with
\deg(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})=0
.
Let
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{i},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{i}\big)

be stable good filtered
\lambda
-flat bundles
of rank
r
on
(X_{i},D)

with
\deg(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{i})=0
.
For each
P\in D
,
we set

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{P},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{P}\big):=\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)_{|X_{P}}

and

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{i,P},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{i,P}\big):=\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{i},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)_{|X_{P}}
.
Set

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{P},\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}_{P}\big):=\varphi_{P}^{\ast}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{P},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{P}\big)

and

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{i,P},\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}_{i,P}\big):=\varphi_{P}^{\ast}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{i,P},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{i,P}\big)
.
There exist
G_{P}
-invariant subsets

\mathcal{I}(P),\mathcal{I}(i,P)\subset\zeta_{P}^{-1}{\mathbb{C}}[\zeta_{P}^{-1}]

and the formal decompositions

\displaystyle\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{P},\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}_{P}\big)\otimes{\mathbb{C}}[\![\zeta]\!]=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}(P)}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{P,\mathfrak{a}},\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}_{P,\mathfrak{a}}\big),

\displaystyle\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{i,P},\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}_{i,P}\big)\otimes{\mathbb{C}}[\![\zeta]\!]=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}(i,P)}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{i,P,\mathfrak{a}},\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}_{i,P,\mathfrak{a}}\big),

for each
P\in D
.
Suppose moreover that there exist

G_{P}
-invariant bijections

\rho_{i,P}\colon\mathcal{I}(P)\simeq\mathcal{I}(i,P)

such that the following holds:

•

\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{P,\mathfrak{a}}=\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{i,P,\rho_{i,P}(\mathfrak{a})}
.

•

\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits\mathfrak{a}=\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits\rho_{i,P}(\mathfrak{a})

and

\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits(\mathfrak{a}-\mathfrak{b})=\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits\bigl(\rho_{i,P}(\mathfrak{a})-\rho_{i,P}(\mathfrak{b})\bigr)
.

•

\lim\limits_{i\to\infty}\rho_{i,P}(\mathfrak{a})=\mathfrak{a}

in
\zeta^{-1}{\mathbb{C}}\big[\zeta^{-1}\big]
.

We fix such bijections
\rho_{i,P}
.
Let
\pi_{P,\mathfrak{a}}
denote
the projection

\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{P}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}[\![\zeta]\!]\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{P,\mathfrak{a}}
.
Similarly,
let
\pi_{i,P,\mathfrak{a}}
denote
the projection

\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{i,P}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}[\![\zeta]\!]\longrightarrow\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{i,P,\mathfrak{a}}
.

\boldsymbol{C^{\infty}}
-isomorphisms.

We set
\big(E,\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big):=\big(\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)_{|X\setminus D}

and
\big(E_{i},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{i}\big):=\big(\mathcal{V}_{i},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{i}\big)_{|X_{i}\setminus D}
.
Let
h_{0}
denote
C^{\infty}
-metrics of
E

adapted to
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}

such that
R(\det(h_{0}))=0
.
Let
h_{0,i}
denote
C^{\infty}
-metrics of
E_{i}

adapted to
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{i}

such that
R(\det(h_{0,i}))=0
.
Let
d^{\prime\prime}
and
d_{i}^{\prime\prime}
denote the
(0,1)
-parts
of
\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}
and
\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{i}
.
Suppose that there exist

C^{\infty}
-isomorphisms
f_{i}\colon E\simeq E_{i}

satisfying the following conditions:

•

f_{i}^{\ast}(h_{0,i})\to h_{0}
in the
C^{\infty}
-sense
locally on
\Sigma\setminus D
.

•

On
N(D)\setminus D
,

f_{i}
are holomorphic with respect to

d^{\prime\prime}
and
d^{\prime\prime}_{i}
,
and
f_{i}
extend to isomorphisms
of filtered bundles

\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{|N(D)}\simeq\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{i|N(D)}
.

•

For each
P\in D
, we obtain

\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{c}\big(f_{i|X_{P}}\big)\circ\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{P}(\mathbb{D}^{\lambda})=\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{P}\big(\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{i}\big)\circ\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{c}\big(f_{i|X_{P}}\big)

on
\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{c}(\mathcal{V}_{P})
for any
c\in{\mathbb{R}}
.
Moreover, there exists
N(P)\geq 10\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(\mathcal{V})|\mathop{\rm ord}\nolimits\mathfrak{a}|

for any
\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}(P)

such that for the induced isomorphisms

\varphi_{P}^{\ast}\big(f_{i|X_{P}}\big)\colon\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{P}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}[\![\zeta]\!]\simeq\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{i,P}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}[\![\zeta]\!]
,
we obtain

\displaystyle\bigl(\pi_{P,\mathfrak{a}}-\varphi_{P}^{\ast}\big(f_{i|X_{P}}\big)^{-1}\circ\pi_{i,P,\rho_{i,P}(\mathfrak{a})}\circ\varphi_{P}^{\ast}\big(f_{i|X_{P}}\big)\bigr)\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{P}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}[\![\zeta]\!]

\displaystyle\qquad{}\subset\mathcal{P}_{\ast-N(P)}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{i,P}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}[\![\zeta]\!],

(4.1)

and the sequences (
4.1
) are convergent to
0

as
i\to\infty
.

•

\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}-(f_{i}\otimes\kappa_{i})^{-1}\circ\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{i}\circ f_{i}\to 0

in the
C^{\infty}
-sense
with respect to
h_{0}

locally on
\Sigma\setminus D
.

Perturbation.

We take
\eta_{i}

(i=1,2)
satisfying

10e\eta_{1}<\widetilde{\mathop{\rm gap}\nolimits}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})

and

10r\eta_{2}<\eta_{1}
. We take
{\boldsymbol{a}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{D}

for
\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}{\rm ar}}}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},P)

(P\in D)

as in Lemma
3.19
.
For any
0<\epsilon<\eta_{2}
,
by taking
\psi_{P,\epsilon}

(P\in D)

as in Section
3.7.1
,
we obtain families of
good filtered
\lambda
-flat bundles

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}^{(\epsilon)}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

and

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}^{(\epsilon)}\mathcal{V}_{i},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{i}\big)
. We assume the following for each
P\in D
:

\displaystyle\sum_{a(P)-1<c\leq a(P)}\psi_{P,\epsilon}(c)\,\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{c}\big(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V}_{|P}\big)=\sum_{a(P)-1<c\leq a(P)}c\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{c}\big(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V}_{|P}\big).

In particular,

\deg(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})=\deg\big(\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}\big)

and

\deg(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{i})=\deg\big(\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{i}\big)

hold. By making
\eta_{2}
smaller,
we may assume that

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}^{(\epsilon)}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

are stable for any
0\leq\epsilon\leq\eta_{2}
.

4.2.2
Continuity of the family of harmonic metrics

According to Theorem
4.1
,
there exists a harmonic metric

h^{(\epsilon)}_{i}

of
\big(E_{i},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

adapted to

\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{i}

such that

\det h_{i}^{(\epsilon)}=\det h_{0,i}
.
Similarly,
there exists a harmonic metric
h^{(0)}

of
\big(E,\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

adapted to
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}

such that

\det h^{(0)}=\det h_{0}
.
The following proposition is
a variant of
[
49
, Propositions 4.1 and 4.2]
.

Proposition 4.5
.

For any sequence

\epsilon_{i}\to 0
,
the sequence
h_{i}^{(\epsilon_{i})}
is convergent to
h^{(0)}

locally on
X\setminus D

in the
C^{\infty}
-sense.

Proof.

For
0\leq\epsilon\leq\eta_{2}
,
let
g_{X\setminus D,\epsilon}
be
the Kähler metric on
X\setminus D

such that the following holds on
X_{P}^{\ast}
for any
P\in D
:

\displaystyle g_{X\setminus D,\epsilon|X_{P}^{\ast}}=\big(\epsilon^{2}|z_{P}|^{2\epsilon}+\eta_{1}^{2}|z_{P}|^{2\eta_{1}}\big)|z_{P}|^{-2}{\rm d}z_{P}\,{\rm d}\overline{z}_{P}.

Let
\Lambda_{\epsilon}

denote the adjoint of the multiplication by
the Kähler form
\omega_{X\setminus D,\epsilon}

associated with
g_{X\setminus D,\epsilon}
.

By the isomorphisms

\kappa_{i}\colon(T\Sigma,J)\simeq(T\Sigma,J_{i})

and the metrics
g_{X\setminus D,\epsilon}
,
we obtain the Kähler metrics

g_{X_{i}\setminus D,\epsilon}

of
X_{i}\setminus D
.
Let
\Lambda_{i,\epsilon}

denote the adjoint of the multiplication by
the Kähler form
\omega_{X_{i}\setminus D,\epsilon}

associated with
g_{X_{i}\setminus D,\epsilon}
.

There exists an approximation of

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)_{|X_{P}}

by a model filtered
\lambda
-flat bundle as in
Section
3.6
. By using a family of Hermitian metrics for
the model
\lambda
-flat bundle as in Proposition
3.26
,
and by using Proposition
3.27
,
we construct a family of metrics

h^{(\epsilon)}_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}

(0\leq\epsilon\leq\eta_{2})

of
E

such that the following holds:

•

h^{(\epsilon)}_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}
is adapted to

\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}
.

•

\det h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}^{(\epsilon)}=\det h_{0}
.

•

h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}^{(\epsilon)}\longrightarrow h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}^{(0)}

locally on
X\setminus D
in the
C^{\infty}
-sense
as
\epsilon\longrightarrow 0
.

•

There exists
C_{1}>0

such that

\big|G\big(h^{(\epsilon)}_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}\big)\big|_{g_{X\setminus D,\epsilon},h^{(\epsilon)}_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}}<C_{1}

for any
\epsilon
.

Let
\nu_{i}
be the function on
X_{i}\setminus D

determined by

\big(f_{i}^{-1}\big)^{\ast}(\det h_{0})={\rm e}^{\nu_{i}}\det h_{i,0}
. We obtain the Hermitian metrics

h_{i,\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}^{(\epsilon)}:={\rm e}^{\nu_{i}/\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits\mathcal{V}}\big(f_{i}^{-1}\big)^{\ast}\big(h^{(\epsilon)}_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}\big)

(0\leq\epsilon\leq\eta_{2})

of
E_{i}
.
Then, by Proposition
3.27
,
we obtain the following:

•

h^{(\epsilon)}_{i,\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}
is adapted to

\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{i}
.

•

\det h_{i,\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}^{(\epsilon)}=\det h_{i,0}
.

•

h_{i,\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}^{(\epsilon)}\longrightarrow h_{i,\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}^{(0)}

locally on
X\setminus D
in the
C^{\infty}
-sense
as
\epsilon\longrightarrow 0
.

•

By replacing
C_{1}
with a larger constant,
we may assume

\big|G\big(h^{(\epsilon)}_{i,\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}\big)\big|_{g_{X_{i}\setminus D,\epsilon},h^{(\epsilon)}_{i,\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}}<C_{1}

for any
\epsilon
and any
i
.

Lemma 4.6
.

Let
u^{(i)}

(\epsilon_{i}\to 0)

be automorphisms of

E_{i}
which are self-adjoint with respect to
h^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{i,\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}

such that the following holds:

•

\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big(u^{(i)}\big)=0
.

•

h_{i,\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}^{(\epsilon_{i})}{\rm e}^{u^{(i)}}\in\mathcal{H}\big(h_{in}^{(\epsilon_{i})}\big)
,
i.e.,

\sup\big\|u^{(i)}\big\|_{h^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{i,\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}}+\big\|\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{i}u^{(i)}\big\|_{L^{2}}+\big\|\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{i}\mathbb{D}^{\lambda\star}_{i,h^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{i,\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}}u^{(i)}\big\|_{L^{1}}<\infty
,
where the
L^{p}
-norms are taken
with respect to
h^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{i,\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}
and

g_{X_{i}\setminus D,\epsilon_{i}}
. We do not assume that the estimate is uniform in
i
.

•

There exists
C_{2}>0
such that

\bigl|\Lambda_{i,\epsilon_{i}}G\big(h^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{i,\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}{\rm e}^{u^{(i)}}\big)\bigr|_{h^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{i,\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}}<C_{2}

for any
i
.

Then, there exists
C_{3},C_{4}>0
such that
the following holds for any
\epsilon_{i}

\displaystyle\sup\big|u^{(i)}\big|_{h^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{i,\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}}<C_{3}+C_{4}M\big(h^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{i,\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits},h^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{i,\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}{\rm e}^{u^{(i)}}\big).

Proof.

By identifying the vector bundles
E_{i}
and
E
by
f_{i}
,
we apply the same argument
as in the proof of
[
49
, Lemma 2.45]
.
∎

Let
b_{i,1}^{(\epsilon)}
be the automorphism of

E_{i}
which is self-adjoint with respect to

h_{i,\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}^{(\epsilon)}
and
h^{(\epsilon)}_{i}

and determined by

h^{(\epsilon)}_{i}=h_{i,\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}^{(\epsilon)}b_{i,1}^{(\epsilon)}
.
Note that
\det(b_{i,1}^{(\epsilon)})=1
.
Take any sequence
\epsilon_{i}\to 0
. By Proposition
4.4

and Lemma
4.6
,
there exists a constant
C_{10}>0

such that the following holds for any
i
:

\displaystyle\sup_{Q\in X_{i}\setminus D}\bigl|b^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{i,1|Q}\bigr|_{h_{i,\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}^{(\epsilon_{i})}}<C_{10},\qquad\sup_{Q\in X_{i}\setminus D}\bigl|\big(b^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{i,1|Q}\big)^{-1}\bigr|_{h_{i,\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}^{(\epsilon_{i})}}<C_{10}.

Lemma 4.7
.

\int\Lambda_{i,\epsilon_{i}}\bigl(\overline{\partial}_{X_{i}}\partial_{X_{i}}\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big(b_{i,1}^{(\epsilon_{i})}\big)\bigr)\omega_{X_{i}\setminus D,\epsilon_{i}}=0
holds.

Proof.

We use Proposition
4.4
.
Because

\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{i}\mathbb{D}^{\lambda\star}_{i}b_{i,1}^{(\epsilon_{i})}

is
L^{1}
with respect to
h_{i,\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}^{(\epsilon_{i})}

and
g_{X_{i}\setminus D,\epsilon_{i}}
,
we obtain that

\Lambda_{i,\epsilon_{i}}\overline{\partial}_{X_{i}}\partial_{X_{i}}\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big(b_{i,1}^{(\epsilon_{i})}\big)
is
L^{1}

with respect to

g_{X_{i}\setminus D,\epsilon_{i}}
.
Because

\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{i}b_{i,1}^{(\epsilon_{i})}
is
L^{2}

with respect to
g_{X_{i}\setminus D,\epsilon_{i}}

and
h^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{i,\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}
,
we obtain that
\partial_{X_{i}}\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big(b_{i,1}^{(\epsilon_{i})}\big)
is
L^{2}

with respect to
g_{X_{i}\setminus D,\epsilon_{i}}
.
Therefore, we obtain the claim of the lemma
by using
[
62
, Lemma 5.2]
.
∎

By
[
62
, Lemma 3.1]
,
the following holds:

\displaystyle\sqrt{-1}\Lambda_{i,\epsilon_{i}}\overline{\partial}_{X_{i}}\partial_{X_{i}}\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big(b_{i,1}^{(\epsilon_{i})}\big)=-\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\bigl(b_{i,1}^{(\epsilon_{i})}\Lambda_{i,\epsilon_{i}}G\big(h^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{i,\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}\big)\bigr)-\bigl|\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{i}\big(b_{i,1}^{(\epsilon_{i})}\big)\cdot\big(b_{i,1}^{(\epsilon_{i})}\big)^{-1/2}\bigr|^{2}_{h^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{i,\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits},g_{X_{i}\setminus D,\epsilon_{i}}}.

Therefore, there exists
C_{12}>0
such that
the following holds for any
i
:

\displaystyle\int\bigl|\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{i}b_{i,1}^{(\epsilon_{i})}\bigr|^{2}_{h^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{i,\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits},g_{X_{i}\setminus D,\epsilon_{i}}}\omega_{X_{i}\setminus D,\epsilon_{i}}<C_{12}.

We also obtain

\displaystyle\int\bigl|\mathbb{D}^{\lambda\star}_{i}b_{i,1}^{(\epsilon_{i})}\bigr|^{2}_{h^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{i,\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits},g_{X_{i}\setminus D,\epsilon_{i}}}\omega_{X_{i}\setminus D,\epsilon_{i}}<C_{12}.

(4.2)

Let
\big(E_{i},\overline{\partial}^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{E_{i}},\theta_{i}^{(\epsilon_{i})}\big)

be the Higgs bundles underlying

\big(E_{i},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda},h_{i}^{(\epsilon_{i})}\big)
.
Then, there exists
C_{13}>0

such that the following holds for any
i
:

\displaystyle\int\bigl|\theta^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{i}\bigr|^{2}_{h^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{i,\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits},g_{X_{i}\setminus D,\epsilon_{i}}}\omega_{X_{i}\setminus D,\epsilon_{i}}<C_{13}.

Then, by applying the argument
in
[
49
, Section 4.5.3]
,
we obtain the desired convergence
of the sequence
h^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{i}
.
∎

4.2.3
Continuity of some families of Hermitian metrics

For
P\in D
,
we set
X_{P}^{\ast}:=X_{P}\setminus\{P\}
. We may naturally regard
X_{P}^{\ast}

as a subset of
X_{i}\setminus D
.
Fix
N>10
.
Let
g_{i,\epsilon}
be a sequence of
Kähler metrics of
X_{i}\setminus D
,
such that

g_{i,\epsilon}\to g_{\epsilon}

(i\to\infty)

and that

\displaystyle g_{i,\epsilon|X_{P}^{\ast}}=\bigl(\epsilon^{N+2}|z_{P}|^{2\epsilon}+|z_{P}|^{2}\bigr)\frac{{\rm d}z_{P}\,{\rm d}\overline{z}_{P}}{|z_{P}|^{2}}.

Let
\epsilon_{i}

(i=1,2,\ldots)

be a sequence such that

\epsilon_{i}\to 0
.
The following proposition
is a variant and a refinement
of
[
49
, Proposition 5.1]
.

Proposition 4.8
.

Let
h_{i,1}^{(\epsilon_{i})}

(i=1,2,\ldots)

be Hermitian metrics
of
E_{i}
satisfying the following conditions:

•

\det h_{i,1}^{(\epsilon_{i})}=\det h_{i,0}
.

•

\big\|G\big(h_{i,1}^{(\epsilon_{i})}\big)\big\|_{L^{2},g_{i,\epsilon},h_{i,1}^{(\epsilon_{i})}}\to 0
as
i\to\infty
.

•

Let
s^{(i)}
be the automorphism of
E_{i}

which is self-adjoint
with respect to
h_{i}^{(\epsilon_{i})}

and determined by

h_{i,1}^{(\epsilon_{i})}=h_{i}^{(\epsilon_{i})}s^{(i)}
.
Then,
s^{(i)}
and
\big(s^{(i)}\big)^{-1}
are bounded
with respect to
h_{i}^{(\epsilon_{i})}

on
X\setminus D
,
and
\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{i}s^{(i)}
are
L^{2}

with respect to
h_{i}^{(\epsilon_{i})}

and
g_{i,\epsilon_{i}}
.
The estimates may depend on
i
.

Then,
the sequence

\big\{f_{i}^{\ast}\big(s^{(i)}\big)\big\}
is weakly convergent to
\mathop{\rm id}\nolimits_{E}

in
L_{1}^{2}
locally on
X\setminus D
.
Moreover,
there exists
A>0
such that

\big|s^{(i)}\big|_{h_{i}^{(\epsilon_{i})}}<A

and
\big|\big(s^{(i)}\big)^{-1}\big|_{h_{i}^{(\epsilon_{i})}}<A

for any
i
.

Proof.

This is essentially
the same as
[
49
, Proposition 5.1]
. We explain an outline of the proof. We identify
E_{i}
with
E
by
f_{i}
. We set

\displaystyle c_{i}:=\sup_{\Sigma\setminus D}\big|s^{(i)}\big|_{h^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{i}}.

We set

\widetilde{s}^{(i)}:=c_{i}^{-1}s^{(i)}
. We set

\widetilde{h}^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{i,1}:=c_{i}^{-1}h^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{i,1}=h^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{i}\widetilde{s}^{(i)}
.
The following holds.

\displaystyle(1+|\lambda|^{2})\Delta_{g_{i,0}}\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\widetilde{s}^{(i)}=\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\bigl(\widetilde{s}^{(i)}\sqrt{-1}\Lambda_{g_{i,0}}G\big(\widetilde{h}^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{i,1}\big)\bigr)+\sqrt{-1}\Lambda_{g_{i,0}}\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\bigl(\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{i}\widetilde{s}^{(i)}\big(\widetilde{s}^{(i)}\big)^{-1}\mathbb{D}^{\lambda\star}_{i,h^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{i}}\widetilde{s}^{(i)}\bigr).

From the boundedness of
\widetilde{s}^{(i)}

and the
L^{2}
-property of

\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\widetilde{s}^{(i)}
,
we obtain

\int\Delta_{g_{i,0}}\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big(\widetilde{s}^{(i)}\big)\mathop{\rm dvol}\nolimits_{g_{i,0}}=0

as in Lemma
4.7
. We obtain the following for some
A>0
and
A^{\prime}>0
:

\displaystyle\int\bigl|\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big(\widetilde{s}^{(i)}\big)\big(\widetilde{s}^{(i)}\big)^{-1/2}\bigr|_{g_{i,0},h^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{i}}\mathop{\rm dvol}\nolimits_{g_{i,0}}

\displaystyle\leq A\cdot\int\bigl|\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\Lambda_{g_{i,0}}G\big(\widetilde{h}^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{i,1}\big)\bigr|\cdot\mathop{\rm dvol}\nolimits_{g_{i,0}}

\displaystyle=A\cdot\int\bigl|\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\Lambda_{g_{i,\epsilon}}G\big(\widetilde{h}^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{i,1}\big)\bigr|\cdot\mathop{\rm dvol}\nolimits_{g_{i,\epsilon}}

\displaystyle\leq A^{\prime}\bigl\|G\big(\widetilde{h}^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{i,1}\big)\bigr\|_{L^{2},h^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{i},g_{i,\epsilon}}.

Hence,
the sequence

\widetilde{s}^{(i)}
is
L_{1}^{2}
-bounded
on any compact subset of
X\setminus D
. By taking an appropriate subsequence,
it is weakly convergent in
L_{1}^{2}

locally on
X\setminus D
.
Let
\widetilde{s}^{(\infty)}
denote the weak limit of the sequence. We obtain
\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\widetilde{s}^{(\infty)}=0
.
Because
\widetilde{s}^{(i)}
are self-adjoint
and uniformly bounded with respect to
h_{i}^{(\epsilon_{i})}
,

\widetilde{s}^{(\infty)}
is self-adjoint and bounded
with respect to
h^{(0)}
. We can prove that

\widetilde{s}^{(\infty)}\neq 0

by the same argument
as in the proof of
[
49
, Lemma 5.2]
.
Hence,
\widetilde{s}^{(\infty)}
is a non-zero endomorphism of

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
.
It implies that
\widetilde{s}^{(\infty)}
is a multiplication
by a positive constant
u_{\infty}
.

Note that the sequence
\widetilde{s}^{(i)}

is convergent in
L^{p}
for any
p

locally on
X\setminus D
,
and hence

\det\big(\widetilde{s}^{(i)}\big)
is convergent to

\det\big(\widetilde{s}^{(\infty)}\big)
in
L^{p}
for any
p

locally on
X\setminus D
.
Because
\det\big(s^{(i)}\big)=1
, we obtain that
the sequence
c_{i}^{-\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(\mathcal{V})}
is convergent to

u_{\infty}^{\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(\mathcal{V})}
. In particular, it implies that
the sequence
c_{i}
is bounded.
Then, we obtain the claim of the proposition.
∎

4.3
Tensor product of stable filtered
\boldsymbol{\lambda}
-flat sheaves

Let us state a consequence of Theorem
4.1

on the tensor product of reflexive filtered
\lambda
-flat sheaves
on arbitrary dimensional projective varieties.

Let
X
be an
n
-dimensional non-singular projective variety
equipped with a very ample line bundle
L
.
Let
H
be a simple normal crossing hypersurface of
X

with the irreducible decomposition

H=\bigcup_{i\in\Lambda}H_{i}
.
Let
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{i},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{i}\big)

(i=1,2)

be reflexive filtered
\lambda
-flat sheaves on
(X,H)
. We assume the following condition:

Condition 4.9
.

There exists a Zariski closed subset
Z\subset H

with
\dim Z<n-1

such that

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{i},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{i}\big)_{|X\setminus Z}

(i=1,2)

are good filtered
\lambda
-flat bundles on

(X\setminus Z,H\setminus Z)
.

For example,
if
\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}
is logarithmic and if
\lambda\neq 0
,
Condition
4.9
is satisfied.

We set

\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}:=\mathcal{V}_{1}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H)}\mathcal{V}_{2}

which is equipped with
the induced flat
\lambda
-connection
\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}
.
Note that
Z\subset H

and that
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}_{|X\setminus Z}
is
a locally free
\mathcal{O}_{X\setminus Z}(\ast(H\setminus Z))
-module.
There exists the natural morphism

\displaystyle\varphi\colon\ \widetilde{\mathcal{V}}\longrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{\lor\lor}:={\mathcal{H}{\rm om}}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H)}\bigl({\mathcal{H}{\rm om}}_{\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H)}\big(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}},\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H)\big),\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H)\bigr).

The
\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H)
-modules

\mathop{\rm Ker}\nolimits\varphi

and
\mathop{\rm Cok}\nolimits\varphi

are coherent,
and their supports are contained in
Z\subset H
.
Hence, we obtain that
\mathop{\rm Ker}\nolimits\varphi=\mathop{\rm Cok}\nolimits\varphi=0
.
It implies that
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}\simeq\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}^{\lor\lor}
,
i.e.,
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}

is a reflexive
\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H)
-module.
For
{\boldsymbol{a}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\Lambda}
,
we set

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}:=\sum_{{\boldsymbol{b}}_{1}+{\boldsymbol{b}}_{2}={\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathop{\rm Im}\nolimits\bigl(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{b}}_{1}}\mathcal{V}_{1}\otimes_{\mathcal{O}_{X}}\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{b}}_{2}}\mathcal{V}_{2}\longrightarrow\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}\bigr).

Let
\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}

denote the coherent reflexive subsheaf
of
\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}

generated by

\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}
.
Thus, we obtain a reflexive filtered
\lambda
-flat sheaf

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}},\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}\big)

on
(X,H)
.

Proposition 4.10
.

If
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{i},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{i}\big)

are
\mu_{L}
-stable,
then

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}},\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}\big)

is
\mu_{L}
-polystable.

Proof.

According to Propositions
3.8
,

3.9

and Condition
4.9
,
there exists a positive integer
m

such that the following holds
for any general complete intersection curve
Y
of

L^{\otimes\,m}
.

•

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{i},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{i}\big)_{|Y}

are stable good filtered
\lambda
-flat bundles.

•

The natural morphism

\displaystyle\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits\bigl(\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}},\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}\big),\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}},\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}\big)\bigr)\longrightarrow\mathop{\rm Hom}\nolimits\bigl(\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}},\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}\big)_{|Y},\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}},\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}\big)_{|Y}\bigr)

is an isomorphism.

Because
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{i},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{i}\big)_{|Y}

are stable good filtered
\lambda
-flat bundles,
each
\lambda
-flat bundle

(\mathcal{V}_{i},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{i})_{|Y\setminus H}

are equipped with a Hermitian–Einstein metric
h_{i}

adapted to the filtered bundle

\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{i}

by Theorem
4.1
.
Because
h_{1}\otimes h_{2}
is a Hermitian–Einstein
metric of the
\lambda
-flat bundle

\big(\widetilde{\mathcal{V}},\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}\big)_{|Y\setminus H}

adapted to the filtered bundle
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}}
,
we obtain that

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}},\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}\big)_{|Y}

is polystable. By Corollary
3.10
,
we obtain that

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\widetilde{\mathcal{V}},\widetilde{\mathbb{D}}^{\lambda}\big)
is

\mu_{L}
-polystable.
∎

5
Preliminary existence theorem for Hermitian–Einstein

metrics

5.1
Statements

5.1.1
Kähler metrics

Let
X
be a smooth projective surface
with a simple normal crossing hypersurface

H=\bigcup_{i\in\Lambda}H_{i}
.
Let
L
be an ample line bundle on
X
.
Let
g_{X}
be the Kähler metric of
X

such that the associated Kähler form
\omega_{X}

represents
c_{1}(L)
.

We take Hermitian metrics
g_{i}
of
\mathcal{O}(H_{i})
.
Let
\sigma_{i}\colon\mathcal{O}_{X}\longrightarrow\mathcal{O}_{X}(H_{i})

denote the canonical section.
Take
N>10
.
There exists
C>0
such that
the following form defines a Kähler form
on
X\setminus H
for any
0\leq\epsilon<1/10
:

\displaystyle\omega_{\epsilon}:=\omega_{X}+\sum_{i\in\Lambda}C\cdot\epsilon^{N+2}\cdot\sqrt{-1}\partial\overline{\partial}|\sigma_{i}|^{2\epsilon}_{g_{i}}.

It is easy to observe that

\int_{X}\omega_{\epsilon}^{2}=\int_{X}\omega_{X}^{2}
and
that

\int_{X}\omega_{\epsilon}\tau=\int_{X}\omega_{X}\tau

for any closed
C^{\infty}
-
(1,1)
-form
\tau
on
X
.

5.1.2
Condition for good filtered
\boldsymbol{\lambda}
-flat bundles and initial metrics

Let
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

be a good filtered
\lambda
-flat bundle on
(X,H)

satisfying the following condition: We set
e:=\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(\mathcal{V})!
.

Condition 5.1
.

•

There exists
{\boldsymbol{c}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\Lambda}

and
m\in e{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}
such that

{\mathcal{P}{\rm ar}}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},i)=\{c_{i}+n/m\mid n\in{\mathbb{Z}}\}

for each
i\in\Lambda
.

•

The nilpotent part of
\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits_{i}\big(\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

on
{}^{i}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{b}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})

are
0

for any
i\in\Lambda
,

{\boldsymbol{a}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\Lambda}

and
b\in]a_{i}-1,a_{i}[
.

Let
\big(E,\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
denote the
\lambda
-flat bundle
on
X\setminus H

obtained as the restriction of
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
.

Let
P
be any point of
H_{i}\setminus\bigcup_{j\neq i}H_{j}
.
Let
(X_{P},z_{1},z_{2})
be an admissible coordinate
neighbourhood around
P
.
There exists an open subset

X_{P}^{\prime}
in
{\mathbb{C}}^{2}=\{(\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2})\}

such that the map

\varphi_{P}\colon X_{P}^{\prime}\longrightarrow X_{P}

given by

\varphi_{P}(\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2})=(\zeta_{1}^{m},\zeta_{2})

is a ramified covering. We set
H_{P}^{\prime}:=\{\zeta_{1}=0\}\allowbreak\cap X_{P}^{\prime}
. We obtain the induced good filtered
\lambda
-flat bundle

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\varphi_{P}^{\ast}\mathcal{V},\varphi_{P}^{\ast}\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

on
(X_{P}^{\prime},H_{P}^{\prime})

such that

{\mathcal{P}{\rm ar}}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\varphi_{P}^{\ast}\mathcal{V}\big)=\{m\cdot c_{i}\}+{\mathbb{Z}}
.

Definition 5.2
.

A Hermitian metric
h_{P}
of
E_{|X_{P}\setminus H}

is called strongly adapted to

\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{|X_{P}}

if there exists a
C^{\infty}
Hermitian metric
h_{P}^{\prime}
of

\mathcal{P}_{mc_{i}}\big(\varphi_{P}^{\ast}\mathcal{V}\big)
on
X_{P}^{\prime}

such that

\varphi^{-1}(h_{P})=|\zeta_{1}|^{-2mc_{i}}h_{P}^{\prime}
.

Let
P
be any point of
H_{i}\cap H_{j}

(i\neq j)
.
Let
(X_{P},z_{1},z_{2})
be a admissible coordinate neighbourhood
around
P
such that

X_{P}\cap H_{i}=\{z_{1}=0\}

and
X_{P}\cap H_{j}=\{z_{2}=0\}
.
There exists an open subset

X_{P}^{\prime}
in
{\mathbb{C}}^{2}=\{(\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2})\}

such that the map

\varphi_{P}\colon X_{P}^{\prime}\longrightarrow X_{P}

given by

\varphi_{P}(\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2})=(\zeta_{1}^{m},\zeta_{2}^{m})

is a ramified covering. We set
H_{P}^{\prime}:=\{\zeta_{1}\zeta_{2}=0\}\cap X_{P}^{\prime}
. We obtain the induced good filtered
\lambda
-flat bundle

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\varphi_{P}^{\ast}\mathcal{V},\varphi^{\ast}\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

on
(X_{P}^{\prime},H_{P}^{\prime})

such that

{\mathcal{P}{\rm ar}}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\varphi_{P}^{\ast}\mathcal{V},1\big)=\{m\cdot c_{i}\}+{\mathbb{Z}}

and

{\mathcal{P}{\rm ar}}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\varphi_{P}^{\ast}\mathcal{V},2\big)=\{m\cdot c_{j}\}+{\mathbb{Z}}
.

Definition 5.3
.

A Hermitian metric
h_{P}
of

E_{|X_{P}\setminus H}
is called strongly adapted
to
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{|X_{P}}

if there exists a
C^{\infty}
-Hermitian metric
h_{P}^{\prime}

of
\mathcal{P}_{(mc_{i},mc_{j})}\varphi_{P}^{\ast}(\mathcal{V})

such that

\varphi^{\ast}(h_{P})=|\zeta_{1}|^{-mc_{i}}|\zeta_{2}|^{-mc_{j}}h_{P}^{\prime}
.

Definition 5.4
.

A Hermitian metric
h
of
E

is called strongly adapted to
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}

if the following holds:

•

For any
P\in H
,
there exists a neighbourhood
X_{P}
of
P

such that

h_{|X_{P}\setminus H}

is strongly adapted to

\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{|X_{P}}

in the sense of
Definitions
5.2

and
5.3
.

Lemma 5.5
.

Let
h
be a Hermitian metric of
E

strongly adapted to
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}
.
Then, the following holds:

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}\right)^{2}\int_{X\setminus H}\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\bigl(R(h)^{2}\bigr)=2\int_{X}\mathop{\rm ch}\nolimits_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}).

Proof.

It is the equality (36) in the proof of

[
46
, Proposition 4.18]
.
∎

For each
i\in\Lambda
,
we choose

b_{i}\in{\mathcal{P}{\rm ar}}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\det\mathcal{V},i)
.
Set
{\boldsymbol{b}}=(b_{i})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\Lambda}
. We take a Hermitian metric
h_{\det(E)}

of
\det(E)

such that

h_{\det(E)}\prod_{i\in\Lambda}|\sigma_{i}|_{g_{i}}^{2b_{i}}

induces a Hermitian metric of

\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{b}}}\det\mathcal{V}
of
C^{\infty}
-class.

Proposition 5.6
.

There exists a Hermitian metric
h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}
of
E

such that the following holds:

•

h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}
is strongly adapted to
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}
.

•

G(h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits})
is bounded
with respect to
h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}
and
\omega_{\epsilon}
,
where
\epsilon:=m^{-1}
.

•

The following holds:

\displaystyle\int_{X\setminus H}\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\bigl(R(h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits})^{2}\bigr)=\frac{1}{(1+|\lambda|^{2})^{2}}\int_{X\setminus H}\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\bigl(G(h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits})^{2}\bigr).

(5.1)

•

\det(h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits})=h_{\det(E)}
.

Such a Hermitian metric
h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}
is called
an initial metric of

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
.

The case
\lambda=1
was studied
in
[
51
, Sections 14.1, 14.2 and Lemma 14.4.2]
.
The case
\lambda\neq 1
can be argued
in the essentially same way. We shall explain the construction in the case
\lambda=0

in Section
5.4

after preliminaries in
Sections
5.2
–
5.3
.

5.1.3
Preliminary existence theorem for Hermitian–Einstein metrics

Let
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

be a good filtered
\lambda
-flat bundle
satisfying Condition
5.1
.
Let
h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}
be an initial metric for
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

as in Proposition
5.6
. We shall prove the following theorem
in Section
5.5
.

Theorem 5.7
.

Suppose that
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

is
\mu_{L}
-stable.
Then, there exists a Hermitian–Einstein metric
h_{\mathop{\rm HE}\nolimits}

of
\big(E,\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

with respect to the Kähler form
\omega_{\epsilon}

\big(\epsilon:=m^{-1}\big)

satisfying the following conditions:

(i)

h_{\mathop{\rm HE}\nolimits}
and
h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}
are mutually bounded.

(ii)

\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big(h_{\mathop{\rm HE}\nolimits}\cdot h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}^{-1}\big)

is
L^{2}

with respect to
h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}
and
\omega_{\epsilon}
.

(iii)

\det(h_{\mathop{\rm HE}\nolimits})=\det(h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits})

holds. In particular, the following holds:

\displaystyle\frac{1}{1+|\lambda|^{2}}\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big(G(h_{\mathop{\rm HE}\nolimits})\big)=\frac{1}{1+|\lambda|^{2}}\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big(G(h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits})\big)=\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big(R(h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits})\big).

(iv)

The following equality holds:

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}\bigg)^{2}\frac{1}{(1+|\lambda|^{2})^{2}}\int_{X\setminus H}\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\bigl(G(h_{\mathop{\rm HE}\nolimits})^{2}\bigr)=2\int_{X}\mathop{\rm ch}\nolimits_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}).

(5.2)

5.2
Around cross points

Let
X_{0}:=\bigl\{(z_{1},z_{2})\in{\mathbb{C}}^{2}\mid|z_{i}|<1\bigr\}
. We set

H_{i}:=X_{0}\cap\{z_{i}=0\}
and
H:=H_{1}\cup H_{2}
.
Let
(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\theta)

be a good filtered Higgs bundle on
(X_{0},H)
. We choose

b_{i}\in{\mathcal{P}{\rm ar}}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},i)

(i=1,2)
,
and set
{\boldsymbol{b}}=(b_{1},b_{2})
. We also choose any Hermitian metric

h_{\det(E)}
of
\det(E)

such that

h_{\det(E)}|z_{1}|^{2b_{1}}|z_{2}|^{2b_{2}}
is a Hermitian metric
of
\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{b}}}(\det\mathcal{V})
of
C^{\infty}
-class.

5.2.1
Unramified case

Suppose that
(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\theta)

satisfies the following condition:

Condition 5.8
.

•

There exists
{\boldsymbol{c}}=(c_{1},c_{2})\in{\mathbb{R}}^{2}

such that

(i)

-1<c_{i}\leq 0
,

(ii)

{\mathcal{P}{\rm ar}}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},i)=\{c_{i}+n\mid n\in{\mathbb{Z}}\}
.

•

There exists a decomposition of good filtered Higgs bundles

\displaystyle(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\theta)=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}}\bigoplus_{{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}\in{\mathbb{C}}^{2}}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{a},{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}},\theta_{\mathfrak{a},{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}})

(5.3)

such that

\theta_{\mathfrak{a},\alpha}-\big({\rm d}\mathfrak{a}+\sum\alpha_{i}{\rm d}z_{i}/z_{i}\big)\mathop{\rm id}\nolimits_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{a},{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}}

induce holomorphic Higgs fields
of
\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{c}}}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{a},{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}}
.

We take any holomorphic frame

{\boldsymbol{v}}=(v_{j})

of
\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{c}}}\mathcal{V}

compatible with the decomposition (
5.3
).
For
j=1,\ldots,r
,
we obtain
(\mathfrak{a}_{j},{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{j})

determined by

v_{j}\in\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{c}}}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{a}_{j},{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{j}}
.
Let
h_{0}
be the metric of

\mathcal{V}_{|X_{0}\setminus H}

determined by

h_{0}(v_{i},v_{i})=|z_{1}|^{-2c_{1}}|z_{2}|^{-2c_{2}}

and

h_{0}(v_{i},v_{j})=0

(i\neq j)
.
Note that

\partial_{h_{0}}{\boldsymbol{v}}={\boldsymbol{v}}\bigl(-\sum_{k=1,2}c_{k}{\rm d}z_{k}/z_{k}\bigr)\,I
,
where
I
denotes the identity matrix.
Hence,

\big[\partial_{h_{0}},\overline{\partial}\,\big]=0
holds. We obtain the description

\theta{\boldsymbol{v}}={\boldsymbol{v}}\bigl(\Lambda_{0}+\Lambda_{1}\bigr)

such that the following holds:

•

(\Lambda_{0})_{ii}=({\rm d}\mathfrak{a}_{i}+\sum_{k=1,2}\alpha_{ik}{\rm d}z_{k}/z_{k})

and

(\Lambda_{0})_{ij}=0

(i\neq j)
.

•

(\Lambda_{1})_{ij}
are holomorphic
1
-forms
for any
i
and
j
.
Moreover,

(\Lambda_{1})_{ij}=0
holds
unless
(\mathfrak{a}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{i})=(\mathfrak{a}_{j},{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{j})
.

We obtain

\theta^{\dagger}_{h_{0}}{\boldsymbol{v}}={\boldsymbol{v}}\big(\overline{\Lambda}_{0}+{}^{t}\!\overline{\Lambda}_{1}\big)

and

\big[\theta,\theta_{h_{0}}^{\dagger}\big]{\boldsymbol{v}}={\boldsymbol{v}}\big[\Lambda_{1},{}^{t}\!\overline{\Lambda}_{1}\big]
,
where the entries of

\big[\Lambda_{1},{}^{t}\!\overline{\Lambda}_{1}\big]

are
C^{\infty}
on
X_{0}
. We have

(\partial_{h_{0}}\theta){\boldsymbol{v}}={\boldsymbol{v}}(\partial\Lambda_{1})
,
where
any entries of
\partial\Lambda_{1}
are holomorphic
2
-forms,
and
(\partial\Lambda_{1})_{ij}=0
unless

(\mathfrak{a}_{i},{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{i})=(\mathfrak{a}_{j},{\boldsymbol{\alpha}}_{j})
.

Note that there exists a
C^{\infty}
-function
u
on
X_{0}

such that

\det(h_{0})={\rm e}^{u}h_{\det(E)}
. We set

h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}:=h_{0}{\rm e}^{-u/\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits E}
.

Lemma 5.9
.

\big[\theta,\theta_{h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}}^{\dagger}\big]
,

\partial_{h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}}\theta
and
\overline{\partial}\theta_{h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}}^{\dagger}

are bounded with respect to
h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}

and

\sum_{k=1,2}{\rm d}z_{k}\,{\rm d}\overline{z}_{k}
.

5.2.2
Ramified case

Let
\varphi\colon{\mathbb{C}}^{2}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{2}

be given by
\varphi(\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2})=(\zeta_{1}^{m},\zeta_{2}^{m})
. We set
X_{0}^{\prime}:=\varphi^{-1}(X_{0})
,

H_{i}^{\prime}:=X_{0}^{\prime}\cap\varphi^{-1}(H_{i})

and
H^{\prime}:=H_{1}^{\prime}\cup H_{2}^{\prime}
. We set

\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(\varphi):=\{(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2})\in{\mathbb{C}}^{2}\mid\kappa_{i}^{m}=1\}
,
which acts on
X^{\prime}_{0}
by

(\kappa_{1},\kappa_{2})(\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2})=(\kappa_{1}\zeta_{1},\kappa_{2}\zeta_{2})
.

Suppose that

\varphi^{\ast}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\theta)

satisfies Condition
5.8

on
(X^{\prime},H^{\prime})
. We construct a
C^{\infty}
-metric
h_{0}^{\prime}

of
\varphi^{\ast}(E)_{|X_{0}^{\prime}\setminus H^{\prime}_{0}}

as in the previous subsection. We may assume that
h_{0}^{\prime}
is

\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(\varphi)
-invariant.
Note that there exists a
\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(\varphi)
-invariant

C^{\infty}
-function
u
on
X_{0}^{\prime}

such that

\det(h_{0}^{\prime})={\rm e}^{u}\varphi^{-1}(h_{\det(E)})
. We set

h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}^{\prime}:=h_{0}^{\prime}{\rm e}^{-u/\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(E)}
.
Because it is
\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(\varphi)
-invariant,
we obtain the induced metric
h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}
of
E
.

Let
g_{X_{0}^{\prime}}
denote the Kähler metric

\sum_{k=1,2}{\rm d}\zeta_{k}\,{\rm d}\overline{\zeta}_{k}
on
X_{0}^{\prime}
.
Because
\varphi\colon X_{0}^{\prime}\setminus H^{\prime}\longrightarrow X_{0}\setminus H

is a covering map,
it induces a Kähler metric

\varphi_{\ast}(g_{X_{0}^{\prime}})

of
X_{0}\setminus H
.

Lemma 5.10
.

\bigl[\theta,\theta_{h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}}^{\dagger}\bigr]
,

\partial_{h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}}\theta
and
\overline{\partial}\theta_{h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}}^{\dagger}

are bounded with respect to

\big(h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits},\varphi_{\ast}g_{X_{0}^{\prime}}\big)
.

5.2.3
An estimate

We set

Y(\epsilon):=\bigl\{(z_{1},z_{2})\in X_{0}\mid\min(|z_{i}|)=\epsilon\bigr\}
.

Lemma 5.11
.

We obtain

\lim\limits_{\epsilon\to 0}\int_{Y(\epsilon)}\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big(\theta\overline{\partial}\theta^{\dagger}\big)=0

and

\lim\limits_{\epsilon\to 0}\int_{Y(\epsilon)}\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big(\theta^{\dagger}\partial\theta\big)=0
.

Proof.

It is enough to consider the case
where Condition
5.8

is satisfied for
(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\theta)
.
Let
f
be any anti-holomorphic function on
X_{0}
.
Let us consider
