\int_{Y(\epsilon)}{\rm d}\mathfrak{a}\,f\,{\rm d}\overline{z}_{1}{\rm d}\overline{z}_{2}
. We set

Y_{1}(\epsilon):=\{|z_{1}|=\epsilon,|z_{2}|\geq\epsilon\}

and

Y_{2}(\epsilon):=\{|z_{2}|=\epsilon,|z_{1}|\geq\epsilon\}
. We have

\displaystyle\int_{Y_{1}(\epsilon)}{\rm d}\mathfrak{a}f\,{\rm d}\overline{z}_{1}{\rm d}\overline{z}_{2}=\int_{Y_{1}(\epsilon)}\partial_{2}\mathfrak{a}{\rm d}z_{2}f{\rm d}\overline{z}_{1}{\rm d}\overline{z}_{2}.

It is of the form

\displaystyle\int_{Y_{1}(\epsilon)}\frac{\mathfrak{b}(z_{1},z_{2})}{z_{1}^{\ell_{1}}z_{2}^{\ell_{2}}}f(\overline{z}_{1},\overline{z}_{2}){\rm d}\overline{z}_{1}{\rm d}\overline{z}_{2}.

(5.4)

Here,
\mathfrak{b}
is a holomorphic function. We consider the Taylor expansion of

\mathfrak{b}
and
f
.
Then, the contributions of the terms

\displaystyle\frac{z_{1}^{k_{1}}\overline{z}_{1}^{m_{1}}}{z_{1}^{\ell_{1}}}\,{\rm d}\overline{z}_{1}\,\frac{z_{2}^{k_{2}}}{z_{2}^{\ell_{2}}}\,\overline{z}_{2}^{m_{2}}\,{\rm d}\overline{z}_{2}\,{\rm d}z_{2}

to (
5.4
) is
0

unless
k_{1}-\ell_{1}-m_{1}=1

and
k_{2}-\ell_{2}-m_{2}=0
.
If the equalities hold,
we have
k_{1}-\ell_{1}+m_{1}=2m_{1}+1\geq 1

and

k_{2}-\ell_{2}+m_{2}=2m_{2}\geq 0
.
Hence, we obtain

\lim\limits_{\epsilon\to 0}\int_{Y_{1}(\epsilon)}{\rm d}\mathfrak{a}f\,{\rm d}\overline{z}_{1}{\rm d}\overline{z}_{2}\allowbreak=0
.
Similarly, we obtain

\lim\limits_{\epsilon\to 0}\int_{Y_{2}(\epsilon)}{\rm d}\mathfrak{a}f\,{\rm d}\overline{z}_{1}{\rm d}\overline{z}_{2}=0
.
Similarly and more easily,
we obtain

\lim\limits_{\epsilon\to 0}\int_{Y(\epsilon)}(\alpha_{i}{\rm d}z_{i}/z_{i})f{\rm d}\overline{z}_{1}{\rm d}\overline{z}_{2}=0
.
Then, the claim of the lemma follows.
∎

5.3
Around smooth points

We set
X_{0}:=\bigl\{(z_{1},z_{2})\in{\mathbb{C}}^{2}\mid|z_{i}|<1\bigr\}

and
H:=\{z_{1}=0\}
.
Let
\nu\colon X_{0}\setminus H\longrightarrow{\mathbb{R}}_{>0}

be a
C^{\infty}
-function
such that

\nu|z_{1}|^{-1}
induces
a nowhere vanishing
C^{\infty}
-function on
X_{0}
.
Let
(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\theta)

be a good filtered Higgs bundle on
(X_{0},H)
.
Let
\big(E,\overline{\partial}_{E},\theta\big)
be the Higgs bundle
obtained as the restriction of
(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\theta)

to
X_{0}\setminus H
. We choose
b\in{\mathcal{P}{\rm ar}}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\det\mathcal{V})

and a Hermitian metric
h_{\det(E)}
of

\det(E)
such that

h_{\det(E)}\nu^{2b}
induces a
C^{\infty}
metric of

\mathcal{P}_{b}(\det\mathcal{V})
.

5.3.1
Unramified case

Suppose that
(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\theta)

satisfies Condition
5.12
.

Condition 5.12
.

•

There exists
-1<c\leq 0
such that

{\mathcal{P}{\rm ar}}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})=\{c+n\mid n\in{\mathbb{Z}}\}
.

•

There exists a decomposition of good filtered Higgs bundles

\displaystyle(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\theta)=\bigoplus_{\mathfrak{a}\in\mathcal{I}}\bigoplus_{\alpha\in{\mathbb{C}}}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{a},\alpha},\theta_{\mathfrak{a},\alpha}).

•

\theta_{\mathfrak{a},\alpha}-({\rm d}\mathfrak{a}+\alpha{\rm d}z_{1}/z_{1})\mathop{\rm id}\nolimits_{\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{a},\alpha}}

are holomorphic Higgs fields of

\mathcal{P}_{c}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{a},\alpha}
.

We take
C^{\infty}
-metrics

h_{\mathfrak{a},\alpha}

of
\mathcal{P}_{c}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{a},\alpha}
,
and we set

h_{0}:=\bigoplus\nu^{-2c}h_{\mathfrak{a},\alpha}
. We may assume that

\det(h_{0})=h_{\det(E)}
.

Let
{\boldsymbol{v}}=(v_{1},\ldots,v_{r})
be any holomorphic frame of

\mathcal{P}_{c}\mathcal{V}
compatible with
the decomposition.
For each
i
,

\mathfrak{a}_{i}
and
\alpha_{i}

are determined by the condition that

v_{i}
is a section of

\mathcal{P}_{c}\mathcal{V}_{\mathfrak{a}_{i},\alpha_{i}}
.
There exist matrix valued
C^{\infty}
-
(1,0)
-forms

A_{\mathfrak{a},\alpha}

such that

\displaystyle\partial_{h_{0}}{\boldsymbol{v}}={\boldsymbol{v}}\Bigl((-c\cdot\partial\log\nu^{2})I+\sum A_{\mathfrak{a},\alpha}\Bigr),

where
I
denotes the identity matrix,
and
(A_{\mathfrak{a},\alpha})_{i,j}=0

unless
(\mathfrak{a}_{i},\alpha_{i})=(\mathfrak{a}_{j},\alpha_{j})=(\mathfrak{a},\alpha)
.
Let
\Lambda
denote the matrix valued holomorphic
1
-form
determined by

\theta{\boldsymbol{v}}={\boldsymbol{v}}\Lambda
.
There exists the decomposition

\Lambda=\Lambda_{0}+\Lambda_{1}

such that the following holds:

•

(\Lambda_{0})_{ij}=({\rm d}\mathfrak{a}_{i}+\alpha_{i}{\rm d}z_{1}/z_{1})

if
i=j
,
and

(\Lambda_{0})_{ij}=0
if
i\neq j
.

•

(\Lambda_{1})_{ij}
are holomorphic
1
-forms,
and
(\Lambda_{1})_{ij}=0

unless
(\mathfrak{a}_{i},\alpha_{i})=(\mathfrak{a}_{j},\alpha_{j})
.

There exists
a matrix valued
C^{\infty}

(0,1)
-form
\Lambda_{2}

such that

\theta_{h_{0}}^{\dagger}{\boldsymbol{v}}={\boldsymbol{v}}\big(\overline{\Lambda}_{0}+\Lambda_{2}\big)
.
Moreover,

(\Lambda_{2})_{ij}=0
holds unless

(\mathfrak{a}_{i},\alpha_{i})=(\mathfrak{a}_{j},\alpha_{j})
.

We have

R(h_{0})=\big({-}c\overline{\partial}\partial\log\sigma^{2}\big)I+\bigoplus R(h_{\mathfrak{a},\alpha})
,
where
R(h_{\mathfrak{a},\alpha})

are
C^{\infty}
.
Note that

d\Lambda_{0}=0

and

[\Lambda_{0},\Lambda_{i}]=\big[\Lambda_{0},\overline{\Lambda}_{i}\big]=0
.
Hence,

[\theta,\theta_{h_{0}}^{\dagger}]
,

\partial_{h_{0}}\theta
and

\overline{\partial}\theta_{h_{0}}^{\dagger}

are
C^{\infty}
. We also have

\displaystyle\bigl(\partial_{h_{0}}\theta\bigr){\boldsymbol{v}}={\boldsymbol{v}}\Bigl(\partial\Lambda_{1}+\Bigl[\bigoplus A_{\mathfrak{a},\alpha},\Lambda_{1}\Bigr]\Bigr),\qquad\bigl(\overline{\partial}\theta_{h_{0}}^{\dagger}\bigr){\boldsymbol{v}}={\boldsymbol{v}}\big(\overline{\partial}\Lambda_{2}\big).

We set

w_{1}=z_{1}|z_{1}|^{-1}\nu

and
w_{2}=z_{2}
.
Then, it is easy to check that

(w_{1},w_{2})
is a
C^{\infty}
complex coordinate system.
Clearly,

{\rm d}\overline{z}_{2}={\rm d}\overline{w}_{2}
.
There exists a
C^{\infty}
-function

\gamma
and a
C^{\infty}

(0,1)
-form
\kappa

such that

{\rm d}\overline{z}_{1}=\gamma{\rm d}\overline{w}_{1}+\overline{w}_{1}\kappa_{1}
. We set
Y(\epsilon)=\{\nu=\epsilon\}=\{|w_{1}|=\epsilon\}
.

Lemma 5.13
.

\lim\limits_{\epsilon\to 0}\int_{Y(\epsilon)}\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big(\theta\overline{\partial}\theta^{\dagger}\big)=0

and

\lim\limits_{\epsilon\to 0}\int_{Y(\epsilon)}\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big(\theta^{\dagger}\partial\theta\big)=0

hold.

Proof.

It is enough to prove

\lim\limits_{\epsilon\to 0}\int_{Y(\epsilon)}\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big(\theta\overline{\partial}\theta^{\dagger}\big)=0
.
It is easy to see that

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\int_{Y(\epsilon)}\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big(\Lambda_{1}\overline{\partial}\Lambda_{2}\big)=0.

Let us study

\int_{Y(\epsilon)}\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big(\Lambda_{0}\overline{\partial}\Lambda_{2}\big)
.
For any
C^{\infty}
-function
g
,
we consider the following integral:

\displaystyle\int_{Y(\epsilon)}g({\rm d}\mathfrak{a}\cdot{\rm d}\overline{z}_{1}\,{\rm d}\overline{z}_{2})=\int_{Y(\epsilon)}(g\gamma)\cdot{\rm d}\mathfrak{a}\,{\rm d}\overline{w}_{1}\,{\rm d}\overline{w}_{2}+\int_{Y(\epsilon)}g\overline{w}_{1}\cdot{\rm d}\mathfrak{a}\,\kappa\,{\rm d}\overline{w}_{2}.

(5.5)

We can rewrite the first term in the right hand side of
(
5.5
) as follows,
for some non-negative integer
\ell

and for a
C^{\infty}
-function
\mathfrak{b}
:

\displaystyle\int_{Y(\epsilon)}(g\gamma)\,{\rm d}\mathfrak{a}\,{\rm d}\overline{w}_{1}\,{\rm d}\overline{w}_{2}=\int_{Y(\epsilon)}(g\gamma\mathfrak{b})w_{1}^{-\ell}\,{\rm d}\overline{w}_{1}\,{\rm d}w_{2}\,{\rm d}\overline{w}_{2}

Take
N>\ell
. We consider the expansion

\displaystyle g\gamma\mathfrak{b}=\sum_{\begin{subarray}{c}k,m\geq 0\\
k+m\leq N\end{subarray}}(g\gamma\mathfrak{b})_{k,m}(w_{2})w_{1}^{k}\overline{w}_{1}^{m}+O\big(|w_{1}|^{N}\big).

Here,

(g\gamma\mathfrak{b})_{k,m}(w_{2})
are
C^{\infty}
-functions of
w_{2}
.
The contributions

\displaystyle\int_{Y(\epsilon)}(g\gamma\mathfrak{b})_{k,m}(w_{2})\frac{w_{1}^{k}\overline{w}_{1}^{m}}{w_{1}^{\ell}}{\rm d}\overline{w}_{1}\,{\rm d}w_{2}\,{\rm d}\overline{w}_{2}

are
0

unless
k-\ell-m=1
.
If
k-\ell-m=1
,
then
k-\ell+m=2m+1\geq 1
holds.
Hence, we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\int_{Y(\epsilon)}(g\gamma)\,{\rm d}\mathfrak{a}\,{\rm d}\overline{w}_{1}{\rm d}\overline{w}_{2}=0.

We rewrite the second term in
the right hand side of (
5.5
)
as follows,
for some
C^{\infty}
-functions
f_{i}

(i=1,2)

and a non-negative integer
\ell
:

\displaystyle\int_{Y(\epsilon)}g\overline{w}_{1}\cdot{\rm d}\mathfrak{a}\,\kappa\,{\rm d}\overline{w}_{2}=\int_{Y(\epsilon)}f_{1}w_{1}^{-\ell-1}\overline{w}_{1}\,\,{\rm d}w_{1}\,{\rm d}w_{2}\,{\rm d}\overline{w}_{2}+\int_{Y(\epsilon)}f_{2}w_{1}^{-\ell}\,\overline{w}_{1}\,{\rm d}\overline{w}_{1}\,{\rm d}w_{2}\,{\rm d}\overline{w}_{2}.

Take
N>\ell+1
.
Consider the expansions

f_{i}=\sum(f_{i})_{k,m}(w_{2})\,w_{1}^{k}\overline{w}_{1}^{m}+O\big(|w_{1}|^{N}\big)
.
The contributions

\displaystyle\int_{Y(\epsilon)}(f_{1})_{k,m}(w_{2})\,\frac{w_{1}^{k}\overline{w}_{1}^{m+1}}{w_{1}^{\ell+1}}\,{\rm d}w_{1}\,{\rm d}w_{2}\,{\rm d}\overline{w}_{2}

are
0

unless
k-(\ell+1)-(m+1)=-1
.
If
k-(\ell+1)-(m+1)=-1
holds,
then we have

k-(\ell+1)+(m+1)=2m+1\geq 1
.
The contributions

\displaystyle\int_{Y(\epsilon)}(f_{2})_{k,m}(w_{2})\,\frac{w_{1}^{k}\overline{w}_{1}^{m+1}}{w_{1}^{\ell}}\,{\rm d}\overline{w}_{1}\,{\rm d}w_{2}\,{\rm d}\overline{w}_{2}

are
0
unless

k-\ell-(m+1)=1
.
If
k-\ell-(m+1)=1
holds,
then we have

k-\ell+(m+1)=2(m+1)+1\geq 3
.
Hence, we obtain

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\int_{Y(\epsilon)}g\overline{w}_{1}\cdot{\rm d}\mathfrak{a}\,\kappa\,{\rm d}\overline{w}_{2}=0.

Similarly and more easily,
we obtain

\lim\limits_{\epsilon\to 0}\int_{Y(\epsilon)}g(\alpha{\rm d}z_{1}/z_{1})\,{\rm d}\overline{z}_{1}{\rm d}\overline{z}_{2}=0

for any
\alpha\in{\mathbb{C}}

and for any
C^{\infty}
-function
g
.
Thus, we obtain the claim of the lemma.
∎

5.3.2
Ramified case

Let
\varphi\colon{\mathbb{C}}^{2}\longrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{2}

be given by

\varphi(\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2})=\big(\zeta_{1}^{m},\zeta_{2}\big)
. We set

X_{0}^{\prime}:=\varphi^{-1}(X_{0})

and
H^{\prime}:=\varphi^{-1}(H)
.
Let
\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(\varphi):=\{\mu\in{\mathbb{C}}\mid\mu^{m}=1\}
,
which acts on
X_{0}^{\prime}

by
\mu\cdot(\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2})=(\mu\zeta_{1},\zeta_{2})
.

Suppose that

\varphi^{\ast}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\theta)

satisfies Condition
5.12
. We construct a Hermitian metric
h_{0}^{\prime}

for
\varphi^{\ast}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\theta)

as in the previous subsection. We may assume that

h_{0}^{\prime}
is
\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(\varphi)
-invariant.
There exists a
C^{\infty}
-function

f
on
X_{0}^{\prime}

determined by

\det(h_{0}^{\prime})=e^{f}\varphi^{-1}(h_{\det(E)})
. We set

h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}^{\prime}:=h_{0}^{\prime}e^{-f/\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(E)}
.
Because
h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits^{\prime}}
is
\mathop{\rm Gal}\nolimits(\varphi)
-invariant,
we obtain a Hermitian metric
h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}
of
E

induced by
h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}^{\prime}
.
Let
\varphi_{\ast}(g_{X_{0}^{\prime}})

denote the Kähler metric of

X_{0}\setminus H_{0}

induced by
\sum_{k=1,2}{\rm d}\zeta_{k}\,{\rm d}\overline{\zeta}_{k}
.

Lemma 5.14
.

R(h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits})
,

\big[\theta,\theta_{h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}}^{\dagger}\big]
,

\partial_{h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}}\theta

and
\overline{\partial}\theta_{h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}}^{\dagger}

are bounded with respect to

\varphi_{\ast}g_{X^{\prime}_{0}}
and
h_{0}
. We also have

\displaystyle\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\int_{Y(\epsilon)}\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big(\theta\overline{\partial}\theta_{h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}}^{\dagger}\big)=0,\qquad\lim_{\epsilon\to 0}\int_{Y(\epsilon)}\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big(\theta^{\dagger}\partial_{h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}}\theta\big)=0.

5.4
Proof of Proposition
5.6

Let
X
,
H
and
L
be as in Section
5.1.1
.
Let
(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\theta)

be a good filtered Higgs bundle on
(X,H)

satisfying Condition
5.1
.
Note that

(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\theta)

is as in Section
5.2.2

around any cross point of
H
,
and

(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\theta)

is as in Section
5.3.2

around any smooth points of
H
.
There exists a Hermitian metric
h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}
of
E

such that

(i)

\det(h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits})=h_{\det(E)}
,

(ii)
the restriction of
h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}
around any points of
H

are as in Section
5.2.2

or Section
5.3.2
. By the construction,

h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}
is strongly adapted to

\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}
. By Lemmas
5.10

and
5.14
,
we obtain that

R(h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits})
,

\big[\theta,\theta_{h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}}^{\dagger}\big]
,

\partial_{h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}}\theta

and
\overline{\partial}\theta_{h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}}^{\dagger}

are bounded with respect to

h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}
and
\omega_{\epsilon}
.
As in the proof of
[
46
, Proposition 4.18]
,
we have

\displaystyle\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\bigl(G(h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits})^{2}\bigr)=\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\bigl(R(h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits})^{2}\bigr)+{\rm d}\bigl(\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big(\theta\overline{\partial}\theta_{h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}}^{\dagger}\big)+\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\big(\theta_{h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}}^{\dagger}\partial_{h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}}\theta\big)\bigr).

Then,
we obtain (
5.1
)
from Lemmas
5.11
and
5.14
.
Thus, we obtain Proposition
5.6
.
∎

5.5
Proof of Theorem
5.7

Let
E^{\prime}\subset E
be
any coherent
\lambda
-flat
\mathcal{O}_{X\setminus H}
-subsheaf. We assume that

E^{\prime}
is saturated,
i.e.,
E/E^{\prime}
is torsion-free.
Let
\big(E^{\prime},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{E^{\prime}}\big)
be the induced
\lambda
-flat sheaf
on
X\setminus H
.
There exists a discrete subset
Z\subset X\setminus H

such that

E^{\prime}_{|X\setminus(H\cup Z)}
is a subbundle of

E_{|X\setminus(H\cup Z)}
.
Let
h^{\prime}
denote the metric of
E^{\prime}_{|X\setminus(H\cup Z)}

induced by
h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}
. We obtain the Chern connection

\nabla_{h^{\prime}}
of
\big(E^{\prime},d^{\prime\prime}_{E^{\prime}},h^{\prime}\big)

and the operator

\mathbb{D}^{\lambda\star}_{E^{\prime},h^{\prime}}

from
\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{E^{\prime}}
and
h^{\prime}
.
Let
R(E^{\prime},h^{\prime})
denote the curvature of
\nabla_{h^{\prime}}
. We obtain

G(E^{\prime},h^{\prime}):=\bigl[\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{E^{\prime}},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda\star}_{E^{\prime},h^{\prime}}\bigr]
.
Following
[
62
]
, we define

\displaystyle\deg_{\omega_{\epsilon}}(E^{\prime},h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}):=\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}\frac{1}{1+|\lambda|^{2}}\int_{X\setminus H}\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\bigl(\Lambda_{\omega_{\epsilon}}G(E^{\prime},\theta^{\prime},h^{\prime})\bigr)\mathop{\rm dvol}\nolimits_{\omega_{\epsilon}}.

It is well defined in
{\mathbb{R}}\cup\{-\infty\}

by the Chern–Weil formula
[
62
, Lemma 3.2]
:

\displaystyle\deg_{\omega_{\epsilon}}(E^{\prime},h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits})=\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}\frac{1}{1+|\lambda|^{2}}\int_{X\setminus H}\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\bigl(\pi_{E^{\prime}}\Lambda_{\omega_{\epsilon}}G(h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits})\bigr)-\frac{1}{2\pi}\frac{1}{1+|\lambda|^{2}}\int_{X\setminus H}\bigl|\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\pi_{E^{\prime}}\bigr|^{2}_{h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits},\omega_{\epsilon}}.

Here,
\pi_{E^{\prime}}
denotes the orthogonal projection
of
E_{|X\setminus(H\cup Z)}
onto
E^{\prime}_{|X\setminus(H\cup Z)}
.

Lemma 5.15
.

If
\deg_{\omega_{\epsilon}}(E^{\prime},\theta)\neq-\infty
,
then

E^{\prime}
extends to a filtered subsheaf

\mathcal{P}^{h^{\prime}}_{\ast}E^{\prime}
of
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}

and

\displaystyle\deg_{\omega_{\epsilon}}(E^{\prime},h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits})=\int_{X}c_{1}\big(\mathcal{P}^{h^{\prime}}_{\ast}E^{\prime}\big)\omega_{X}

holds. As a result,

\big(E,\overline{\partial}_{E},\theta,h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}\big)

is analytically stable
in the sense of
[
62
]

(
see also
[
49
, Section 2.3]
)
.

Proof.

If
\deg_{\omega_{\epsilon}}(E^{\prime},h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits})\neq-\infty
,
we obtain

\int|d^{\prime\prime}\pi_{E^{\prime}}|^{2}<\infty
.
As studied in
[
37
,
38
]

on the basis of
[
68
]
,
we obtain a coherent

\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H)
-submodule

\mathcal{P}^{h^{\prime}}(E^{\prime})\subset\mathcal{V}

as an extension of
E^{\prime}
.
Moreover, as proved in
[
46
, Lemma 4.20]
,
we obtain the equality

\deg_{\omega_{\epsilon}}(E^{\prime},h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits})=\int_{X}c_{1}\big(\mathcal{P}^{h^{\prime}}_{\ast}E^{\prime}\big)\omega_{X}
.
∎

According to the fundamental theorem of Simpson
[
62
, Theorem 1]

and its variant
[
49
, Proposition 2.49]
,
there exists a Hermitian–Einstein metric
h_{\mathop{\rm HE}\nolimits}

of
\big(E,\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

satisfying the conditions
(i)
,
(ii)
and
(iii)
. By
[
62
, Proposition 3.5]

and
[
62
, Lemma 7.4]

(see also
[
49
, Proposition 2.49]
), we obtain

\displaystyle\bigg(\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}\bigg)^{2}\frac{1}{(1+|\lambda|^{2})^{2}}\int_{X\setminus H}\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\bigl(G(h_{\mathop{\rm HE}\nolimits})^{2}\bigr)=\bigg(\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}\bigg)^{2}\frac{1}{(1+|\lambda|^{2})^{2}}\int_{X\setminus H}\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits\bigl(G(h_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits})^{2}\bigr).

It is equal to

2\int_{X}\mathop{\rm ch}\nolimits_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})

by Lemma
5.5

and Proposition
5.6
.
Thus, Theorem
5.7

is proved.
∎

6
Bogomolov–Gieseker inequality

Let
X
be any dimensional smooth connected projective variety
with a simple normal crossing hypersurface

H=\bigcup_{i\in\Lambda}H_{i}
.
Let
L
be any ample line bundle on
X
.

Theorem 6.1
.

Let
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

be a
\mu_{L}
-polystable good filtered
\lambda
-flat bundle on
(X,H)
.
Then, the Bogomolov–Gieseker inequality holds:

\displaystyle\int_{X}\mathop{\rm ch}\nolimits_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})c_{1}(L)^{\dim X-2}\leq\frac{\int_{X}c_{1}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})^{2}c_{1}(L)^{\dim X-2}}{2\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits\mathcal{V}}.

Proof.

By the Mehta–Ramanathan type theorem
(Proposition
3.8
),
it is enough to study the case
\dim X=2
,
which we shall assume in the rest of the proof. We use the notation in Section
3.7.2
.
Let

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)=\bigoplus\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{j},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{j}\big)

be the decomposition
into the stable components.

We set
e:=\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(\mathcal{V})!
. We choose
\eta>0
such that

0<10e\eta<\widetilde{\mathop{\rm gap}\nolimits}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})
. We take
{\boldsymbol{a}}\in{\mathbb{R}}^{\Lambda}

for
\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}{\rm ar}}}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},i)

(i\in\Lambda)

as in Lemma
3.19
.

Let
m\in e{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}

such that
\epsilon:=m^{-1}<\eta/10\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits(\mathcal{V})
.
For any
b\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}{\rm ar}}}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},i)
, we set

b(\epsilon):=\max\bigl\{d\in\epsilon{\mathbb{Z}}\mid d<b\bigr\}
. We set

\displaystyle c_{i,j}:=\frac{1}{\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits\mathcal{V}_{j}}\sum_{b\in{\mathcal{P}{\rm ar}}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{j},{\boldsymbol{a}},i)}(b-b(\epsilon))\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits{}^{i}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{b}(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V}_{j}).

We have
0\leq c_{i,j}\leq\epsilon
.
For any
b\in\widetilde{{\mathcal{P}{\rm ar}}}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{j},i)
,
we set

\psi_{\epsilon,i,j}(b):=b(\epsilon)+c_{i,j}
.
Then,
we obtain

|\psi_{\epsilon,i,j}(b)-b|<2\epsilon

and the following equalities:

\displaystyle\sum_{b\in{\mathcal{P}{\rm ar}}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{j},{\boldsymbol{a}},i)}\psi_{\epsilon,i,j}(b)\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits{}^{i}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{b}(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V}_{j})=\sum_{b\in{\mathcal{P}{\rm ar}}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{j},{\boldsymbol{a}},i)}b\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits{}^{i}\!\mathop{\rm Gr}\nolimits^{F}_{b}(\mathcal{P}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}\mathcal{V}_{j}).

Moreover, we have

\psi_{\epsilon,i,j}(b)-c_{i,j}\in\epsilon{\mathbb{Z}}
.

Applying the construction in Section
3.7.2
,
we obtain good filtered
\lambda
-flat bundles

\big(\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{j},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{j}\big)

on
(X,H)
. By the construction, they satisfy
Condition
5.1
. By Lemma
3.25
,
there exists
m_{0}
such that

\big(\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{j},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{j}\big)

are
\mu_{L}
-stable if
m\geq m_{0}
.
Let
\big(E_{j},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{j}\big)
be the
\lambda
-flat bundle
obtained as the restriction of

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{j},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{j}\big)
to
X\setminus H
. We use the Kähler metric
g_{\epsilon}

of
X\setminus H

as in Section
5.1.1
.
There exist Hermitian–Einstein metrics

h^{(\epsilon)}_{j,\mathop{\rm HE}\nolimits}

of the
\lambda
-flat bundles
\big(E_{j},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{j}\big)

as in Theorem
5.7

for the good filtered
\lambda
-flat bundles

\big(\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{j},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{j}\big)
.
Note that

\bigoplus h^{(\epsilon)}_{j,\mathop{\rm HE}\nolimits}

is a Hermitian–Einstein metric of

\bigoplus\big(E_{j},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{j}\big)
.

By Proposition
3.1
,
the equality (
5.2
),
and the equality

\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}\frac{1}{1+|\lambda|^{2}}\mathop{\rm Tr}\nolimits G\big(h^{(\epsilon)}_{\mathop{\rm HE}\nolimits}\big)=\frac{\sqrt{-1}}{2\pi}R(h_{\det E})
,
we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{X}\mathop{\rm ch}\nolimits_{2}\big(\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}\big)\leq\frac{\int_{X}c_{1}\big(\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}\big)^{2}}{2\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits\mathcal{V}}.

By taking the limit as
m\to\infty
, i.e.,
\epsilon\to 0
,
we obtain the desired inequality.
∎

Corollary 6.2
.

Let
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
be
a
\mu_{L}
-polystable good filtered

\lambda
-flat bundle on
(X,H)
.
Suppose that

\displaystyle\int_{X}c_{1}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})c_{1}(L)^{\dim X-1}=0,\qquad\int_{X}\mathop{\rm ch}\nolimits_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})c_{1}(L)^{\dim X-2}=0.

Then,
c_{1}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})=0
holds.

Moreover,
for any decomposition

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)=\bigoplus\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{j},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{j}\big)

into
\mu_{L}
-stable good filtered
\lambda
-flat bundles,
we obtain

c_{1}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{j})=0

and

\int_{X}\mathop{\rm ch}\nolimits_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{j})c_{1}(L)^{\dim X-2}=0
.

Proof.

On one hand,
because of the Hodge index theorem
and

\int_{X}c_{1}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})c_{1}(L)^{\dim X-1}=0
,
we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{X}c_{1}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})^{2}c_{1}(L)^{\dim X-2}\leq 0,

and the equality holds if and only if

c_{1}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})=0
.
On the other hand,
by the Bogomolov–Gieseker inequality
and
\int_{X}\mathop{\rm ch}\nolimits_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})c_{1}(L)^{\dim X-2}=0
,
we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{X}c_{1}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})^{2}c_{1}(L)^{\dim X-2}\geq 0.

Hence, we obtain
c_{1}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})=0
.

Let

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)=\bigoplus\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{j},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{j}\big)

be a decomposition
into
\mu_{L}
-stable good filtered
\lambda
-flat bundles. We have

\int_{X}c_{1}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{j})c_{1}(L)^{\dim X-1}=0
.
Hence, by the Hodge index theorem,
we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{X}c_{1}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{j})^{2}c_{1}(L)^{\dim X-2}\leq 0.

By the Bogomolov–Gieseker type inequality,
we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{X}\mathop{\rm ch}\nolimits_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{j})c_{1}(L)^{\dim X-2}\leq 0.

Because

\sum_{j}\int_{X}\mathop{\rm ch}\nolimits_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{j})c_{1}(L)^{\dim X-2}=\int_{X}\mathop{\rm ch}\nolimits_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})=0
,
we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{X}\mathop{\rm ch}\nolimits_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{j})c_{1}(L)^{\dim X-2}=0.

Thus, we obtain the claim of the corollary.
∎

Remark 6.3
.

Although
H
was assumed to be ample in

[
51
, Section 14.4, Corollary 14.5.1]
,
it is not essential.
Indeed,
for any simple normal crossing hypersurface
H
,
there exists an ample simple normal crossing hypersurface
H^{\prime}

such that
H\subset H^{\prime}
.
Let
\big(\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

be the filtered
\lambda
-flat bundle on
(X,H^{\prime})

naturally induced by
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
.
The Chern characters of

\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}

and
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}^{\prime}\mathcal{V}
are equal,
and hence the Bogomolov–Gieseker inequalities for

\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}

and
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}^{\prime}\mathcal{V}
are equivalent.

7
Existence theorem of pluri-harmonic metrics

7.1
Statement

Let us prove Theorem
2.23
.
According to Corollary
6.2
,
it is enough to study the case
where
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

is a
\mu_{L}
-stable
good filtered
\lambda
-flat bundle
on
(X,H)
such that

\displaystyle c_{1}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})=0,\quad\int_{X}\mathop{\rm ch}\nolimits_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})c_{1}(L)^{\dim X-2}=0.

Let
\big(E,\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
be the
\lambda
-flat bundle
obtained as the restriction

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)_{|X\setminus H}
.
Let
h_{\det(E)}
denote the pluri-harmonic metric
of
\big(\det(E),\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{\det(E)}\big)

strongly adapted to
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}(\det(E))
.
For the proof of Theorem
2.23
,
it is enough to prove the following theorem.

Theorem 7.1
.

There exists a unique pluri-harmonic metric
h

of the
\lambda
-flat bundle
\big(E,\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

such that

\mathcal{P}^{h}_{\ast}E=\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}

and
\det(h)=h_{\det(E)}
.

The proof is given in the rest of this section.

7.2
Surface case

Let us study the case

\dim X=2
.
The following argument is essentially
the same as the proof of

[
49
, Theorem 5.5]
.
Let
\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

be as in Section
7.1
. We use the notation in the proof of Theorem
6.1
.
For large
m\in e{\mathbb{Z}}_{>0}
,
we set
\epsilon:=m^{-1}
. We have the perturbations

\big(\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
. We use the Kähler metrics
g_{\epsilon}

of
X\setminus H

as in Section
5.1.1
.
There exist the Hermitian–Einstein metrics

h^{(\epsilon)}_{\mathop{\rm HE}\nolimits}

of
\big(E,\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

adapted to
\big(\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon)}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

such that
\det\big(h^{(\epsilon)}_{\mathop{\rm HE}\nolimits}\big)=h_{\det(E)}
.

Proposition 7.2
.

For any sequence
m_{i}\to\infty
,
we set
\epsilon_{i}:=m_{i}^{-1}
.
Then, after going to a subsequence,

h^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{\mathop{\rm HE}\nolimits}

is convergent almost everywhere on
X\setminus H
,
and the limit
h
is a pluri-harmonic metric of
the
\lambda
-flat bundle
\big(E,\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

adapted to
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}

such that
\det(h)=h_{\det E}
.

7.2.1
Family of ample hypersurfaces

There exists a
0
-dimensional closed subset

Z\subset H
such that

(i)

Z
contains the singular points of
H
,

(ii)
any
P\in H\setminus Z
has
a neighbourhood
H_{P}
in
H

on which the conjugacy classes of
\mathop{\rm Res}\nolimits(\mathbb{D}^{\lambda})_{|Q}

(Q\in H_{P})
are constant.

Take a sufficiently large integer
M
. We set

\mathfrak{Z}_{M}:=H^{0}\big(X,L^{\otimes\,M}\big)\setminus\{0\}
.
It is equipped with a natural
{\mathbb{C}}^{\ast}
-action.
Let
p_{i}
denote the projection of

X\times\mathfrak{Z}_{M}
onto the
i
-th component.
There exists the universal section

\mathfrak{s}
of
p_{1}^{\ast}\big(L^{\otimes M}\big)
.
Let
\mathfrak{X}_{M}
denote the scheme
obtained as
\mathfrak{s}^{-1}(0)
.
Let
\texttt{P}_{1}\colon\mathfrak{X}_{M}\longrightarrow X

and
\texttt{P}_{2}\colon\mathfrak{X}_{M}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{Z}_{M}

denote the morphism induced by
p_{i}
.
For each
s\in\mathfrak{Z}_{M}
,
let
X_{s}
denote the fiber product of

\texttt{P}_{2}

and the inclusion
\{s\}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{Z}_{M}
.

There exists the
{\mathbb{C}}^{\ast}
-invariant
maximal Zariski open subset

\mathfrak{Z}^{\circ}_{M}\subset\mathfrak{Z}_{M}

such that

(i)
the induced morphism

\texttt{P}_{2}^{\circ}\colon\mathfrak{X}_{M}^{\circ}:=\mathfrak{X}_{M}\times_{\mathfrak{Z}_{M}}\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\circ}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\circ}

is smooth,

(ii)

X_{s}\cup H
is normal crossing for any
s\in\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\circ}
,

(iii)

(X_{s}\cap H)\cap Z=\varnothing
.
Let
\texttt{P}_{1}^{\circ}
denote the restriction of

\texttt{P}_{1}
to
\mathfrak{X}^{\circ}_{M}
.
For any
Q\in\mathfrak{X}_{M}^{\circ}
,
we obtain the subspace

T_{\texttt{P}_{1}(Q)}X_{\texttt{P}_{2}(Q)}\subset T_{\texttt{P}_{1}(Q)}X

of codimension
1
.
It determines a point
in
\mathbb{P}\big(T^{\ast}_{\texttt{P}_{1}(Q)}X\big)
.
Hence, we obtain the natural morphism

\widetilde{\texttt{P}}^{\circ}_{1}\colon\mathfrak{X}_{M}^{\circ}\longrightarrow\mathbb{P}(T^{\ast}X)
.
If
M
is sufficiently large,

\texttt{P}_{1}^{\circ}
and
\widetilde{\texttt{P}}_{1}^{\circ}

are surjective.

By the Mehta–Ramanathan type theorem
(Proposition
3.8
),
there exists a non-empty

{\mathbb{C}}^{\ast}
-invariant Zariski open subset

\mathfrak{Z}^{\triangle}_{M}
of
\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\circ}

such that the following holds:

•

For each
s\in\mathfrak{Z}^{\triangle}_{M}
,

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)_{|X_{s}}

is stable.

We set

\mathfrak{X}_{M}^{\triangle}:=\mathfrak{X}_{M}^{\circ}\times_{\mathfrak{Z}^{\circ}_{M}}\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\triangle}
.
Note that

W_{M}:=X\setminus\texttt{P}^{\circ}_{1}\big(\mathfrak{X}_{M}^{\triangle}\big)

is a finite set.
For each
P\in X\setminus(H\cup W_{M})
,
the intersection

\widetilde{\texttt{P}}^{\circ}_{1}\big(\mathfrak{X}_{M}^{\triangle}\big)\cap\mathbb{P}(T^{\ast}_{P}X)

in
\mathbb{P}(T^{\ast}X)

is Zariski dense in
\mathbb{P}(T^{\ast}_{P}X)
.

We set
H_{s}:=X_{s}\cap H
.
Let
(E_{s},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{s})

denote the
\lambda
-flat bundle on
X_{s}\setminus H_{s}

obtained as the restriction of
\big(E,\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
.
For each
s\in\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\triangle}
,
there exists a pluri-harmonic metric

h_{s}
of
\big(E_{s},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{s}\big)

such that

(i)

h_{s}
is adapted to
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{|X_{s}}
,

(ii)

\det(h_{s})=h_{\det(E)|X_{s}\setminus H_{s}}
.

Let
\texttt{P}_{1}^{\triangle}\colon\mathfrak{X}^{\triangle}_{M}\longrightarrow X

be the induced map.
Let
\mathfrak{H}_{M}^{\triangle}:=\big(\texttt{P}_{1}^{\triangle}\big)^{-1}(H)
. We set

\big(E^{\triangle},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{E^{\triangle}}\big):=\big(\texttt{P}_{1}^{\triangle}\big)^{-1}\big(E,\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

on
\mathfrak{X}_{M}^{\triangle}\setminus\mathfrak{H}^{\triangle}_{M}
. By Lemma
3.21
and Proposition
4.5
,
the family of pluri harmonic metrics
h_{s}

(s\in\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\triangle})

induces a continuous Hermitian metric
h^{\triangle}
of

E^{\triangle}
. We also obtain Hermitian metrics

h^{\triangle(\epsilon_{i})}:=\big(\texttt{P}_{1}^{\triangle}\big)^{-1}\big(h^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{\mathop{\rm HE}\nolimits}\big)
.

7.2.2
Local holomorphic coordinate systems

Let
P\in X\setminus W_{M}
. We take
s_{\infty}\in\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\triangle}

such that
P\not\in X_{s_{\infty}}
.
The following is clear
because

\widetilde{\texttt{P}}_{1}\big(\mathfrak{X}^{\triangle}_{M}\big)\cap\mathbb{P}(T^{\ast}_{P}X)

is dense in
\mathbb{P}(T^{\ast}_{P}X)
.

Lemma 7.3
.

There exist
s_{i}\in\mathfrak{Z}^{\triangle}_{M}

(i=1,2)

and
\delta>0

such that the following holds:

•

P\in X_{s_{i}}

(i=1,2)
.

•

X_{s_{1}}
and
X_{s_{2}}
are transversal at
P
.

•

\{s_{1}+as_{\infty}\mid|a|<\delta\}
,

\{s_{2}+as_{\infty}\mid|a|<\delta\}
,

\bigl\{s_{1}+s_{2}+as_{\infty}\mid|a|<\delta\bigr\}

and

\bigl\{s_{1}+\sqrt{-1}s_{2}+as_{\infty}\mid|a|<\delta\bigr\}

are contained in

\mathfrak{Z}^{\triangle}_{M}
.

We set
x_{i}:=s_{i}/s_{\infty}

(i=1,2)
.
There exists a neighbourhood
U_{P}

of
P
in
X\setminus H

such that

(x_{1},x_{2})
is a holomorphic coordinate system
on
U_{P}
.
Note that

\bigl\{\sum b_{i}x_{i}=c\bigr\}\cap U_{P}

is equal to
U_{P}\cap X_{b_{1}s_{1}+b_{2}s_{2}-cs_{\infty}}
.

7.2.3
Proof of Proposition
7.2

Take a sequence
m_{i}\to\infty
in
{\mathbb{Z}}
. We set
\epsilon_{i}:=m_{i}^{-1}
. By Proposition
3.1
,
we obtain the following convergence:

\displaystyle\lim_{i\to\infty}\int_{X\setminus H}\bigl|G\big(h^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{\mathop{\rm HE}\nolimits}\big)\bigr|^{2}_{h^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{\mathop{\rm HE}\nolimits},\omega_{\epsilon_{i}}}=0.

Let
h^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}
be a Hermitian metric
for
\big(\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)

as in Proposition
5.6
.
Let
b_{i}
be the automorphism of
E

determined by

h^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{\mathop{\rm HE}\nolimits}=h^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits}\cdot b_{i}
.
Then, for each
i
,

\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}(b_{i})
is
L^{2}

with respect to

h^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{\mathop{\rm HE}\nolimits}
and
\omega_{\epsilon_{i}}
.

Let
\omega_{\epsilon_{i},s}
denote
the Kähler form of
X_{s}\setminus H_{s}

induced by
\omega_{\epsilon_{i}}
.
Let
h_{s}^{(\epsilon_{i})}
denote the restriction of

h^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{\mathop{\rm HE}\nolimits}
to
X_{s}\setminus H_{s}
.

By Fubini’s theorem,
after going to a subsequence,
there exists a
{\mathbb{C}}^{\ast}
-invariant subset

\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\sharp}\!\subset\!\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\triangle}

with the following property:

(a1)

\lim\limits_{i\to\infty}\int_{X_{s}\setminus H_{s}}\bigl|G\big(h_{s}^{(\epsilon_{i})}\big)\bigr|^{2}_{h_{s}^{(\epsilon_{i})},\omega_{\epsilon_{i},s}}=0

holds
for each
s\in\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\sharp}
.

(a2)

For each
s\in\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\sharp}
,

\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{s}\big(b_{i|X_{s}\setminus H_{s}}\big)

is
L^{2}
with respect to

h^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{s}

and
\omega_{\epsilon_{i},s}
.

(a3)

The Lebesgue measure of

\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\triangle}\setminus\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\sharp}

is
0
.

Note that the condition
(a2)
implies the following.

Lemma 7.4
.

Let
s\in\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\sharp}
.
Let
\widetilde{h}^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{s}

be a harmonic metric of
(E_{s},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{s})

adapted to

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}^{(\epsilon_{i})}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)_{|X_{s}}

such that

\det\big(\widetilde{h}^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{s}\big)=h_{\det(E)|X_{s}\setminus H_{s}}
.
Let
\widetilde{b}_{i,s}
be the automorphism of

E_{|X_{s}\setminus H_{s}}

determined by

h^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{s}=\widetilde{h}^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{s}\widetilde{b}_{i,s}
.
Then,

\widetilde{b}_{i,s}

and
\widetilde{b}_{i,s}^{-1}

are bounded with respect to

\widetilde{h}^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{s}
,
and
\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big(\widetilde{b}_{i,s}\big)

is
L^{2}
with respect to

\widetilde{h}^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{s}

and
\omega_{\epsilon_{i},s}

Proof.

Let
b^{\prime}_{i,s}
be the automorphism of
E_{s}

determined by

\widetilde{h}^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{s}=h^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits|X_{s}\setminus H_{s}}
.
Then,

b^{\prime}_{i,s}
and
(b^{\prime}_{i,s})^{-1}
are bounded
with respect to
h^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits|X_{s}\setminus H_{s}}
,
and

\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}(b^{\prime}_{i,s})
is
L^{2}

with respect to

h^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{\mathop{\rm in}\nolimits|X_{s}\setminus H_{s}}

and
\omega_{\epsilon_{i},s}
,
according to Proposition
4.4
.
Then, we obtain the claim of the lemma.
∎

Lemma 7.5
.

There exists a
{\mathbb{C}}^{\ast}
-invariant subset

\mathfrak{X}_{M}^{\sharp}\subset\mathfrak{X}_{M}^{\triangle}\times_{\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\triangle}}\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\sharp}

such that
the following holds:

•

The measure of

\mathfrak{X}_{M}^{\triangle}\setminus\mathfrak{X}_{M}^{\sharp}

is
0
.

•

A subsequence of

h^{\triangle(\epsilon_{i})}_{|\mathfrak{X}_{M}^{\sharp}}

is convergent to
h^{\triangle}_{|\mathfrak{X}_{M}^{\sharp}}

at any point of
\mathfrak{X}_{M}^{\sharp}
.

Proof.

By Proposition
4.8
,
for any
s\in\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\sharp}
,
the sequence

h_{s}^{(\epsilon_{i})}

is weakly convergent to
h_{s}
in
L_{1}^{2}

locally on
X_{s}\setminus H_{s}
. We set

b^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{s}:=h_{s}^{(\epsilon_{i})}h_{s}^{-1}
. We obtain

\det\big(b_{s}^{(\epsilon_{i})}\big)=1
,
and
b^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{s}
converges
to the identity
locally on
X_{s}\setminus H_{s}

in
L^{p}
for any
p\geq 1
. We set

g^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{s}:=\bigl|b^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{s}\bigr|_{h_{s}}
on
X_{s}\setminus H_{s}
. We obtain the function

g^{(\epsilon_{i})}

on

\mathfrak{X}_{M}^{\triangle}\times_{\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\triangle}}\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\sharp}

from

g^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{s}

(s\in\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\sharp})
. By Lemma
3.21

and Proposition
4.8
,
for any compact subset

K\subset\mathfrak{X}_{M}^{\triangle}\setminus\mathfrak{H}_{M}^{\triangle}
,
the restriction of

g^{(\epsilon_{i})}

to

K\cap\bigl(\mathfrak{X}_{M}^{\triangle}\times_{\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\triangle}}\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\sharp}\bigr)

are uniformly bounded.
Note that the sequence

\bigl(h^{\triangle(\epsilon_{i})}\big(h^{\triangle}\big)^{-1}\bigr)_{|K\cap X_{s}}=b_{s|K\cap X_{s}}^{(\epsilon_{i})}

is convergent in
L^{p}
for any
p\geq 1
. By Fubini’s theorem and Lebesgue theorem,
we obtain the
L^{p}
-convergence
of
h^{\triangle(\epsilon_{i})}\big(h^{\triangle}\big)^{-1}

to the identity for any
p

on
K\cap\bigl(\mathfrak{X}_{M}^{\triangle}\times_{\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\triangle}}\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\sharp}\bigr)
.
Then,
after going to a subsequence,
we obtain the desired convergence.
∎

Remark 7.6
.

If
\lambda=0
,
the argument can be simplified.
Indeed, by Proposition
3.31
,
the curvature
R\big(h_{s}^{(\epsilon_{i})}\big)
of
h_{s}^{(\epsilon_{i})}

are bounded locally on
X_{s}\setminus H
.
Hence, we obtain that

h^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{s}
is weakly convergent to
h_{s}
in
L_{2}^{2}
. In particular,

h_{s}^{(\epsilon_{i})}

is convergent to
h_{s}

in the
C^{0}
-sense locally on
X_{s}\setminus H_{s}
.

There exists a subset

X^{\sharp}\subset\texttt{P}^{\triangle}_{1}\big(\mathfrak{X}_{M}^{\sharp}\big)

such that for any
P\in X^{\sharp}
,
the measure of

\big(\texttt{P}^{\triangle}_{1}\big)^{-1}(P)\setminus\bigl(\mathfrak{X}_{M}^{\sharp}\bigr)

is
0

in
\big(\texttt{P}^{\triangle}_{1}\big)^{-1}(P)
,
and that
the measure of
X\setminus X^{\sharp}
is
0

in
X
. We obtain that the sequence
h^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{\mathop{\rm HE}\nolimits|X^{\sharp}}

is convergent
to a Hermitian metric

h_{\infty}
of
E_{|X^{\sharp}}
.

Lemma 7.7
.

For any
P\in X^{\sharp}

and

s\in\texttt{P}_{2}\bigl(\big(\texttt{P}_{1}^{\triangle}\big)^{-1}(P)\bigr)
,
we obtain
(h_{s})_{|P}=h_{\infty|P}
.

Proof.

For any
s\in\texttt{P}_{2}\bigl(\big(\texttt{P}^{\triangle}_{1}\big)^{-1}(P)\bigr)\cap\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\sharp}
,
we obtain

(h_{s})_{|P}=h_{\infty|P}
.
Then, by using the continuity of
h_{s}
on
s
, we obtain

(h_{s})_{|P}=h_{\infty|P}

for any
s\in\texttt{P}_{2}\bigl(\big(\texttt{P}^{\triangle}_{1}\big)^{-1}(P)\bigr)
.
∎

Lemma 7.8
.

Let
P\in X\setminus(W_{M}\cup H)
.
Then,
for any
s_{1},s_{2}\in\texttt{P}_{2}\bigl(\big(\texttt{P}^{\triangle}_{1}\big)^{-1}(P)\bigr)
,
we obtain

h_{s_{1}|P}=h_{s_{2}|P}
.

Proof.

For any
P\in X^{\sharp}

and for any

s_{1},s_{2}\in\texttt{P}_{2}\bigl(\big(\texttt{P}^{\triangle}_{1}\big)^{-1}(P)\bigr)
,
we obtain

h_{s_{1}|P}=h_{\infty|P}=h_{s_{2}|P}
. By the continuity of
h_{s}
on
s
,
we obtain the claim of the lemma.
∎

Then,
h_{\infty}

extends to a Hermitian metric
of
E_{|X\setminus(H\cup W_{M})}

by setting

h_{\infty|P}:=(h_{s})_{|P}

for
s\in\texttt{P}_{2}\big(\big(\texttt{P}^{\triangle}_{1}\big)^{-1}(P)\big)
.

Lemma 7.9
.

h_{\infty}
induces a Hermitian metric of

E_{|X\setminus(H\cup W_{M})}

of
C^{1}
-class.
The
C^{1}
-Hermitian metric is also denoted by

h_{\infty}
.

Proof.

Let
P
be any point of
X\setminus W_{M}
.
Let
(U_{P},x_{1},x_{2})

be a holomorphic coordinate neighbourhood
as in Section
7.2.2
. By using Proposition
4.5
,
we define the continuous Hermitian metric
h^{(i)}_{P}
of
E_{|U_{P}}

by the condition that

h_{P|\{x_{i}=a\}}

is equal to the restriction of

h_{s_{1}+as_{\infty}}
. By the construction of
h_{\infty}
,
we obtain

h_{\infty|U_{P}}=h^{(i)}_{P}
.
Hence, we obtain that
h_{\infty|U_{P}\cap X^{\sharp}}
induces
a continuous Hermitian metric
h_{P,\infty}
of

E_{|U_{P}}
,
and
h^{(1)}_{P}=h_{P,\infty}=h^{(2)}_{P}
hold.
Moreover, by Proposition
4.5
,
any derivative of
h^{(j)}_{P}

with respect to
\partial_{z_{i}}
and
\partial_{\overline{z}_{i}}

(i\neq j)

are continuous. We obtain that
h_{P,\infty}
is
C^{1}
.
Thus, we obtain the claim of the lemma.
∎

We obtain the operator
\mathbb{D}^{\lambda\star}_{h_{\infty}}

from
\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}
and
h_{\infty}
. We define

G(h_{\infty}):=\bigl[\mathbb{D}^{\lambda},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda\star}_{h_{\infty}}\bigr]

as a current.

Lemma 7.10
.

G(h_{\infty})^{(1,1)}=0

on
X\setminus(H\cup W_{M})
.

Proof.

Let
P\in X\setminus(H\cup W_{M})
.
Let
(U_{P},x_{1},x_{2})
be a holomorphic coordinate neighbourhood
as in Section
7.2.2
. We have the expression

\displaystyle G(h_{\infty})^{(1,1)}=G(h_{\infty})_{11}{\rm d}x_{1}\,{\rm d}\overline{x}_{1}+G(h_{\infty})_{12}{\rm d}x_{1}\,{\rm d}\overline{x}_{2}+G(h_{\infty})_{21}{\rm d}x_{2}\,{\rm d}\overline{x}_{1}+G(h_{\infty})_{22}{\rm d}x_{2}\,{\rm d}\overline{x}_{2}.

Because
h_{\infty|\{x_{i}=a\}}
is equal to

h_{s_{i}+as_{\infty}}
,
we obtain
G(h_{\infty})_{ii}=0
for
i=1,2
.

By considering
the holomorphic coordinate system

(w_{1},w_{2})=(x_{1}+x_{2},x_{1}-x_{2})

and the coefficient of

{\rm d}w_{1}\,{\rm d}\overline{w}_{1}
in
G(h_{\infty})^{(1,1)}
,
we obtain
G(h_{\infty})_{12}+G(h_{\infty})_{21}=0
. By considering the holomorphic coordinate system

(z_{1},z_{2})=\big(x_{1}+\sqrt{-1}x_{2},x_{1}-\sqrt{-1}x_{2}\big)

and the coefficient of

{\rm d}z_{1}\,{\rm d}\overline{z}_{1}
in
G(h_{\infty})^{(1,1)}
,
we obtain
G(h_{\infty})_{12}-G(h_{\infty})_{21}=0
.
Therefore, we obtain that

G(h_{\infty})_{ij}=0
.
∎

Lemma 7.11
.

We obtain
\Lambda G(h_{\infty})=0
on
X\setminus(H\cup W_{M})
.
As a result,
h_{\infty}
is
C^{\infty}

on
X\setminus(H\cup W_{M})
.
If moreover
\lambda\neq 0
,
then
h_{\infty}
is a pluri-harmonic metric of

\big(E,\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)_{|X\setminus H\cup W_{M}}
.

Proof.

The first claim immediately follows from
Lemma
7.10
. We obtain the second claim by
the elliptic regularity
and a standard bootstrapping argument.
The last claim follows from Corollary
2.16
.
∎

Lemma 7.12
.

In the case
\lambda=0
,
we obtain
\partial_{h_{\infty}}\theta=0
,
i.e.,

h_{\infty}
is a pluri-harmonic metric of
the Higgs bundle

\big(E,\overline{\partial}_{E},\theta\big)_{|X\setminus(H\cup W_{M})}
.

Proof.

Let us observe that the sequence

\partial_{h^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{\mathop{\rm HE}\nolimits}}-\partial_{h_{\infty}}

is convergent to
0

almost everywhere on
X\setminus H
.
It is enough to prove that

\partial_{h^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{\mathop{\rm HE}\nolimits|X_{s}}}-\partial_{h_{s}}

is convergent to
0

for
s\in\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\sharp}
.
Let
b^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{s}
be the automorphism of

E_{|X_{s}\setminus H}

which is self-adjoint with respect to

h_{s}
and
h_{\mathop{\rm HE}\nolimits|X_{s}}^{(\epsilon_{i})}

determined by
h_{\mathop{\rm HE}\nolimits|X_{s}}^{(\epsilon_{i})}=h_{s}b^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{s}
. By Proposition
4.8
,
the sequence

\big(b_{s}^{(\epsilon_{i})}\big)^{-1}\partial_{h_{s}}\big(b_{s}^{(\epsilon_{i})}\big)

is convergent to
0
weakly in
L^{2}

locally on
X_{s}\setminus H
. By Proposition
3.31
,
the sequence

\big(b_{s}^{(\epsilon_{i})}\big)^{-1}\partial_{h_{s}}\big(b_{s}^{(\epsilon_{i})}\big)

is bounded in
L_{2}^{p}

locally on
X_{s}\setminus H

for any
p\geq 1
.

Lemma 7.13
.

\big(b_{s}^{(\epsilon_{i})}\big)^{-1}\partial_{h_{s}}\big(b_{s}^{(\epsilon_{i})}\big)

is convergent to
0
in
L_{1}^{p}

locally on
X_{s}\setminus H
.

Proof.

Let

\big(b_{s}^{\prime(\epsilon_{i})}\big)^{-1}\partial_{h_{s}}\big(b_{s}^{\prime(\epsilon_{i})}\big)

be any subsequence
of
\big(b_{s}^{(\epsilon_{i})}\big)^{-1}\partial_{h_{s}}\big(b_{s}^{(\epsilon_{i})}\big)
.
Because it is bounded in
L_{2}^{p}
locally on
X_{s}\setminus H
,
it contains a subsequence

\big(b_{s}^{\prime\prime(\epsilon_{i})}\big)^{-1}\partial_{h_{s}}\big(b_{s}^{\prime\prime(\epsilon_{i})}\big)

which is weakly convergent
in
L_{2}^{p}
locally on
X_{s}\setminus H

for any
p\geq 2
. By the Sobolev embedding theorem,
the sequence

\big(b_{s}^{\prime\prime(\epsilon_{i})}\big)^{-1}\partial_{h_{s}}\big(b_{s}^{\prime\prime(\epsilon_{i})}\big)

is convergent in
L_{1}^{p}
locally on
X_{s}\setminus H
.
Because

\big(b_{s}^{(\epsilon_{i})}\big)^{-1}\partial_{h_{s}}\big(b_{s}^{(\epsilon_{i})}\big)

is convergent to
0
weakly in
L^{2}
locally on
X_{s}\setminus H
,
the limit should be
0
.
Therefore, we obtain the claim of Lemma
7.13
.
∎

As a result,

\partial_{h_{\mathop{\rm HE}\nolimits}^{(\epsilon_{i})}}\theta

is convergent to

\partial_{h_{\infty}}\theta
almost everywhere.
Note that

\displaystyle 0\leq\int_{X\setminus H}\bigl|\partial_{h_{\mathop{\rm HE}\nolimits}^{(\epsilon_{i})}}\theta\bigr|_{h_{\mathop{\rm HE}\nolimits}^{(\epsilon_{i})},\omega_{\epsilon_{i}}}^{2}\leq\int_{X\setminus H}\bigl|G\big(h_{\mathop{\rm HE}\nolimits}^{(\epsilon_{i})}\big)\bigr|_{h_{\mathop{\rm HE}\nolimits}^{(\epsilon_{i})},\omega_{\epsilon_{i}}}^{2}=-8\pi^{2}\int_{X}\mathop{\rm ch}\nolimits_{2}\big(\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}\big).

We also have

\lim\limits_{i\to\infty}\int_{X}\mathop{\rm ch}\nolimits_{2}\big(\mathcal{P}^{(\epsilon_{i})}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}\big)=0
. We have the following convergence almost everywhere
on
X\setminus H
:

\displaystyle\lim_{i\to\infty}\bigl|\partial_{h_{\mathop{\rm HE}\nolimits}^{(\epsilon_{i})}}\theta\bigr|_{h_{\mathop{\rm HE}\nolimits}^{(\epsilon_{i})},\omega_{\epsilon_{i}}}^{2}=\bigl|\partial_{h_{\infty}}\theta\bigr|_{h_{\infty},\omega_{X}}^{2}.

Therefore, we obtain

\int\bigl|\partial_{h_{\infty}}\theta\bigr|^{2}_{h_{\infty},\omega_{X}}=0

by Fatou’s lemma.
∎

Lemma 7.14
.

h_{\infty}
induces a
C^{\infty}
-metric
of
E
on
X\setminus H
,
and hence it is a pluri-harmonic metric of

\big(E,\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
.

Proof.

It is enough to prove that

h_{\infty}
is a
C^{\infty}
-metric
around any point of
W_{M}\setminus H
. We have only to apply the argument
in
[
49
, Lemma 5.15]
.
∎

If
\lambda=0
,
we obtain that

\big(E,\overline{\partial}_{E},\theta,h_{\infty}\big)

is a good wild harmonic bundle on
(X,H)
,
because
(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\theta)
is a good filtered Higgs bundle.
If
\lambda\neq 0
,
the associated Higgs bundle

\big(E,\overline{\partial}_{E},\theta\big)

with the pluri-harmonic metric
h_{\infty}

is a good wild harmonic bundle
by
[
51
, Proposition 13.5.1]
. We obtain a good filtered
\lambda
-flat bundle

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}^{h_{\infty}}E,\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
on
(X,H)
. We put
H^{[2]}=\bigcup_{i\neq j}(H_{i}\cap H_{j})
.
For any
P\in H\setminus\big(W_{M}\cup H^{[2]}\big)
,
there exists
s\in\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\triangle}

such that
P\in X_{s}
. By the construction,

h_{\infty|X_{s}\setminus H_{s}}=h_{s}
.
Hence, we obtain

\mathcal{P}_{\ast}^{h_{\infty}}(E)_{|X_{s}}=\mathcal{P}_{\ast}(\mathcal{V})_{|X_{s}}
.
Let
Y:=(H\cap W_{M})\cup H^{[2]}
,
which is a finite subset of
H
. We obtain that

\mathcal{P}_{\ast}^{h_{\infty}}(E)_{|X\setminus Y}\simeq\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{|X\setminus Y}
. By Hartogs theorem,
we obtain that

\mathcal{P}_{\ast}^{h_{\infty}}(E)\simeq\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}
.
Thus, the proof of Proposition
7.2

is completed.
∎

7.3
Higher dimensional case

Let us prove Theorem
7.1

in the case
\dim X\geq 3

by an induction on
\dim X
.
Take a sufficiently large integer
M
. We set

\mathfrak{Z}_{M}:=H^{0}\big(X,L^{\otimes\,M}\big)\setminus\{0\}
,
and let
\mathfrak{X}_{M}\subset X\times\mathfrak{Z}_{M}

be defined as
\mathfrak{s}^{-1}(0)

as in Section
7.2.1
.
For any
s\in\mathfrak{Z}_{M}
,
set
X_{s}:=s^{-1}(0)
.
Let
\mathbb{P}(T^{\ast}X)
denote the projectivization of
the cotangent bundle of
X
.
If
M
is sufficiently large,
there exists a Zariski dense open subset

\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\circ}\subset\mathfrak{Z}_{M}

such that the following holds:

•

\texttt{P}_{2}^{\circ}\colon\mathfrak{X}_{M}^{\circ}:=\mathfrak{X}_{M}\times_{\mathfrak{Z}_{M}}\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\circ}\longrightarrow\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\circ}

is smooth.

•

\mathfrak{X}_{M}^{\circ}\cup\bigl(H\times\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\circ}\bigr)

is simply normal crossing.
Moreover
the intersections of any tuple of irreducible components
are smooth over
\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\circ}
.

•

The induced map

\texttt{P}_{1}\colon\mathfrak{X}_{M}^{\circ}\longrightarrow X
is surjective.
Moreover,
the induced morphism

\widetilde{\texttt{P}}_{1}
:
\mathfrak{X}_{M}^{\circ}\longrightarrow\mathbb{P}(T^{\ast}X)

is surjective.

Let
p_{i,j}
denote the projection of

X\times\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\circ}\times\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\circ}

onto the product of the
i
-th component
and the
j
-th component.
For
j=2,3
,
let
\big(\mathfrak{X}_{M}^{\circ}\big)^{(j)}
denote
the pull back of

\mathfrak{X}_{M}^{\circ}

by
p_{1,j}
.
There exists a Zariski dense open subset

\mathfrak{U}_{M}\subset\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\circ}\times\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\circ}

such that the following holds:

•

Let
\big(\mathfrak{X}_{M}^{\circ}\big)^{(j)}_{\mathfrak{U}_{M}}

denote the fiber product of

\big(\mathfrak{X}_{M}^{\circ}\big)^{(j)}

and
\mathfrak{U}_{M}

over
\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\circ}\times\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\circ}
.
Then,

\big(\mathfrak{X}_{M}^{\circ}\big)^{(2)}_{\mathfrak{U}_{M}}\cup\big(\mathfrak{X}_{M}^{\circ}\big)^{(3)}_{\mathfrak{U}_{M}}\cup(H\times\mathfrak{U}_{M})

is simply normal crossing.
Moreover,
the intersection of any tuple of irreducible components
are smooth over
\mathfrak{U}
.

By the Mehta–Ramanathan type theorem
(Proposition
3.8
),
there exists a Zariski dense open subset

\mathfrak{U}_{M}^{\triangle}\subset\mathfrak{U}_{M}

such that the following holds:

•

For
{\boldsymbol{s}}=(s_{1},s_{2})\in\mathfrak{U}_{M}^{\triangle}
,
we set

X_{{\boldsymbol{s}}}:=X_{s_{1}}\cap X_{s_{2}}
.
Then, the restriction

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)_{|X_{{\boldsymbol{s}}}}

is a
\mu_{L}
-stable good filtered
\lambda
-flat bundle
on
(X_{{\boldsymbol{s}}},H\cap X_{{\boldsymbol{s}}})
.

Hence, there exists a Zariski dense open subset

\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\triangle}\subset\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\circ}

such that the following holds:

•

For any
s\in\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\triangle}
,

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)_{|X_{s}}

is a
\mu_{L}
-stable good filtered
\lambda
-flat bundle on

(X_{s},H\cap X_{s})
.

•

For any
s_{1},s_{2}\in\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\triangle}
,
there exists a Zariski open subset

\mathfrak{V}(s_{1},s_{2})\subset\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\triangle}

such that the restrictions

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)_{|X_{(s_{i},s_{3})}}

(i=1,2)

are
\mu_{L}
-stable for any
s_{3}\in\mathfrak{V}(s_{1},s_{2})
.

We set
\mathfrak{X}_{M}^{\triangle}:=\mathfrak{X}_{M}\times_{\mathfrak{Z}_{M}}\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\triangle}
.
Let
\texttt{P}_{2}^{\triangle}\colon\mathfrak{X}_{M}^{\triangle}\longrightarrow X

denote the naturally induced morphism.
Then,
W_{M}:=X\setminus\texttt{P}_{2}^{\triangle}\big(\mathfrak{X}_{M}^{\triangle}\big)

is a finite subset.

For any
P\in X\setminus(H\cup W_{M})
,
there exists
s\in\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\triangle}

such that
P\in X_{s}
.
Then,

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{s},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{s}\big):=\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)_{|X_{s}}

is
\mu_{L}
-stable,
and the following holds:

\displaystyle\int_{X_{s}}c_{1}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{s})c_{1}\big(L_{|X_{s}}\big)^{\dim X_{s}-1}=0,\qquad\int_{X_{s}}\mathop{\rm ch}\nolimits_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{s})c_{1}\big(L_{|X_{s}}\big)^{\dim X_{s}-2}=0.

There exists a pluri-harmonic metric
h_{s}

of
\big(E_{s},\overline{\partial}_{E_{s}},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{s}\big):=\big(E,\overline{\partial}_{E},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)_{|X_{s}\setminus H}

adapted to

\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{s}

such that
\det(h_{s})=h_{\det(E)|X_{s}\setminus H}
.
Take another
s^{\prime}\in\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\triangle}

such that
P\in X_{s^{\prime}}
.
There exists a pluri-harmonic metric
h_{s^{\prime}}

of
\big(E_{s^{\prime}},\overline{\partial}_{E_{s^{\prime}}},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{s^{\prime}}\big)

adapted to
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{s^{\prime}}

such that
\det(h_{s^{\prime}})=h_{\det(E)|X_{s^{\prime}}\setminus H}
.

Lemma 7.15
.

h_{s|P}=h_{s^{\prime}|P}
.

Proof.

Suppose that
X_{s}\cup X_{s^{\prime}}\cup H
is
simply normal crossing. We set
X_{s,s^{\prime}}:=X_{s}\cap X_{s^{\prime}}
.
It is smooth and connected. We obtain a good filtered
\lambda
-flat bundle

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)_{|X_{s,s^{\prime}}}
,
and
h_{s|X_{s,s^{\prime}}}
and
h_{s^{\prime}|X_{s,s^{\prime}}}

are adapted to

\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{|X_{s,s^{\prime}}}
.
Let
b_{s,s^{\prime}}
be the automorphism of

E_{|X_{s,s^{\prime}}}
which is self-adjoint
with respect to both
h_{s|X_{s,s^{\prime}}}
and
h_{s^{\prime}|X_{s,s^{\prime}}}
,
and determined by

h_{s^{\prime}|X_{s,s^{\prime}}}=h_{s|X_{s,s^{\prime}}}\cdot b_{s,s^{\prime}}
.
There exists a decomposition

\displaystyle\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)_{|X_{s,s^{\prime}}}=\bigoplus\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{i},\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}_{i}\big),

which is orthogonal with respect to both

h_{s|X_{s,s^{\prime}}}
and
h_{s^{\prime}|X_{s,s^{\prime}}}
,
and

b_{s,s^{\prime}}=\bigoplus a_{i}\mathop{\rm id}\nolimits_{\mathcal{V}_{i}}

for some positive constants
a_{i}
.

There exists
s_{1}\in\mathfrak{V}(s,s^{\prime})
.
Then,

(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\theta)_{|X_{s_{1},s}}

and

(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\theta)_{|X_{s_{1},s^{\prime}}}

are
\mu_{L}
-stable.
Therefore,
we have

h_{s|X_{s_{1},s}}=h_{s_{1}|X_{s_{1},s}}

and

h_{s^{\prime}|X_{s_{1},s^{\prime}}}=h_{s_{1}|X_{s_{1},s^{\prime}}}
. We obtain that

h_{s|X_{s_{1}}\cap X_{s}\cap X_{s^{\prime}}}=h_{s^{\prime}|X_{s_{1}}\cap X_{s}\cap X_{s^{\prime}}}
.
It implies that
a_{i}
are
1
,
and hence
h_{s|P}=h_{s^{\prime}|P}
.

In general,
there exists
s_{2}\in\mathfrak{Z}_{M}^{\triangle}

such that

(i)

P\in X_{s_{2}}
,

(ii)

X_{s}\cup X_{s_{2}}\cup H

and
X_{s^{\prime}}\cup X_{s_{2}}\cup H

are simply normal crossing. By the above consideration,
we obtain

h_{s|P}=h_{s_{2}|P}=h_{s^{\prime}|P}
.
∎

Therefore,
we obtain Hermitian metrics

h_{P}
of
E_{|P}

(P\in X\setminus(H\cup W_{M}))
. By using the argument in Lemma
7.9
,
we can prove that they induce a Hermitian metric
h

of
E_{|X\setminus(H\cup W_{M})}

of
C^{1}
-class. We obtain
G(h)
from
\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}
and
h

as a current.
Because
h_{|X_{s}}

\big(s\in\mathfrak{U}_{M}^{\triangle}\big)

are pluri-harmonic metrics of

\big(E,\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)_{|X_{s}\setminus H}
,
we obtain that
G(h)=0
.
It also implies that
h
is
C^{\infty}
on

X\setminus(H\cup W_{M})
. By using the argument in
[
49
, Lemma 5.15]
,
we obtain that

h
induces a pluri-harmonic metric
of
\big(E,\mathbb{D}^{\lambda}\big)
on
X\setminus H
.
Then, as in the proof of Proposition
7.2
,
we can conclude that

\big(E,\mathbb{D}^{\lambda},h\big)
is a good wild harmonic bundle,
and that
\mathcal{P}^{h}_{\ast}(E)=\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}
.
Thus, we obtain Theorem
7.1
.
∎

8
Homogeneity with respect to group actions

8.1
Preliminary

8.1.1
Homogeneous harmonic bundles

Let
Y
be a complex manifold.
Let
K
be a compact Lie group.
Let
\rho\colon K\times Y\longrightarrow Y

be a
K
-action on
Y

such that
\rho_{k}\colon Y\longrightarrow Y
is holomorphic
for any
k\in K
.
Let
\kappa\colon K\longrightarrow S^{1}
be
a homomorphism of Lie groups.

Let
\big(E,\overline{\partial}_{E},\theta,h\big)
be a harmonic bundle
on
Y
.
It is called
(K,\rho,\kappa)
-homogeneous
if
\big(E,\overline{\partial}_{E},h\big)
is
K
-equivariant
and
k^{\ast}\theta=\kappa(k)\theta
.

Remark 8.1
.

According to s3,
harmonic bundles are equivalent to
polarized variation of pure twistor structure of weight
w
,
for any given integer
w
.
If
\kappa
is non-trivial,
as studied in
[
53
, Section 3]
,
by choosing a vector
\mathfrak{v}
in the Lie algebra of
K

such that
d\kappa(\mathfrak{v})\neq 0
,
we obtain the integrability of the variation
of pure twistor structure
from the homogeneity of harmonic bundles.

8.1.2
Homogeneous filtered Higgs sheaves
and the stability condition

with respect to the action

Let
X
be a connected complex projective manifold
with a simple normal crossing hypersurface
H
.
Let
G
be a complex reductive algebraic group.
Let
\rho\colon G\times Y\longrightarrow Y
be an algebraic
G
-action
on
Y
which preserves
H
.
Let
\kappa\colon G\longrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{\ast}

be a homomorphism of complex algebraic groups.

Let
(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\theta)

be a filtered Higgs sheaf on
(Y,H)
.
It is called
(G,\rho,\kappa)
-homogeneous
if
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}
is
G
-equivariant
and
g^{\ast}\theta=\kappa(g)\theta

for any
g\in G
.

Let
L
be a
G
-equivariant ample line bundle on
X
.
A
(G,\rho,\kappa)
-homogeneous
filtered Higgs sheaf
(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\theta)
on
(X,H)

is called
\mu_{L}
-stable (resp.
\mu_{L}
-semistable)
with respect to the
G
-action
if the following holds:

•

Let
\mathcal{V}^{\prime}
be a
G
-invariant
saturated Higgs subsheaf of
\mathcal{V}

such that
0<\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits\mathcal{V}^{\prime}<\mathop{\rm rank}\nolimits\mathcal{V}
.
Then,

\mu_{L}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}^{\prime})<\mu_{L}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})

(resp.
\mu_{L}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}^{\prime})\leq\mu_{L}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})
)
holds.

A
(G,\rho,\kappa)
-homogeneous
filtered Higgs sheaf
(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\theta)
on
(X,H)

is called
\mu_{L}
-polystable
with respect to the
G
-action
if it is
\mu_{L}
-semistable with respect to the
G
-action
and isomorphic to a direct sum
of
(G,\rho,\kappa)
-homogeneous filtered sheaves

\bigoplus(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{i},\theta_{i})
,
where each
(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{i},\theta_{i})

is
\mu_{L}
-stable with respect to the
G
-action.

Lemma 8.2
.

(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\theta)
is

\mu_{L}
-semistable
if and only if

(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\theta)

is
\mu_{L}
-semistable with respect to
the
G
-action.

Proof.

The “only if” part is clear.
Let us prove that the “if” part.
Let
\mathcal{V}_{0}\subset\mathcal{V}

be the
\beta
-subobject as in Proposition
3.4
.
Because
g^{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{0}
also has the same property,
we obtain that
\mathcal{V}_{0}
is
G
-invariant.
Then, the claim of the proposition is clear.
∎

The following lemma is clear.

Lemma 8.3
.

If
(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\theta)
is

\mu_{L}
-stable,
then

(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\theta)
is

\mu_{L}
-stable
with respect to the
G
-action.

Lemma 8.4
.

If
(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\theta)
is

\mu_{L}
-stable
with respect to the
G
-action,
then

(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\theta)
is

\mu_{L}
-polystable.

Proof.

According to Lemma
8.2
,

(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\theta)
is
\mu_{L}
-semistable.
Let
\mathcal{V}_{1}
be the socle of

(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\theta)

as in Proposition
3.5
.
Because
g^{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{1}

also has the same property,

\mathcal{V}_{1}
is
G
-invariant.
Moreover,

\mu_{L}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{1})=\mu_{L}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})

holds.
Hence, we obtain

\mathcal{V}_{1}=\mathcal{V}
.
According to Proposition
3.5
,

(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\theta)

is
\mu_{L}
-polystable.
∎

Remark 8.5
.

In general,
even if
(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\theta)
is
\mu_{L}
-stable
with respect to the
G
-action,

(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\theta)
is not necessarily
\mu_{L}
-stable.

8.1.3
Actions of a complex reductive group
and its compact real form

Let
X
be a complex projective manifold
equipped with an algebraic action of
a complex reductive group
G
.
Let
L
be a
G
-equivariant ample line bundle on
X
.
Let
K
be a compact real form of
G
.

Let
\big(E,\overline{\partial}_{E}\big)
be a
G
-equivariant holomorphic vector bundle on
X
.
Then, as the restriction,
we may naturally regard
\big(E,\overline{\partial}_{E}\big)

as a
K
-equivariant holomorphic vector bundle on
X
.

Lemma 8.6
.

The above procedure induces an equivalence
between
G
-equivariant holomorphic vector bundles
and
K
-equivariant holomorphic vector bundles on
X
.

Proof.

Let
\big(E,\overline{\partial}_{E}\big)
be a
K
-equivariant holomorphic vector bundle
on
X
.
There exists
m_{0}>0
such that

E\otimes L^{\otimes\,m_{0}}
is globally generating. We set

\mathcal{G}_{0}:=H^{0}\big(X,E\otimes L^{\otimes\,m_{0}}\big)\otimes\big(L^{\otimes\,m_{0}}\big)^{-1}
.
There exists a naturally induced epimorphism
of
\mathcal{O}_{X}
-modules
\mathcal{G}_{0}\longrightarrow E
.
Let
\mathcal{K}
denote the kernel.
There exists
m_{1}>0
such that

\mathcal{K}\otimes L^{\otimes\,m_{1}}
is globally generating. We set

\mathcal{G}_{1}:=H^{0}\big(X,\mathcal{K}\otimes L^{\otimes\,m_{1}}\big)\otimes\big(L^{\otimes\,m_{1}}\big)^{-1}
.
There exists a naturally induced epimorphism

\mathcal{G}_{1}\longrightarrow\mathcal{K}
.
Thus, we obtain a resolution

\mathcal{G}_{1}\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{0}
of
E
.
Because
E
is
K
-equivariant,

H^{0}\big(X,E\otimes L^{\otimes m_{0}}\big)

is naturally a
K
-representation,

\mathcal{G}_{0}
is a
K
-equivariant holomorphic vector bundle
on
X
,
and
\mathcal{G}_{0}\longrightarrow E
is
K
-equivariant.
Hence,
\mathcal{K}
is a
K
-equivariant holomorphic vector bundle.
Similarly

H^{0}\big(X,\mathcal{K}\otimes L^{\otimes m_{2}}\big)

is a
K
-representation,
and
\mathcal{G}_{1}
is
K
-equivariant holomorphic vector bundle,
and
\mathcal{G}_{1}\longrightarrow\mathcal{K}_{2}
is
K
-equivariant.

The
K
-representations
on
H^{0}\big(X,E\otimes L^{\otimes\,m_{1}}\big)

and
H^{0}\big(X,\mathcal{K}\otimes L^{\otimes m_{2}}\big)

naturally induce

G
-representations
on
H^{0}\big(X,E\otimes L^{\otimes\,m_{1}}\big)

and
H^{0}\big(X,\mathcal{K}\otimes L^{\otimes m_{2}}\big)
.
Hence,

\mathcal{G}_{i}
are naturally
algebraic
G
-equivariant vector bundles on
X
.
Moreover,
the morphism
\mathcal{G}_{1}\longrightarrow\mathcal{G}_{0}

is
G
-equivariant and algebraic.
Hence,
E
is a
G
-equivariant algebraic vector bundle
on
X
.
∎

8.2
An equivalence

8.2.1
Good filtered Higgs bundles associated with
homogeneous good wild Higgs bundles

Let
X
be a connected complex projective manifold
with a simple normal crossing hypersurface
H
.
Let
G
be a complex reductive group acting on
(X,H)
.
Let
K
be a compact real form of
G
.
The actions of
G
and
K
on
X
are denoted by
\rho
.
Let
\kappa\colon G\longrightarrow{\mathbb{C}}^{\ast}

be a character.
The induced homomorphism
K\longrightarrow S^{1}
is also denoted by
\kappa
.

Let
\big(E,\overline{\partial}_{E},\theta,h\big)
be a

(K,\rho,\kappa)
-homogeneous harmonic bundle on
X\setminus H

which is good wild on
(X,H)
. We obtain a good filtered Higgs bundle

\big(\mathcal{P}^{h}_{\ast}E,\theta\big)
on
(X,H)
.
Because each
\mathcal{P}^{h}_{{\boldsymbol{a}}}E
is naturally
a
K
-equivariant holomorphic vector bundle on
X
,

\mathcal{P}_{\ast}^{h}E
is naturally
G
-equivariant
by Lemma
8.6
.
Because
k^{\ast}\theta=\kappa(k)\theta
for any
k\in K
,
we obtain

g^{\ast}\theta=\kappa(g)\theta
for any
g\in G
.
Therefore,

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}^{h}E,\theta\big)

is a
(G,\rho,\kappa)
-homogeneous
good filtered Higgs bundle on
(X,H)
.

Let
L
be a
G
-equivariant ample line bundle on
X
.

Proposition 8.7
.

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}^{h}E,\theta\big)

is
\mu_{L}
-polystable
with respect to the
G
-action,
i.e.,
there exists a decomposition

\big(E,\overline{\partial}_{E},\theta,h\big)=\bigoplus(E_{i},\overline{\partial}_{E_{i}},\theta_{i},h_{i})

of
(G,\rho,\kappa)
-homogeneous harmonic bundles
such that each

\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}^{h_{i}}E_{i},\theta_{i}\big)
is
\mu_{L}
-stable
with respect to the
G
-action.

Proof.

Because
\big(\mathcal{P}^{h}_{\ast}E,\theta\big)

is
\mu_{L}
-polystable,
we obtain that

\big(\mathcal{P}^{h}_{\ast}E,\theta\big)

is
\mu_{L}
-semistable
with respect to the
G
-action.
Let
\mathcal{V}_{1}\subset\mathcal{P}^{h}E

be a
G
-invariant saturated Higgs
\mathcal{O}_{X}(\ast H)
-submodule
such that

\mu_{L}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{1})=\mu_{L}\big(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}^{h}E\big)=0
.
Let
E_{1}
be the Higgs subsheaf of
E

obtained as the restriction of
\mathcal{V}_{1}
to
X\setminus H
.
Then, by the argument in the proof of
[
51
, Proposition 13.6.1]
,
we obtain that
E_{1}
is a subbundle,
and the orthogonal complement

E_{2}:=E_{1}^{\bot}
is also a holomorphic subbundle.
Moreover,

\theta(E_{2})\subset E_{2}\otimes\Omega^{1}_{X\setminus H}
,
and
E_{2}
is
K
-equivariant.
Hence, we obtain a decomposition

\big(E,\overline{\partial}_{E},\theta,h\big)=\big(E_{1},\overline{\partial}_{E_{1}},\theta_{1},h_{1}\big)\oplus\big(E_{2}\overline{\partial}_{E_{2}},\theta_{2},h_{2}\big)

of
(K,\rho,\kappa)
-homogeneous harmonic bundles.
Then, the claim of the proposition is clear.
∎

8.2.2
Uniqueness

Let
\big(E,\overline{\partial}_{E},\theta,h\big)
be a

(K,\rho,\kappa)
-homogeneous harmonic bundle on
X\setminus H

which is good wild on
(X,H)
.
Let
h^{\prime}
be another pluri-harmonic metric of

\big(E,\overline{\partial}_{E},\theta\big)

such that

(i)

h^{\prime}
is
K
-invariant,

(ii)

\mathcal{P}_{\ast}^{h^{\prime}}E=\mathcal{P}_{\ast}^{h}E
.
The following is clear from
Proposition
2.22
.

Proposition 8.8
.

There exists a decomposition

\big(E,\overline{\partial}_{E},\theta\big)=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{m}\big(E_{i},\overline{\partial}_{E_{i}},\theta_{i}\big)

such that

(i)
the decomposition is orthogonal with respect to
both
h
and
h^{\prime}
,

(ii)
there exist
a_{i}>0

(i=1,\ldots,m)

such that

h^{\prime}_{|E_{i}}=a_{i}h_{E_{i}}
,

(iii)
the decomposition
E=\bigoplus E_{i}

is preserved by the
K
-action.

8.2.3
Existence theorem

Let
(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\theta)

be a
(G,\rho,\kappa)
-homogeneous good filtered
Higgs bundle on
(X,H)

such that

\displaystyle\int_{X}c_{1}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})c_{1}(L)^{\dim X-1}=0,\quad\int_{X}\mathop{\rm ch}\nolimits_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})c_{1}(L)^{\dim X-2}=0.

Let
\big(E,\overline{\partial}_{E},\theta\big)

be the Higgs bundle on
X\setminus H

obtained as the restriction of
(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\theta)
.

Theorem 8.9
.

Suppose that

(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\theta)
is
\mu_{L}
-stable
with respect to the
G
-action.
Then, there exists a
K
-invariant pluri-harmonic metric
h

of
\big(E,\overline{\partial}_{E},\theta\big)

such that
\mathcal{P}^{h}_{\ast}E=\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}
.
If
h^{\prime}
is another
K
-invariant pluri-harmonic metric
of
\big(E,\overline{\partial}_{E},\theta\big)
,
there exists a positive constant
a

such that
h^{\prime}=ah
.

Proof.

By Lemma
8.4
,

(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\theta)
is
\mu_{L}
-polystable.
There exists the canonical decomposition

\displaystyle(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\theta)=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{m}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{i},\theta_{i})\otimes U_{i},

where
(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{i},\theta_{i})

are
\mu_{L}
-stable good filtered Higgs bundles
such that

(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{i},\theta_{i})\not\simeq(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{j},\theta_{j})

(i\neq j)
,
and
U_{i}
are finite dimensional complex vector spaces.
Let

\big(E_{i},\overline{\partial}_{E_{i}},\theta_{i}\big)

denote the Higgs bundle obtained as the restriction of

(\mathcal{V}_{i},\theta_{i})
to
X\setminus H
.
There exist pluri-harmonic metrics
h_{i}

of
\big(E_{i},\overline{\partial}_{E_{i}},\theta_{i}\big)

adapted to the filtered bundles
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{i}
.
Let
h^{(0)}_{U_{i}}
be Hermitian metrics of
U_{i}
. We obtain a pluri-harmonic metric

h^{(0)}=\bigoplus\big(h_{i}\otimes h^{(0)}_{U_{i}}\big)

of
\big(E,\overline{\partial}_{E},\theta\big)

adapted to
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}
. By Proposition
2.22

and the uniqueness of the canonical decomposition,
we obtain the following lemma.

Lemma 8.10
.

For any pluri-harmonic metric
h^{(1)}
of

\big(E,\overline{\partial}_{E},\theta\big)

adapted to
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}
,
there uniquely exist Hermitian metrics
h^{(1)}_{U_{i}}
of
U_{i}

such that

h^{(1)}=\bigoplus\big(h_{i}\otimes h^{(1)}_{U_{i}}\big)
.

For any
k\in K
,
we obtain a pluri-harmonic metric
k^{\ast}h^{(0)}

of
\big(E,\overline{\partial}_{E},\kappa(k)\theta\big)

adapted to
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}
.
Because
|\kappa(k)|=1
,

k^{\ast}\big(h^{(0)}\big)
is also a pluri-harmonic metric
of
\big(E,\overline{\partial}_{E},\theta\big)

adapted to
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}
.
Hence, there uniquely exist
Hermitian metrics
h_{U_{i}}(k)

of
U_{i}

such that

k^{\ast}(h)=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{m}(h_{i}\otimes h_{U_{i}}(k))
. By using the Haar measure
{\rm d}k
on
K

with
\int_{K}{\rm d}k=1
,
we define the Hermitian metric
h
of
E
as follows:

\displaystyle h:=\int_{K}k^{\ast}\big(h^{(0)}\big){\rm d}k=\bigoplus_{i=1}^{m}\Bigl(h_{i}\otimes\int_{K}h_{U_{i}}(k){\rm d}k\Bigr).

Then,
h
is also a pluri-harmonic metric. By the construction,
h
is
K
-invariant.

Let
h^{\prime}
be another
K
-invariant pluri-harmonic metric
of
\big(E,\overline{\partial}_{E},\theta\big)
adapted to
\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}
. We obtain the decomposition

\big(E,\overline{\partial}_{E},\theta\big)=\bigoplus\big(E_{i},\overline{\partial}_{E_{i}},\theta_{i}\big)

as in Proposition
2.22
,
which induces a decomposition of
good filtered Higgs bundles

(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})=\bigoplus(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{i},\theta_{i})
.
Because both
h
and
h^{\prime}
are
K
-invariant,
the decompositions are also
K
-invariant.
Hence, the decomposition

(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})=\bigoplus(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V}_{i},\theta_{i})

is
G
-invariant. By the
\mu_{L}
-stability of

(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})
,
we obtain
m=1
,
i.e.,
h^{\prime}=ah
for
a>0
.
∎

Corollary 8.11
.

We obtain the equivalence between
the isomorphism classes of the following objects:

•

(K,\rho,\kappa)
-homogeneous good wild harmonic bundles
on
(X,H)
.

•

(G,\rho,\kappa)
-homogeneous good filtered Higgs bundles

(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V},\theta)

such that

(i)
it is
\mu_{L}
-polystable with respect to the
G
-action,

(ii)

\mu_{L}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})=0
,

\int_{X}\mathop{\rm ch}\nolimits_{2}(\mathcal{P}_{\ast}\mathcal{V})c_{1}(L)^{\dim X-2}=0
.
